# SANDBAGGED — Roadmap

> A living backlog. Pairs with the project instructions (*how to work*) and, once code exists, a **CONTEXT** doc (*how it's built*); this file is *what's decided*, *what's next*, and *what's done*. **The code is the source of truth wherever this drifts.** Name locked v0.5: **Sandbagged**, campaign mode **The Lost Line**, repo slug `sandbagged`.

## Guidelines

- **Stable IDs** (`DES-1`, `ENG-3`…) so items can be referenced across chats. Never renumber; new ideas get a fresh ID in the right category. **Check the changelog before allotting one** — 104 IDs have shipped work against them, and three were handed out twice (`META-2`, `EVT-3`, `SHIP-1`) before anybody noticed. Highest used per family as of v9.27: `BAL-12` `EVT-3` `META-5` `NARR-9` `SHIP-2` `UX-16`. Categories: `DES` design docs · `ENG` combat engine · `CARD` card content · `HOLD` the wall's abilities · `ROUTE` routes/holds/conditions · `RUN` map & run loop · `NARR` story/journal/dialogue · `EVT` events · `SKIRM` skirmish · `ART` art & UI · `META` archetypes/unlocks · `SHIP` launch.
- **On ship:** flip to ✅, move the row out of To-do, prepend a Changelog entry, bump anything it unblocks.
- **Status:** ✅ shipped · 🔜 next up · ⏳ in progress/planned · 💡 idea (needs design) · 🚧 partially built · 🧊 shelved.
- **Priority is leverage, not a queue:** P0 = highest payoff … P3 = long game. "Depends on" is real.

> **Where the work is now.** **v10.48 — `ROPE-2` finished: the one route whose shape you choose.** `design/rope-2.md` is closed. **The Lost Line is now the only route in the game you decide the shape of, and you decide it on the ground** — two explicit buttons on its map card rather than a tap, so neither option is what you get by not reading; with nothing to clip the second reads **NO RACK** and points at the post, closing the loop with v10.47. **What pays for the belays:** the rack costs deck slots, and **the headwall sits above the last belay** — `pitches: 3` gives belays at **5 and 10 of 15** and the Headwall starts at **hold 13**, so the final third is one unbroken push with the window already shut. **You buy two resets and not the top**, and because that falls out of arithmetic it is asserted rather than assumed. Same route otherwise, compared field by field. **§4:** his fourth page is *"this one is mine to be stupid about"*, so roping up is a choice **against his example** — one epilogue line, and a **Marge** reply only a roped ascent can give (*"He would have called that a lack of commitment. He was wrong about that, and it took me twenty-two years and a funeral to be sure."*). **Neither option pays better**, asserted field by field. **And I walked into NARR-17's trap in the same session:** I gated her reply on `ropedUp`, which `newRun` clears, while her line needs the summit page and fires a trip later — **unreachable, with a comment claiming otherwise**, caught only by testing reachability instead of trusting my own comment. Durable `ropedFinale` now, with the per-attempt flag asserted not to leak. Band **45.0%**, off-band by construction. **Process, the worst of the session:** a `cp` from a stale scratch backup **reinstated the change I had retracted**, under a comment saying *"Left exactly as it was"* — nothing shipped wrong and the guard caught it, but ad-hoc backups have now caused trouble three times today. Use `git diff`, not scratch copies. I also re-drove the browser against a **stale `docs/`**, the exact UX-19 mistake I had already recorded. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device. Previously: **v10.47 — `ROPE-2`: the rack ROPE-1 built could not be got, and the change I recommended was wrong.** **What shipped:** the three pieces of protection were **unobtainable**. `Quickdraw`, `Wired Nut` and `Bomber Cam` carry `clip: true` and ROPE-1's whole decision rests on them, and they were in **no reward pool and no starting loadout** — **4,000 act-3 shop stockings offered one 0.0% of the time**, the sole source being the `moraine` event's one branch. The map card meanwhile says *"Nothing on your rack"* and `coach()` warns about running it out: the game described a rack you could not have. Now **sold** in any act that has ropes — derived from that act's map, priced, rotating by stage, and picked from the **stage rather than the run RNG** (the kit shelf's rule, so it perturbs no rolls). **Sold, not given, is a measured distinction:** putting them in `REWARDS` moved the band **44.3% → 48.0%** with act 3 deaths *falling* 33% → 29% — the clip is narrow but `shed 1, draw 1` at cost 0 is free value on all thirty-seven lines. Band **45.5%**, drift guard content. **What was retracted — my own recommendation.** I read `lastPiece: spec.roped ? 0 : -1` as a symptom patch and proposed `-1`, and Evan chose it on my advice. `sim/test.mjs` refused it by name: **`a roped route never starts you on nothing`** — *"69% of roped attempts used to end as ground falls… You are tied to the belay."* **The 49–86% ground-fall rate I measured is that same 69% bug, re-derived** — I found the number the fix exists for and proposed bringing it back. Now a kept injection, with the retraction at the **top** of `design/rope-2.md`. **And a dead line turned live:** `coach()` promised a ground fall nobody could see; with only the belay in a fall costs the **whole pitch**, and it says so. That is what the rope's tension actually is. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · ROPE-2's **roped finale** (spec §3) still stands. Previously: **v10.46 — `ROUTE-16`: two of the four roped lines were one line under two names.** **Found by arithmetic, not by reading.** Baselining `ROPE-2`, **The Cornice and The Hanging Slab reported identical send rates, ground-fall rates AND caught-falls-per-session** across 600 sessions each on different seeds — three statistics agreeing to three figures, which sampling does not do. They were the same route: same grade, style, clear, crux, feet, roped, pitches, and signatures with identical stats. **So the rope inventory I gave was wrong — three distinct roped lines, not four, one shipping twice** — which matters for a ticket premised on the rope being under-used. **The fix was already written in the route's own note:** *"a band of ice that lets go in the afternoon"* is a `ROUTE-6` closing window, so the prose did not change to fit the fix. It lands at 0.5 and takes the **feet** (`dSupport: -2`), because ice over slab is a Support problem — **never Power** (ENG-20). With a rack **80.8% → 76.0%**; band **44.1%** at n=3000 against the ~44.3 pin, act 3 deaths 32% → 33%. The no-rack pair moved too but that is the `CARD-11` reshuffle effect, not evidence, and is not banked. **Why nothing caught it:** `ROUTE-12` checks a signature is real, named, used once and does something — all four passed; none asks whether two signatures do the **same** thing. **And the new guard corrected me twice by being run:** it flagged `therail`/`thesitdown`, which *should* be identical (ROUTE-15's `local` pool pays only in information so naming a hold cannot move the band), and `thenave`/`softtouch`, identical mechanics on genuinely different routes — duplicated flavour, not gameplay. So the rule is the one that carries the defect: **routes of the same shape must have signatures that differ mechanically**. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ROPE-2` spec'd at `design/rope-2.md`, decision taken (ground fall on the first pitch only), **not yet built — and its inventory needs correcting for this**. Previously: **v10.45 — `UX-19`: the tutorial pushed COMMIT off the screen, and the bar meant to stop that had never worked.** **Reported from a phone, with a screenshot.** Two things were wrong and only one was the tutorial. The teaching banner costs **75px at NORMAL and 113px at LARGER** and the climb screen has no slack — measured overflow 8px at 874/NORMAL but **142px at 740/NORMAL**, 107px at LARGE, 252px at LARGER — so on any phone shorter than ~870 CSS px a first-timer is shown a banner telling them to press a button that is not on the screen. **And `A11Y-5`'s thumb-reachable COMMIT bar had never worked for anybody:** it was `position: sticky`, and a bare sticky-bottom control div in the same parent sat at **1170px in an 874px viewport**, so `DEV-1`'s goal of keeping COMMIT out of the OS swipe-up zone was never met either. **The sticky failure is still unexplained** — not `.wrap` being a scroll container, and `overflow-x: clip` did not fix it; `fixed` sidesteps the question rather than answering it. The controls are **fixed, ungated and 15px clear**, verified by geometry *and* hit-test at five viewport/text combinations, so COMMIT is reachable **even if the page never scrolls** — which is the property that matters, since scrolling could not be tested here. The bail row moved **above** them, because a fixed bar covers what follows it and removing the problem beat reserving space for it. **Process:** I **retracted a reproduction** — the "page will not scroll" finding was an artefact, since a plain 3000px control page also reported zero touch scroll; **backticks in the CSS template literal, twice**; a first cut that lost on specificity to the weather tint and so did not move at all; and **three `region()` anchors in my own guard containing the text their injections rewrite**, all three failing through "the opening anchor is gone" instead of naming the defect. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ROPE-2` spec'd at `design/rope-2.md`, decision taken (ground fall on the first pitch only), not yet built. Previously: **v10.44 — `ENG-9`: engine.ts 6,527 → 5,882, and the reason v6.6 failed.** The row said the first attempt fell over on *"mechanical import surgery, not on design"*. That was half right. **Rule one: engine.ts re-exports everything it moves out**, so `App.tsx` and `sim/entry.ts` were **not touched by any of this** — import surgery becomes zero lines when you refuse to do it. **Rule two, and the half the old row missed: data out, rules stay.** `CARDS` looks like the biggest content block in the file (346 lines) and is `Record<string, CardDef> = {}` filled by a loop over `mv(...)` factories — **data built by rules**; move it and the factories go too, and the content file holds rules. Same for `CONTENT: DIALOGUE`, 602 lines of `TALKS` wrapped in five functions that need `GameState`. **~1,000 of the 2,350 "content" lines are rules wearing content's clothes, and that is what v6.6 actually hit.** Both correctly stayed, and the guard fails if either arrives. **645 lines moved for 1 guard fix, 11 injection paths and 0 caller changes** — `types.ts` 88, `content.ts` 642. **The density was measured before starting and it corrected me:** I had said churn would get *worse* in content, and it is ~2 references per 100 lines with only **seven** guard windows in all 2,350. **The net worked three times:** `UI-4` broke loudly (*"read 0 node types"*) and got **stronger** when repointed at `E.ACTS` — every node type that really appears on a map now needs a mark; GUARD-9 named all six orphaned anchors by id; `region()` never returned a bad window. **Ceiling revised down:** ~1,350 movable, not 2,350, of which 645 is done. **Process:** I invented `E.NODE_KINDS`, left a malformed line in the new guard, and wrote the cycle check as `!A || B` — which the injection walked straight through, the **fourth vacuous assertion this session** and the second caught by its own ticket. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.43 — `NARR-21`: the audit was closed against a bracket, not a measurement.** A gap in the five versions before it, found by looking back rather than forward. **`NARR-16`–`NARR-20` were all measured against two harness policies** — never take a trail node, or take every one — which produced `known` ending figures of **0.0% and 51.1%**: a bracket, not a measurement. And **`NARR-18` is unmeasurable by either**, because it changed what the node *says* and the sim cannot read. Five versions of narrative work with no number anybody could defend. The new `reads` policy models the player that node is written for — takes it while there are still pages to find — and at 60×8 gives **43.1% known / 11.2 of 14 pages** against **0.0% / 6.0**. **The finding is that `reads` is indistinguishable from `events`** (50.7% vs 51.1% at 200×10, 12.5 pages each): the clause about stopping once the journal is full almost never fires, because the journal does not fill inside a career. **That is the answer, not a flaw in the model** — the trail node costs *nothing* (completion is HIGHER with it, 41.7% against 28.3%, because pages become beta), so the optimal informed policy really is "take every one", and the two "extremes" were only ever **believes the label** and **does not**. The gap between them is what NARR-18 bought: **~43 points of the known ending and 5 pages**. The **delta** is asserted rather than just the band, and the sharpest of the four injections collapses the two arms into one — vacuous measurement, still passing its own band. **Two honest gaps:** the `nextPage` clause carries **no injection**, because removing it changes nothing measurable today; and **whether a human reads the label is not something any harness can answer** — that one needs playing. **Next: `ENG-9`.** `src/engine.ts` is **6,527 lines**, of which ~2,350 are `CONTENT:` sections and 229 are `TYPES` — a split takes it to about **3,900**. The cost is real and now measured: **32 guards read the file by path and 112 injection patches are keyed to it**. What makes the retry viable where v6.6 failed is that every moved declaration makes its `region()` throw loudly and GUARD-9 flags every orphaned anchor, so the job is mechanical and fully supervised. One section at a time, `check` between each. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.42 — `NARR-20`: the largest body of prose in the game did not know the story existed.** Thirty-six events, more words than the journal and the conversations put together, and **nine of them knew there was a story** — the biggest thing in the game inert while the smallest carried the whole campaign. **Two things it deliberately is not:** more events, and the dead man sprinkled through all thirty-six — *a world where every rockfall reminds you of him is worse, not better*, so the restraint is **asserted** rather than trusted. **Ten events now answer a page he wrote, and only for somebody carrying that page**, so the terrain starts recognising him as you read him: *Seepage* ← page 3 *Conditions* ("you are standing in the rest of the time"), *A Line Nobody Has Done* ← 9 *On the rock* ("this is the rubbish. That is almost encouraging"), *Rockfall* ← 12 *On being frightened* ("you have managed about four seconds"), *The Old Campsite* ← 4 *On going alone* ("this is not the Cathedral"), and six more. **A named page is correct HERE** — nothing depends on it, so miss the page and you miss a sentence, whereas NARR-17 gated **progression** on one; a count gate would print "he wrote about this rock" to somebody who has read three pages about the weather. **Text and never power:** `lore` has no outcome and cannot grow one (checked on the data as well as the type), and `loreFor` is asserted to be called **exactly once in the engine — its own declaration** — which is what makes this band-neutral by construction rather than by measurement. **Rendered in a browser both ways round**, because half the feature is its absence; the pool had to be forced, since the first attempt rolled an event with nothing to say and proved nothing. What the guard **cannot** check is whether each line truly answers its page — that is a reading, and it is written down rather than pretended at. **9 injections, 9 caught first pass**, the first ticket in this run with no mislabelled ones. **The campaign audit is now closed on all five tickets** (`NARR-16`–`NARR-20`), band 44.3% throughout. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.41 — `NARR-19`: seven beats over six expeditions, and nowhere to read it back.** **The campaign audit's fourth and last, and the only one that adds rather than repairs.** `seen` recorded THAT a conversation had happened and nothing whatever about it, and your own half was discarded the moment you left the fire — so Marge's seven beats, delivered across about six expeditions and days of real time, arrived as seven half-remembered moments. **Who You Have Met** keeps both halves, **grouped by person and in authored order**, because `after` chains the beats and that is the only order they can happen in; a trip-ordered list would interleave five people into noise. **Three deliberate refusals:** the reply is stored as its **label** and not its index (an index rots silently, showing you a sentence you never said); conversations from before this shipped **say so** rather than being given an invented answer; and `fa:`/`con:` talks are counted but **not quoted**, because their prose is rebuilt from current state and quoting it would show text nobody said. **SAVE-7's guard fired, on the first new account field since it was written** — `said` went into `saveGame` first on purpose to find out whether that guard had bought anything, and it had: *"carryOver does not hand it to a new expedition — that is SAVE-7 again"*. A transcript that emptied itself every trip is a memory feature losing its memory. An existing nav guard also caught the new screen's missing BACK parent before a browser was involved. **Three of eleven injections were mislabelled, and chasing them found two real faults in my own guard:** `/said:/` was **vacuous** (`said: st.said` matches it *and* throws the answer away), and removing the loader's type filter made the test **crash rather than fail** — because `.slice` on a number throws, `loadGame` catches, and **one crafted value makes the whole slot unreadable**, which is SAVE-6's sharpest lesson and now has its own assertion. Off band by construction; **44.3%** at `PAGES=14`. **The audit is closed on all four tickets.** What is left of it is `NARR-20`: 36 events and ~1,700 words, the biggest body of prose in the game, of which only nine reference the thread. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.40 — `NARR-18`: the only door to the campaign advertised nothing.** Not a defect in the code — a defect in what the game was willing to say. Every node on the map sells itself with numbers; **one did not**: *"Something on the trail · ? · No climbing. No telling."* — and that node is the only route to his journal outside the conversations. The audit's two brackets: careers that take trail nodes reach the `known` ending **51.1%** of the time and careers that never take one **0.0%** — and the ones that take them **finish MORE often** (44.7% by trip ten against 37%), because pages become beta. **The story-seeking path was strictly better in every dimension and the game never said so.** It now adds the one thing true of every event — *his pages turn up out here · you are carrying n of 14*, off `pagesHeld` and `FINDABLE` — while ***"No telling" is untouched***, because `rollEvent` picks after you commit and the not-knowing is the node. **And the journal screen says what the pages are FOR**: it said *"they keep between runs"* and never that they are the finale's beta and the ending's threshold, both now derived from `unreadGrip` (the same call the wall makes) and `KNOWN_AT`. **Rendered in a browser at 0 pages and at 7**, where the Grip figure moves +6 → +3 — NARR-16's fix visible on screen. The guard pulls against itself on purpose: say enough to inform the choice, never name the event. **Two process notes.** The browser check found the change **absent** at first — I was driving the shipped `docs/`, which was still v10.39. And the negative test caught a **vacuous half of my own assertion**: `/his pages/` matches either branch of the ternary, so blanking the advertising branch still passed. That is the **repeated-fragment** trap, and the first time it has been caught by the injection written for its own ticket rather than versions later. It counts to two now. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.39 — `NARR-17`: the story's spine dead-ended in 100% of careers.** **Nobody who has played this game has seen the back half of Marge's thread.** `marge4` — *"That is his hand. I would know it upside down in the rain. Where."* — gated on **page 4**, which is the *seventh* page delivered because the journal is interleaved on purpose, and **ten of the fourteen page grants in the game live inside the chain page 4 unblocks**. Pages gated the talks; the talks supplied the pages. Measured over 200 careers on the policy that plays to climb: `marge4` through `marge8` fired in **0.0%**, the talks plateaued at 15/20 by trip six and never moved, `known` never happened in 673 wins. **Now a gate on a COUNT** — three of his pages, any three — which cannot deadlock, and which is what the scene means anyway. `marge4` **0.0% → 100%**, all twenty conversations **85.5% within ten trips** (median trip 6), the `stranger` ending **35.5% → 15.1%**. `marge6`'s gate is `needsSummit: true` rather than `needsPage: 7`, and that fact — page 7 is the one he wrote at the top — is `SUMMIT_PAGE` in all **nine places across three files** it was spelled as a bare number. The availability rule existed **twice**, four hand-written clauses each in `postTalk` and `availableTalk`; both callers ask `talkOpen` now. **The guard that should have caught this handed itself the journal** (`journal: [1,2,3,4,5,6,7]`) — it proved the chain works *given* the pages and never asked whether they arrive. It starts from nothing now, collects only what a reply hands over, and is given **no event pages at all**. Band **44.3%** at n=3000, unchanged for a stateable reason. **Carve-out: the `known` ending is still 0.0% on this policy** — it wants ten pages and the climber gets six; event-biased careers reach **51.1%**, so it is gated behind knowing the unlabelled node is where his pages are. That is **`NARR-18`, next**. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.38 — `NARR-16`: eight of his fifteen journal pages were worth nothing on the wall.** **The eighth audit, and the first on story rather than systems.** It needed a new harness — the old one measures a RUN and a story is not run-scale — so `career` mode carries `seen` and `journal` across expeditions the way `carryOver` now does. Two hundred careers, ten trips each: **talks plateau at 15.0/20 by trip six and never move again**, `marge4`–`marge8` fire in **0.0% of careers**, and the `known` epilogue in **0.0% of 673 wins**. Marge's thread — the one the source calls the spine — stops dead at *"I looked for two seasons."* That is **`NARR-17`, next**. **This version is the other defect the audit found.** The finale's blindness read `JOURNAL.length - 1 - journal.filter(p => p <= 6).length` — one expression in which **both halves meant six**. NARR-11 grew the journal to fifteen and only the first half followed. Two consequences, neither measured: the finale got a flat **+8 Grip a hold** for everybody, and **the eight pages NARR-11 added became worth nothing** — 24.37 mean hold grip holding all eight, *identical* to holding none. Meanwhile the map card read *"6/6 of his pages — you can read the whole thing"* with eight Grip a hold still on it. Now two named constants: `BLIND_HOLD = 8` **kept rather than reverted** (eleven versions of balance sit on it) and `UNREAD_MAX = 6`, NARR-7's range, driven by **every findable page**. Both endpoints unmoved by construction, so the band measured **44.3% at n=3000 — the baseline number digit for digit**. A **ceiling** not a round, so the *first* page he wrote is worth something and the flat steps land at the top where the ending and the every-page deed already pay. The guard asserts the **invariant** rather than the numbers — relief may depend on how many of his pages you hold and never on which — by comparing two disjoint halves of the same journal, because a uniform bug is invisible to the relative test that sat next to it for eleven versions. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.37 — `SAVE-7`: the journal never survived a second expedition.** `NARR-9` was meant to be closed as stale, and auditing it found a bug live since `carryOver` was written. **The ticket first:** the count was already done (`NARR-11`, fifteen pages, v9.32), and *"you will read most of them in one run"* is false — measured **2.0 pages a run**, 2.6 taking every event, of fourteen. Gating by act would have slowed an already-slow drip. **Then the reason the journal feels inert:** pages are saved as ACCOUNT state, the "known" ending needs ten and the `everypage` deed needs all fourteen — and **`carryOver` never carried `journal`**, so starting an expedition reset it to `[]` and the next autosave wrote the empty array to disk. The only meta-progression never survived a second trip, and both the ending and the deed were unreachable in normal play. Its own docstring warns about exactly this — *"hand-copying a subset is how it got lost"* — directly above the list that lost it. **A second field went the same way:** `dailyTried`, SAVE-4's anti-farm gate, so starting a trip made today's daily available again. **The fix is the diff, not the two fields:** a guard compares what `saveGame` keeps as account state against what `carryOver` hands a new run, with five named exemptions whose reasons are themselves re-checked — so the next field added to the save fails the suite instead of going quiet for a year. Off-band by construction (the harness never calls `carryOver`) and measured at 45.1% against the ~44.3 pin. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.36 — `A11Y-10` and `BAL-17`: the two decisions, decided.** **A11Y-10's answer was neither option.** Three screens carried `role="status"` at mount and seventeen carried nothing, which under A11Y-9's rule looked like noise to delete — and deleting it would have made the app **worse**, because **the only `.focus()` in App.tsx was the bottom-sheet one**. Nothing moved focus on a phase change, and in a single-page app that means a screen reader is told **nothing**: those three were the only announcement three screens had. So the gap got fixed instead — focus moves to the screen's heading on every phase change, so **all ~20 screens announce** and a keyboard user lands at the top of each. Every title already carried `role=heading aria-level=1`, so it needed no per-screen markup, and it made the three redundant. Verified in a browser: focus was on `BODY` before. **BAL-17: the floor stays at 5**, with reasons rather than inertia. Re-measured at n=2000 the roster is 6.6–8.7 (spread 1.32×) and **the lowest is the Trad Dad now**, not the Comp Kid. 6.6 clears 5 by **2.9 SE**; raising to 6 would cut that to **1.1 SE** and recreate the unmeasurable state BAL-16 escaped; a relative floor would move with the roster and stop catching every climber sinking together. Revisit at ~6.2. The reasoning sits beside the assertion, not in a changelog. **Left, and none of it mine:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `NARR-9` is content (note: pages feed the finale's beta, so it is band-active) · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.35 — `SKIRM-9` and `PERF-2`: the last defect, and the question closed.** **SKIRM-9:** the run-end subtitle was unconditional, so CIRCUIT OVER read *"Act 1 · the forest · stage 1"* on a mode with no acts and no stages — the class SKIRM-8 caught twice on the walk-off. It reads **"the circuit · the grind · deck 15"** now, worded identically to the walk-off so the mode's two endings match. **PERF-2 is answered, and its premise was stale in both numbers:** React is **38%** of the bundle (190 KB raw / 59 KB gzip against 497 / 157), not "most of 333KB", and the **game** is what grew. Bytes were never the question — measured under Chromium CPU throttling, a **mid-tier phone has the game up in 378 ms and repaints a turn in 50 ms** (low-end: 605 ms and 110 ms). React's share of that boot is bounded at about **90 ms**, against hand-rolling a renderer underneath four thousand lines of `App.tsx`. **It does not matter**, and the question is closed rather than left for somebody to re-ask from the same guess. `npm run perf` keeps it runnable and the size is pinned — the answer holds at this size, not at any size. **The repeated-fragment lesson landed three times in one ticket:** `setCPUThrottlingRate` and `[1, 4, 6]` each appear twice in the perf script, so checking for either *anywhere* would pass with one removed — which is how you quote a phone number measured on a desktop. **Count when the fragment repeats**, three tickets running now. **Left, and all yours:** `SHIP-3` needs a Play account, key and device (`ship/README.md`) · `ART-3` needs a device · `BAL-17` and `A11Y-10` are decisions · `NARR-9` is content · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.34 — `SHIP-3`: everything for Play that does not need an account.** Packaging rather than porting, and the half that needs no Google account is done: **a real maskable icon as its own file** (the plain artwork does not fit Android's 80% safe zone — the bottom hold sits 208px from centre against a 205px radius — so relabelling it would have had a round mask shave it), **a privacy policy whose every claim was checked against the built file** and is now guarded so a tracker or a new host makes the suite fail rather than the store listing lie, the manifest fields Bubblewrap and Play read, a **Bubblewrap config** guarded against disagreeing with the web manifest, and **`assetlinks.json` emitted on demand and never stubbed** — the fingerprint only exists after the first upload, and a placeholder serves, fails verification silently, and leaves a URL bar nobody can explain. **Two things found on the way:** `.nojekyll` is **load-bearing** — Pages runs Jekyll without it and Jekyll drops dot-directories, so `.well-known/assetlinks.json` would 404 with no diagnosable symptom; and **`build-html.mjs` is not idempotent**, so running half a build doubled the PWA head and **registered the service worker twice**. Nothing shipped was affected (`npm run ship` always rebuilds first, and v10.30–v10.33 were checked), and it refuses loudly now. The comment-strip lesson also landed for the **fifth and sixth** time in two new syntaxes — `// commented out` and `# commented out` both keep their text, and two injections walked past the assertions written for them. **What remains needs an account, a key and a device**, and is written down in `ship/README.md`. **Open:** `SHIP-3` (finish on a Play account) · `NARR-9`, `ART-3` (P2) · `BAL-17`, `SKIRM-9`, `A11Y-10`, `PERF-2` (P3) · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.33 — `BAL-13`: act 1 is frictionless, and no dial fixes that.** **No game change** — the deliverable is an answer, the measurement that produced it, and a pin. The row carried the wrong number for many versions: *"act 1 kills ~3% of runs"* says act 1 is not **lethal**, where the complaint is that it cannot **go wrong**, and a death rate cannot tell those apart. Measured with a new `acts` diagnostic: act 1 puts **4.3%** of runs under skin pressure against act 2's **30.2%**, touches psyche on **0.6%** against **11.3%**, and takes **0.4 of the 3 camps** it offers. Act 1 is not merely non-lethal, it is **frictionless** — you never spend the resources, so no act-1 decision is a decision. **Five levers were screened against all three bars at once** (pressure, the ~44.3 band, the climber spread); every previous attempt checked one, which is how v9.36 took the spread 1.4× → 2.9×. Removing recovery does **nothing**, because the cost never accrues. A flat approach cost *raised* completion six points — the tell that **the slack absorbs the pressure**: the policy answers a cost by taking all three act-1 camps (3.0 ± 0.0 against 0.4) and trading climbs for camps. Cut the slack and act 1 finally bites, at **spread 4.45× with a climber on 4.0%** — and **three independent mechanisms (skin, grip, attempts) each drop a climber under the 5% floor**, with weather ruled out *by construction* (`ignoreWeather`). **The reason is structural:** act 1 is the only stretch where every climber is at full resources, which is why it is frictionless *and* why pressure there separates the roster hardest. **A dial cannot fix this** — act 1's shape can, and that is Evan's design call, not a tuning pass. Pinned in the ledger in both directions so the evidence is not rediscovered. **Open:** `SHIP-3` (P1) · `NARR-9`, `ART-3` (P2) · `BAL-17`, `SKIRM-9`, `A11Y-10`, `PERF-2` (P3) · `ENG-9`, `ROPE-2` parked. Previously: **v10.32 — `ART-4`: the day comes out as a picture.** SOCIAL-1 made a result you can paste anywhere and what it made was text; an image is what people actually post, and the grid was already a picture with no way out of the app. **The delivery was the hard half and the row said so:** `<a download>` is the wrong answer for this device — iOS has never handled downloading a blob from a web app, and an installed PWA has nowhere to put it — so there is no download link. `navigator.share` **with the file**, behind `canShare` because a browser can have share() and refuse files; and where that is missing, **the picture itself, to press and hold**, which is how a phone saves an image. Picture and paste are one result rendered twice and read one projection, so a guard can assert they show the same grid. **The bug a browser found, and only a browser could have:** the fallback sheet went into the **climb screen's tree**, which returns earlier than the screen its button is on — so the picture was drawn, correctly, and **had nowhere to appear**. Every guard passed, because every guard was reading the right source in the wrong component. Fourth ticket running. **A modal that cannot be escaped is worse than one that never claimed to be**, so the sheet joined A11Y-8's focus/Escape effect — and that guard counted a literal `2`, so a third *correct* sheet failed it while a third *broken* one would have passed. It counts sheets now. **And the comment-strip rule bit a fifth time, the stripper itself being the fault:** three guards filtered comments per LINE, and this project writes block comments whose continuation lines are bare prose — so the sentence forbidding a download link *was* the download link the guard found. `stripComments` is shared in `guard.mjs` now. **The sixth set is done.** Previously: **v10.31 — `DEV-7`: an update never takes the player off the screen they are on.** Reported from play: *"I am on the main menu, I tap to enter and get in and then it randomly goes back to the previous menu."* It did. Three links, none of them wrong alone: DEV-2 reloads an open page when a new service worker takes over (because `skipWaiting`+`claim` do not, so a rarely-relaunched PWA runs the old build for days); it gated that on `__SB_BUSY__` meaning **mid-climb**; and `loadGame` restores the save but **not `phase`**, deliberately, because SAVE-4 exists to stop a reload reopening the post, the van and the daily. Together: **a reload was permitted on every screen except a climb, and every one of them lands you back on the splash** — unrequested, a couple of seconds after launch, which is what "randomly" describes. **Reproduced before touching anything** with two builds over HTTP, and `controllerchange` measured at **~1.7s after load**. That 1.7s is the fix: the flag means **"the player has tapped begin"** now, so the only reload that can happen is one nobody can see — splash up, page reloads, splash still up. DEV-2's whole point survives (the build lands in the session that downloaded it) because a second and a bit is the normal case for still being on the splash, not a lucky one. **It also retires the poll:** `booted` is one-way, so an interval waiting for busy to go false could never fire. Verified against the reproduction both ways round — race the worker or let it win — **no navigation after the tap in either case**. And the tempting wrong fix is guarded: restoring `phase` on load would reopen the SAVE-4 hole, so an assertion and an injection fail if anyone tries it. **One P3 left:** `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.30 — `A11Y-9`: a screen reader hears the turn, not the last line of it.** The row said the newest blocks are static divs — "consistency, not a break". Reading the screens found something worse and narrower: the climb screen's live region announced **the last log line**, and **a turn writes a mean of 4.8** (measured over 297 turns of real play — one line on 2% of turns, nine on one). So a screen-reader player was getting **about a fifth of the game**, and not a chosen fifth: the last line is whatever the engine appended last, routinely the route's move for *next* turn rather than the outcome of the move just made. The visible log renders `slice(-3)`, so a sighted player was two lines a turn ahead of a blind one. It sends every line added since the last render now — verified in a browser against the visible log, where one turn announced a **signature read you had paid for** that the old region would have dropped entirely. **The rule this settled on, instead of "make every static div announce":** a live region is for what appears *while you are already on the screen*. Three qualified and said nothing; the nineteen present at mount were left alone, because a live region there is noise. **The phase banner is the deliberate exception** — the phase is already in the log, so only its partner clause announces, the one line there that NARR-14 forbids the log from carrying. **Two windows in my own new guard were wrong and the injections found both:** one swallowed the next banner and passed on *its* live region, and one anchored on the very attribute list under test. Third version running that the window, not the claim, was the broken part. **One P3 left:** `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.29 — `NARR-15`: the moments they were standing through in silence.** The row said the partner speaks at four moments and was two tickets stale — there were ten. What was true is that the game had **built** five more and walked somebody through all of them without a word. Two sting: **the walk-off**, where SKIRM-7 spends the whole circuit saying go again or that is enough and SKIRM-8 gave stopping its own screen, and **nobody joined the two up**; and **the first ascent**, whose own words are *"nothing to compare it to and nobody to ask"* while somebody stands right there. Plus a curse, a phase, and **a fifth the row did not list** — the Circuit has two endings and both were silent, because the `!st.circuit` gate that correctly keeps trip lines off it left the mode's ordinary ending with nobody in it. **NARR-14's guard refused the first draft and was right to:** the phase line went into `resolve`'s log, and a partner the resolution can read is a partner somebody can later make matter. **The render caught two things, and they were the same thing twice:** the walk-off block shipped rendering **nothing** — the gate was written not to read `circuit`, then called `partnerFor`, **which read it for us** — and fixing that woke a block silently dead on that screen, which said *"Feet."* on an ending where nothing fell. Third ticket running that a browser found what the code did not. An injection for a *different* ticket also found **a hole that predates this one**: an unanchored gate assertion is a substring of `{false && …`, so the tutorial banner could be switched off entirely and stay green. **Anchor a JSX gate on its brace** — second time in two versions. **Two P3s left:** `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.28 — `ROUTE-15`: the first lines in the book have a feature too.** ROUTE-5 added named features to kill "a route is a stat line" and left **the four lines you meet first** without one — the sameness concentrated exactly where a new player is deciding whether this game has any texture. They are **tagged, not placed**: `signature` rebuilds the hold from the feature's base and applies its stats, while a tag writes `{ ...hold, sig }` onto a hold already there, from an RNG keyed to the route rather than the run — no number moves, no run entropy is consumed (**44.4% against the ~44.3 pin**). **Three existing guards are what made me find the rule:** written stat-less the four tripped *"is just its base hold with a name on it"*, and the project's answer to what a feature is FOR was already written — ROUTE-12 makes every one pay a one-time **`read`**, information, which is free. The older guard predates that, so it was widened to accept `read`, and an injection proves the widening is not a weakening. **The bug this ticket shipped into its own tree:** "which feature" and "whether to tag" ran through one expression, `null || undefined` is falsy, and **every grind line silently lost its feature** — my 60-seed probe passed throughout because it never looked at the circuit, and **ROUTE-12's guard caught it**. The injections then found two holes in guards I had just written (an assertion satisfied by one of the tagger's two write sites; two assertions that looked identical but guard the flag and the derivation). **And the render caught what the code did not, again:** The Sit-Down's note named a *position* on a line whose holds are shuffled. Tutorial and finale are carved out on content grounds — The Lost Line's own note is *"No chalk. No tick marks. No trail."*, and a named feature means people have been there. **Three P3s left:** `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.27 — `SAVE-6`: a save cannot claim an array longer than the game can make.** SAVE-5 was one unvalidated *number* that froze levelling for ever from a one-field import code; the arrays were the same hole — `owned`, `history`, `journal`, `established`, `trail` and a dozen more taken at whatever length the file claimed, and `history` and `owned` are **rendered in full**. Every array goes through one clamp now with **derived** caps, plus a scan of the loader so a new field cannot be missed. **The find beyond the audit's claim: the larder grows without a ceiling in normal play** — a kit item per streak rung, and only starting a trip takes any out — so it is capped where it grows as well as where it loads. **And the fix reproduced SAVE-5's own failure mode:** `LARDER_MAX` written as `KIT_MAX * 6` two thousand lines above `KIT_MAX` evaluated to **NaN**, and `slice(-NaN)` returns everything, so the cap silently did nothing — every cap is now asserted to be a finite number. The injections also found three faults of one family: a fixture shorter than the cap it tested, a negative assertion matching `slice(0, 40)` **in its own comment**, and **GUARD-9's guard letting a malformed patch through**. **Four P3s left:** `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.26 — `SKIRM-8`: the good outcome gets a screen.** RUN-13 put the circuit walk-off in history because nothing acknowledged the one outcome this mode has that is not a failure; SKIRM-7 gave you somebody who nudges you toward it; and pressing the button set `phase: 'menu'`, so the good ending dropped you on the guidebook with **no screen at all** while every fall got a full account. It ends on `sessionEnd` now — lines, zone, whether you beat your best, and a `circuitShare` — marked `result: 'walked'`, which is per-run and so cannot leak the way a mode flag could (MODE-1), and which stops a stale `circuitScore` being read as "this was a circuit". **Two things the render caught that reading the code did not:** the route header would have named a campaign route you were never on, and — only visible on screen — **"Burns used 3/3. Got 6 of 5"**, one line's burns against a hold count already passed. I also cut two assertions of my own that could not fail, both implied by the `eq` above them: **the fourth vacuous-assertion class this month, and why GUARD-9 exists.** **Five P3s left:** `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.25 — `BAL-16`: a floor nobody can measure is not a floor.** The climber-spread guard says no climber completes under 5%, and it was passing on a coin flip — the Comp Kid read **5.2% at n=600 against a real 5.8%**, and 5.8% clears a floor of 5 by **1.7 SE**, which no affordable sample turns into a claim. An unmeasurable property is a different problem from a false one, so both halves were fixed and **the floor was not touched** (BAL-9's own note records it being lowered once "to accommodate a drift rather than to fix it"). The guard resolves the floor where it lives — coarse pass for the spread, fine n=2000 pass on the two lowest via a new `ARCH_ONLY`, 4,000 runs instead of 12,500 — and it now **reports its margin in standard errors**, so "this cannot be resolved" is a loud failure rather than a flake. The Comp Kid was bought back to **6.6%, 3.2 SE clear**, on the axis it actually died of: **psyche, 19% of its runs against 11–14% for everyone else.** Every existing dial was the wrong size — one skin and one betaGrip are worth *nothing*, one Contact +4.4 and one card of hand **+9.8** — so **this climber is card-starved, not strength-starved**, and it got a dial of its own (`dPsyche`; psyche deaths 19% → 1%). Verified long-standing, not drift: v10.19 reads it at 5.7%. The band is untouched at ~44.3. **Noted for whoever is next: the roster now lives between 6 and 9% and the floor of 5 was set when it spanned 3.3 to 29.8** — a much tighter fit, and the next climber to drift hits the same wall. That wants a dated decision. **Everything left is P3:** `SKIRM-8`, `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.24 — `GUARD-9`: the negative tests are kept.** `test.mjs` exists because fifteen one-off harnesses were each used once and thrown away; the injections were the same story a level up. `sim/mutants.mjs` now holds **52 of them** across GUARD-8, CARD-18, SKIRM-7, SIM-7, SIM-8 and itself, each breaking one thing a guard claims and **naming the failure it expects to hear**. **The case for keeping them is that they found three assertions that could not fail** — one asserting a function was *called* when the block rendering it was gated off, one asserting a send rate that points the opposite way to the bug, one asserting against a list the test had just built itself. GUARD-8 made a fragile source *window* impossible; this is the other shape, an assertion that tests the fixture instead of the claim, and only running it catches that. The runner restores the tree after each mutant, on crash, on Ctrl-C, and byte-compares at exit. **Deliberately not gated on `ship`**, which cuts against GUARD-6 and is stated as such: it protects guard authoring, not release regression, and the half that rots on its own (anchors going dead) runs on every `check`. **What is left: `BAL-16`** (the Comp Kid completes 5.2% against a floor of 5 — a guard passing on a coin flip, in a measurement no re-pin can reach) **and six P3s** — `SKIRM-8`, `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.23 — the band is re-pinned, ~52 → ~44.3, because the old pin was measured on a deck no player can hold.** run.mjs sorted every card by raw stats and capped rares and uncommons **but not beta**, and `copyLimit('beta')` is 3 — so six of the fifteen measured slots were `Beta ·` cards `buildable()` refuses outright. **Every "the band held" in the ledger since the pin was set leaned on that deck.** It now rides the engine's own `buildLoadout` (the BUILD ME ONE builder, with SIM-7's slot rules), so there is one builder and the number describes a deck you could actually arrive at. Measured cost of honesty: **53.4 → 44.3**, of which **+10.5 was the illegal cards alone** and the rest is shape — the game's build carries half the raw Power and wins on consistency. **Only one guard needed retuning, the same one SIM-6 moved:** ROUTE-8's direct line read −7.0 against a −8 floor, so `dCrux` 3 → 2 brings it to −2.0, five points clear — which *restores* the intent its own note states rather than inventing one. CARD-9 (+1.8) and the climber spread are untouched. **Two things recorded because I walked into them:** `dClear: -1` on the direct looks like a fix and is the exact thing CARD-15 removed; and at n=500 that line read +1.0 where n=1500 reads −2.0, so ROUTE-8's own sample resolves its floor and not its ceiling. **New P2 noticed on the way and explicitly not caused by this: `BAL-16`** — the Comp Kid completes 5.2% against a floor of 5, a guard passing on a coin flip, in a measurement no re-pin can reach. Otherwise all that is left is P3: `SKIRM-8`, `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.22 — `SIM-7` opened into a live bug and shipped as one: BUILD ME ONE was handing you an illegal deck.** `RARE_SLOTS` (1) and `UNCOMMON_SLOTS` (3) cap how many rares and uncommons a loadout may hold — the picker enforces them in three places and **`buildLoadout` enforced neither**, so with a full collection it fielded **ten rares against a limit of one**. That deck completed **18.0%** of campaigns against **28.1% for the starter deck it offers to replace**: the button that says "not sure where to start? let it pick" was making you nine points worse. Obeying the rule takes it to **44.3%**, so the fix is worth **+26 points** to anyone who presses it. Off-band by construction — the pinned band still rides run.mjs's own `buildBest` — and the slow ledger now carries the measured floor that was missing. It survived because both existing builder guards check deck SHAPE, and the one send measurement uses a beginner's collection **with no rares in it at all**. **One decision is now waiting on you, logged as `SIM-8`:** the pinned band is itself measured on an illegal deck — `buildBest` caps rares and uncommons but not **beta**, so six of the fifteen measured slots are `Beta ·` cards the game refuses outright, **worth +10.5 points**. The honest band for a legal deck is **~43** stat-sorted or **~44** as the game builds it, against a pin of **~52**. Re-pointing the harness is a re-pin and will recalibrate ROUTE-8, CARD-9 and the climber spread the way SIM-6 did — so it wants your call, not mine. **Otherwise all that is left is P3:** `SKIRM-8`, `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.21 — `SKIRM-7` shipped: the Circuit had nobody in it.** It runs `inRun: false` — correctly, it is not a campaign — so every feature gated on `inRun` skipped it silently, and by v10.20 it was the least-served mode in the game with no partner, no ambience and nothing between lines but a zone name. It is also the longest single sitting there is. Now: **a partner** (the gate widened by exactly one mode, asserted, and stable across a resume because DEV-3 saves the circuit's `runSeed`), **a place keyed to its own zones** so the bed says how deep you are, and **an opinion about the one decision the mode has** — two new lines and a per-person threshold for when they would rather you walked off, which is how **RUN-13's good outcome finally gets somebody to say it out loud.** The season hook is **deliberately carved out** — an endless mode feeding a bounded four-week season is farmable — and the carve-out is asserted rather than forgotten. **The part worth watching: NARR-14's guard forbids any partner field that is not text, and `enough` is a number** — so widening it ships with the assertion that makes it safe (every value `partnerPush` can return must be a key of `says`). **13 injections negative-tested, and one found a vacuous assertion in this ticket's own guard** — it checked that `partnerFor` was *called*, which stays true when the render is gated off. That is the GUARD-8 class in a guard written days after GUARD-8 shipped. **What is left is all P3 plus two P2 leads found by this month's work:** `SIM-7` (the harness's `buildBest` is blind to every keyword, so the measured starting deck is stat-greedy — a re-pin-shaped decision), `SKIRM-8` (walking off the Circuit still has no screen), then `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9` and `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.20 — `CARD-18` shipped: four mechanics the game had and you would never meet twice.** `skinCost`, `read`, `latch` and `restChip` were each carried by exactly one card of 244 — a mechanic the game has and a player will almost certainly never see. Each has a sibling that is a trade rather than a reskin (`Take It All In`, `Rand Smear`, `Milk The Jug`, `Last Of The Skin`), plus keyword entries for the two the game never named. **Two of my four shipped as strict upgrades in the first cut** — Shake Out with a free chip, and Precise Feet's exact stat line with a free latch — and both now pay for their keyword. **`restChip` had no drafter term at all, so Chalk the Hold has read below the take-it line for eleven versions** (ENG-25's blind spot, third instance); it is priced at 3 a point, derived from a probe reading +3.0pt against `anchor`'s +2.6pt, while **`read` deliberately gets no term** because the policy cannot spend it. Band **52.7 → 53.4 at n=2700, and a three-arm measurement proves the move is pool reshuffle, not difficulty** (the four mechanics are −0.2); the ~52 pin holds. **The bigger finding is logged as `SIM-7`:** the harness's own `buildBest` values a card at `power * 2 + contact` and is blind to every keyword in the game, so the measured starting deck is stat-greedy while its in-run picks are smart. **The seventh audit's remaining items are `SKIRM-7`** (the Circuit gets no partner and no ambience) **and five P3s**, plus the sixth set's last item `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.19 — both of the seventh audit's P1s shipped: `GUARD-8` closed the class that let `MODE-1` through.** Half of this suite reads a window of source and asserts what is inside it, and the WINDOW is where those guards rot — silently, because a guard reading the wrong text still reads green. GUARD-3 fixed the instances it found; three more were written during the very next ticket. So this stops fixing instances: **one validated way to take a window** (`sim/guard.mjs`'s `region`), **37 hand-rolled ones converted**, the 22 manual "this moved out from under the guard" length assertions folded into the helper, `ok`/`eq` refusing a message-less assertion **at call time**, and a **meta-guard reading all three suite files** for the three fragile shapes — with `guard-8: allow` markers that demand a written reason. **The repair found two windows the audit did not:** the BACK-parent census read a fixed 4,000 characters and was silently leaving the `deck` screen out of it, and the persist effect's four negative assertions read a fixed 220. **And a correction to the audit itself: its "2 message-less assertions" were both false positives** — a line-based scan reads the first line of a two-line call as a whole one. Eight injections negative-tested, including gutting each of `region`'s own checks. Off-band by construction — no engine content changed. **The seventh audit's remaining items are all P2/P3:** `CARD-18` (four singleton mechanics), `SKIRM-7` (the Circuit gets no partner and no ambience), `SAVE-6`, `ROUTE-15`, `NARR-15`, `A11Y-9`, and the sixth set's last item `ART-4`. Previously: **v10.18 — the SEVENTH audit opened and its first finding shipped: `MODE-1`.** Eight lenses, six of them clean — a shorter audit than any before it, and short honestly rather than padded. The one live bug was mine and two versions old: five flags say what KIND of attempt this is and each mode-start set only its own, so a daily after a challenge quoted somebody else's code and terms, and an abandoned daily followed by an FA **banked the FA's score as the daily** (581 against a real daily's ~353, corrupting score, streak and week). Same shape as SAVE-2, so it gets the same shape of fix: one `modeReset()` that every start spreads, guarded structurally so the class cannot come back. **The audit's other P1 is `GUARD-8`: the guard suite is still fragile** — 4 fixed-length source windows (three written during v10.17's own ticket) and 2 negative assertions over unchecked `indexOf` slices, which is the GUARD-3 vacuous class. Two fixed windows already had to be repaired mid-session. That is the recommended next item, because it is what lets bugs like MODE-1 through. Previously: **v10.17 — `NARR-14` shipped: somebody is out there with you, and they wanted something else.** **One item left in the sixth set: `ART-4` (an image of the send).** Four partners come out with you and speak when you tie in, at the fire, after a burn and at the end of the trip. A partner is TEXT AND NOTHING ELSE — ROUTE-10 and ENG-24 established that this project pays narrative in information, never in power, and a guard proves nothing in `resolve`, `powerAgainst`, `biteAgainst`, `startBurn` or `autoPlay` reads the partner API. Their one mechanic is an OPINION: each would take a particular line, says so before you choose, and has a different word depending on whether you took it. Who you get is derived from the run seed, so a shared seed hands over the same person (UX-5). **Two things the render caught that review did not:** the direct line still read “four more cruxes” next to a panel showing 3 (SIM-6 retuned it the version before and left the sentence) — now guarded by `ROUTE-14`, which checks every line's prose against its own numbers; and the run-end guard sliced a fixed 3,200 characters, so the partner's closing line pushed a needle out of the window and it failed for the wrong reason. That is the second fixed-window guard repaired this session. Previously: **v10.16 — `SIM-6` shipped: the tuning policy can see the feet lane. A better ruler, not an easier climb.** Nine items done across the sixth set and its follow-ups; two remain (`ART-4`, `NARR-14`). `autoPlay` chose the feet by max Support and never asked whether a foot WORKS its hold; it now scores the clear and what the foot will pay once planted, so ENG-32's trade is priced. **Worth +1.9 points of measured completion with NO game rule changed**, and the final model reads n=2700 **52.7 — still on the ~52 pin, so the re-pin this was flagged as needing never happened.** A correction to the ticket as I first logged it: the LANE is ~38% of all clears, the BLINDNESS was ~1.9 points — different claims, and only the second was a measurement error. **What it really broke was the calibration of two other guards**, because they were measured through the same weak policy: a competent policy pulls the guide line ahead of the constrained ones, so ROUTE-8's traverse and direct were both retuned (the guard is a statement about CONTENT, so the better measurement wins), and CARD-9's lift went negative, bringing `WIND_PUMP` 0.45 → 0.22. **If you touch the policy, expect to re-read CARD-9.** The guard also caught two things in me: a value model that could not tell a Flag from a Rock Over (ENG-32's floor makes them identical on the placement turn), and an assertion that passed only by hand ordering. Previously: **v10.15 — `SOCIAL-2` shipped: a challenge is a problem AND the terms it goes on.** Eight of the sixth set are done; two remain (`ART-4`, `NARR-14`), plus the queued `SIM-6`. SOCIAL-1 could only paste a RESULT; a challenge code carries a seed and one of DAILY-2's objectives, which is what makes it something to beat rather than a link. One generator serves the daily and challenges both. **The guard earned its keep twice here:** the hand-rolled first check character summed `seed % 36` and let **210 single-character typos read back as a valid DIFFERENT problem** — so it is ISO 7064 MOD 37,36 now, which provably catches every single-character substitution and adjacent transposition; and an injection then showed the newer-build case in the guard was being refused by the checksum rather than by the bounds test it was meant to exercise. Off-band (a challenge is a skirmish). Next: `ART-4` (send image), `NARR-14` (a partner), and `SIM-6` — the tuning policy is blind to the feet lane, which under-measures about a third of the climb and **will move the band, so it wants a re-pin decision rather than a quiet fix.** Previously: **v10.14 — `VIS-8`: the climb screen was visibly broken for four versions and is fixed.** Reported off a phone with a screenshot. `.board` has named the climb screen's lane rows since v0; the season board added at v10.9 (`DAILY-3`) was called `.board` too, the later rule won, and both lane rows were squeezed into a 30px flexbox with the hold cards overflowing across the header. Renamed `.wkboard`. **The interesting part is why nothing caught it for four releases:** `tsc` cannot see a CSS collision, no guard rendered the climb screen's LAYOUT (the ones that touch it read `innerText`, which is unaffected), and while checking a card caption for CARD-17 I had a screenshot of this exact breakage in front of me and read past it. The fix is a generic tripwire — a bare class may only be declared once, plus an assertion that a stray backtick has not truncated the stylesheet (that has now bitten twice) — and a geometry check on the climb screen rather than a text check. Previously: **v10.13 — `AUD-2` shipped: the game has a sense of place, and the finale has none on purpose.** Seven of the sixth set are done; three remain. The beds are per-place (forest, desert, the alpine wind, a camp stove, the trading post, the van's radio) and **synthesised on the existing WebAudio layer** — which is worth stating because the roadmap claimed for seven versions that Tone.js was already a dependency and it never was; a guard now fails if an audio library or a fetched asset ever appears. **The finale is silent, and that is the feature:** `bedFor` returns `none` there and it outranks the act, while act 3 is otherwise the windiest place in the game, so the silence says something. The design answer to the obvious problem — *I cannot hear a test* — is the split: **the decision is a pure function in the engine and only the oscillators live in the screen**, so the part that can be WRONG is tested even though the part that can be UGLY is not. Verified in a browser that the graph builds exactly as specified and does not stack across renders; **not verified that it sounds good, which needs ears on a device.** Next, unstarted and unapproved: `SOCIAL-2` (challenge codes), `ART-4` (send image), `NARR-14` (a partner), plus the follow-up `SIM-6` (the tuning policy is blind to the feet lane, which under-measures a third of the climb). Previously: **v10.12 — `ENG-32` shipped: the feet lane is a trade and finally says what it is doing.** Six of the sixth set are done; four remain. ENG-32 is the most instructive ticket of the set. Measuring BEFORE designing showed the premise was half wrong: the feet lane already works about **39% of every hold worked in a campaign** — the depth was hidden, not absent — so the deliverable became making the existing trade real and legible rather than inventing one. Support is **earned** now: a rushed placement gives the hands less, and a high-Power foot works its hold and leaves the wall before it can ever settle, so stepping up costs you what standing still would have paid. **Three corrections got it there, all the same shape — a global change landing on something's IDENTITY rather than on a player's choice.** Charged flat it was 2.0 points and broke two guards (a floor fixed that). Freshness first read `settled`, which the Trad Dad's own signature pins to zero for ever, so that climber paid every turn for being itself (`set` is a separate field now — planted is not settled). And charged on turn 1 it taxed every burn's START, which is all a Second Wind burn is, inverting CARD-9 to a negative lift. Two precedented knock-ons: the Comp Kid was **bought back** with `quickFeet` rather than lowering its 5% floor (the CARD-15 pattern), and `WIND_PUMP` went 0.55 → **0.45** — not back to 0.4 — because 0.55 read a lift of −1.3 and 0.4 read 3.8 against a ceiling of 4, so both ends are coin flips and the middle is chosen for MARGIN. Final band **n=2700 51.7%, on the ~52 pin**, whole ledger green. A standing follow-up worth a ticket: **`autoPlay` picks feet by Support alone and is blind to the fact that a foot can work its hold** — an ENG-25-shaped blind spot, left unbundled on purpose rather than stacking a second on-band change. Next, unstarted and unapproved: `AUD-2` (ambience — must be **synthesised**; Tone.js is not a dependency), `SOCIAL-2` (challenge codes), `ART-4` (send image), `NARR-14` (a partner). Previously: **v10.11 — `CARD-17` shipped: the last dead cards in the game are now a tax you can pay off.** Five of the sixth set are done; five remain. CARD-17 is a small change with a disproportionate amount of verification behind it, and the verification is the point: opening the four BONUS curses on disk showed they were **worse than the roadmap recorded** — a played bonus goes to the *discard*, so paying to play one bought nothing AND handed the card back on the next reshuffle. Writing one off now exhausts it for the burn for its printed cost + `CURSE_TAX`, which is CARD-16's own deferred direction (a removal mechanic, not a per-turn effect that compounds) and deliberately a worse deal than the camp's free permanent `cut`. Two lessons from earlier tickets were applied rather than re-learned: it is **visible to `autoPlay`** (ENG-25 — a mechanic the policy cannot use is measured as dead) and it is **surfaced on the card in hand** (because `cardHints` only renders on draft screens, so the option would have been invisible exactly where it is used). **Measured band-neutral, paired at n=2700: 53.0 vs 53.3** — and n=2700 rather than n=900 because ENG-21 established that the smaller sample cannot resolve a question this size. Next, unstarted and unapproved: `ENG-32` (feet lane depth), `AUD-2` (ambience — must be **synthesised**; Tone.js is not a dependency), `SOCIAL-2` (challenge codes), `ART-4` (send image), `NARR-14` (a partner). Previously: **v10.10 — `ENG-21` shipped: the route acts on the climber, not just the stone.** Four of the sixth set are done (the daily trio plus ENG-21); six remain. ENG-21 was the first genuinely ON-BAND item of this set and it earned its measurement budget twice over. The feature is three moves that engage the player rather than the wall (`spit` rejects a card the hold will not take, `pin` denies a shake-out in a lane, `stance` hands you the Support you never placed) — board and lane only, no flat pump term. **Two of the three were wrong on the first cut and both were caught by measuring, not reviewing:** `stance` paid a card draw and shipped DEAD (`refillAndDraw` tops the hand up at end of turn, so the card was absorbed the same turn), and `spit` both destroyed the card and reordered two RNG draws — where the reshuffle ALONE moved the CARD-9 wind arm +2.9 and read as ENG-21 breaking that ceiling. **The methodological finding is the one to carry forward: n=900 cannot resolve a 1.5-point band question here.** An earlier pass chased five weight configs that all sat inside one SE of each other and concluded the wrong SIGN; a paired n=2700 pass settled it (55.6 → 54.1, ~0.8–1.5 harder). Shipped with a stated joint balance pass — `WIND_PUMP` 0.4 → 0.55 — because a less reliable burn makes a RETRY worth more and CARD-9's lift had gone 2.4 → 4.0; trimming ENG-21 instead only reached 3.8, which is a coin flip inside a ~2.3 SE rather than a fix. Next, unstarted and unapproved: `CARD-17` (cleanse at a cost), `ENG-32` (feet lane depth), `AUD-2` (ambience — must be **synthesised**; Tone.js is not a dependency), `SOCIAL-2` (challenge codes), `ART-4` (send image), `NARR-14` (a partner). Previously: **v10.9 — the daily trio is closed: `DAILY-1`, `DAILY-2`, `DAILY-3` shipped in the order Evan set.** The sixth set is not an audit — it is a brief: three items on giving people a reason to come back tomorrow, three on the game you actually play, four brand new. The three retention items are done and **seven remain**. Each one found the honest version of its problem rather than the stated one. `DAILY-1`: `dailyStreak` was a number on the save that exactly one deed read, and `bankDaily` granted nothing at all — it pays a ladder now at 3/7/14/30 days in **kit and cosmetic titles only**, the kit landing in a carried **larder** the App hands over at run start, which is what keeps the pinned ~52 band unable to feel it (the harness calls `newRun` directly and can never be handed a larder); missing a day is forgiven **once a week**. `DAILY-2`: the daily was one number, so the optimal line was the optimal line forever — two objectives a day now, announced before you tie in, off a **derived** seed. That derivation produced the segment's most useful correction: the comment first claimed sharing the route's seed would change today's problem, a **negative test proved it false** (each daily function builds its own RNG), and the real risk is *correlation* — the grade and the objectives locked to one number. Two existing guards were **sharpened, not loosened**, where the new payout collided with them. `DAILY-3`: four weeks make a season that finishes — a board, a title off the total it ENDED on, and tomorrow's conditions posted on the menu (computable since SKIRM-3, never shown). Its guard also needed correcting: the first version assumed a calendar date started a season and failed for its own reason, so the boundary is derived now. **All three off-band by construction; 146 core + 114 balance guards pass, and every ticket was negative-tested (three, seven and nine injections).** Next, unstarted and unapproved: `ENG-21` (the route acts on you), `CARD-17` (cleanse at a cost), `ENG-32` (feet lane depth), `AUD-2` (ambience — **must be synthesised; Tone.js is not and never was a dependency**, a false claim now corrected in the backlog), `SOCIAL-2` (challenge codes), `ART-4` (send image), `NARR-14` (a partner). Previously: **v10.7 — `DAILY-1` shipped: coming back day after day finally pays something.** The sixth set is not an audit — it is a brief Evan set: three items on giving people a reason to come back tomorrow, three on the game you actually play, four brand new. He set the order too: **DAILY-1, DAILY-2, DAILY-3 first.** `DAILY-1` found the honest version of the problem — `dailyStreak` was a number on the save that exactly one deed read, and `bankDaily` granted nothing at all, so the game asked for a habit and paid nothing for keeping one. It pays a ladder now at 3/7/14/30 days, in **kit and cosmetic titles only**, and the kit lands in a carried **larder** the App hands over at run start rather than in your hands — which is what keeps the pinned ~52 band unable to feel it, since the balance harness calls `newRun` directly and can never be handed a larder. Missing a day is forgiven **once a week**: one rest day, not an open door. Next: `DAILY-2` (objectives on the daily's one seed — note there is no rest counter on `GameState` yet) then `DAILY-3` (a four-week season, plus tomorrow's conditions teased on the menu). Previously: **v10.6 — the FIFTH audit is fully shipped, all ten items, in the order Evan set: the SAVE trio (v9.99), the GUARD repairs (v10.0), `GUARD-1` (v10.1), `SAVE-3/4` (v10.2), `DEV-3` (v10.3), `DEV-2` (v10.4), `DEV-1` (v10.5), `UI-4` (v10.6).** This audit used lenses never surveyed before and it paid: it found the worst bugs of five audits, and they were all *reachable* — an IMPORT that destroyed the save while reporting success, a campaign win that vanished if you closed the tab on the epilogue, `xp: undefined` freezing levelling for good from a one-field import code, a Circuit that was never written down at all, and a service worker that could pin a stale or broken build forever. It also turned the discipline on itself: **some guards could not fail** (one provably so since v0.4) and **the balance ledger was never run by a release** — both now fixed, with every guard repair negative-tested, and `ship` gated on the ledger while costing ~30% fewer runs than before. Previously: The guard work came second on purpose: across four audits every ticket got a guard, but some of those guards **could not fail**, and the balance ledger they all lean on **was never run by a release** — so repairing that is what makes the other 41 items trustworthy. `GUARD-2` was provably green (its regex needed `className=` but scanned the one file forbidden to contain it — vacuous since v0.4). `GUARD-3` fixed three guards that die silently when an anchor is renamed, including the very one whose comment records the last time it silently matched nothing. `GUARD-4` fixed a trade asserted by restating itself (Deadpointing's halved Contact was untested anywhere). `GUARD-6` added `test:slow`/`check:slow` and put the ledger in front of `ship` — and paid for the gate by cutting ~4,800 of ~16,100 campaign runs (three guards read one journal band but paid for three), with the measured values byte-identical. **Every repair was negative-tested** — each one now fails when the thing it guards is actually broken. Previously: This audit used lenses never surveyed before — save/persistence, the device reality (touch/PWA/safe-area), the **guard suite itself**, and a rendered pass on the screens UI-1/UI-2 had not touched — which is why it found more, and worse, than the fourth: 22 candidates, every claim opened on disk. The trio was straight data loss: **`SAVE-1`** an unreadable slot was indistinguishable from an empty one (so a corrupt save got overwritten with a fresh game), one renamed card threw out of `spawn` and took the whole save with it, and neither IMPORT nor WIPE actually applied — IMPORT reported *"Imported. Reloading the slot."* and never reloaded, so the persist effect wrote the stale game back over it; **`SAVE-2`** the run was dropped on disk the moment you tapped into any boss (a boss is the last tier and starting a climb pre-writes tier+1), and the persist allowlist excluded every screen after a send — so closing the tab on the **epilogue lost the campaign win itself**; **`SAVE-5`** `level`/`xp` were the only fields loaded without a default, so `xp: undefined` made `gainXp` return NaN and **froze levelling permanently** — reachable from a one-field import code, since `importSave` validated one field. All off-band; verified end-to-end in a browser (reload resumes, xp reads 0/35 not NaN). **The audit's meta-finding is the one worth acting on next: some guards cannot fail** (one is a provable tautology — it greps `engine.ts` for `className=`, which another guard forbids there), and **the dated balance ledger never runs on a release** (`ship` does not pass `slow`, and there is no CI). **v9.98 — the fourth audit closed 10 for 10.** Previously: `ROUTE-13` finally landed, and the road to it was the interesting part: it was blocked by `CARD-9`, so a **balance-headroom session** went after the cause and found the *item* was wrong, not the guard — a Second Wind's retry started on an empty clock, worth +6 points, which is the "skip" that ceiling exists to forbid. `BAL-15` made a wind burn start part-pumped (like a caught fall), the lift fell 6.0 → 2.0, clearance went 1.7 → 5.7, and `ROUTE-13`'s 25 named holds then fit with the ~52 pin held. `ENG-28` fixed a live bug found on the way (three readers took a hold's ability from its BASE type, so the preview shed a phantom pump on Icebox Corner and the spotter shouted the wrong beta). Before that, `UI-2` rebuilt the main menu as tiles and added a title card (reported off a phone: "boring, and not clear what goes to what"); pure presentation, band untouched. **The fourth audit closed at 10 of 10: `UI-1`, `ENG-25`, `BAL-13`, `CARD-15`, `ENG-26`, `ENG-27`, `RUN-13`, `CARD-16` and `FA-1` shipped. `ROUTE-13` is DEFERRED — it hit a real balance wall.** The fourth is a four-lens survey — engine/balance · content/narrative · run-loop/meta · a rendered UI pass on the real build — every load-bearing claim verified on disk. **`ROUTE-13` finding (v9.94):** the full ~20-signature pass holds the ~52 base band (grip-tuned) but breaks `CARD-9` — the Second-Wind kit-run climbs to ~62% against its 58% ceiling ("an extra burn is a leg-up, not a skip"). The mechanism is a design tension, not a tuning miss: a distinctive signature hold is exactly what a bought extra burn beats on the retry, so signatures inflate burn value regardless of grip (harder signatures *lower* the base but *raise* the retry value). And the clean v9.94 kit-run is already 56.3% — the `CARD-15` re-pin to ~52 left only ~1.7 pts of headroom under `CARD-9`, so there is no room for a band-active signature pass at all. Deferred to a dedicated balance session (create band headroom, or rethink the Second-Wind ceiling, or purely-cosmetic signatures that skip the stat-change requirement). Also surfaced a latent exactness bug — `previewPump` reads a hold's base ability (`HOLD_STATS`) not `abilityOf`, so the existing `wetjug` signature (a jug overridden to Greasy) makes the preview shed a phantom pump (logged below). `FA-1` made THE FA a real first-ascent mode (the menu button had run a plain skirmish that never reached the claim) and turned the claimed grade into an economy — sandbag for quiet XP, spray for loud cash at the Ego risk, honest for a little XP (`claimStep`, extracted into the engine per SIM-5); off-band (the sim's honest-claim stub never calls it, XP feeds the built loadout). `CARD-16` gave the three inert MOVE curses (Tweaked Pulley, Tweaky Finger, Cold Shut) the same hex teeth CARD-13 gave the others, so every placeable curse costs a position; the four BONUS curses are a documented carve-out (an active effect there risks the ENG-23 pump-compounding trap). `RUN-13` recorded the Circuit walk-off (the good outcome; history only showed the losses) as a won-circuit record — kept out of the Lost-Line `runs`/`wins` metrics on purpose (folding it in would corrupt the finale success rate). `ENG-27` made the roped caught-fall honour the same fall-skin modifiers a boulder fall does (Long Game/Static's `dFallSkin` drawback and Crash Pads' `skinSave` were both ignored on rope); band holds at the ~52 pin. `ENG-26` closed two exact-preview breaks (allCrux tax keyed on the crux FLAG not the Committing ability; same-turn hex/restChip grip was invisible to the preview) — preview-only, band-neutral by construction. **`CARD-15` was the hard one and produced the audit's most important finding: the ~46 pin was structurally held by the reward pool being thin.** The pool was a frozen 22-card subset, so tough/friction/static and the synergy specialists were undraftable;
