enriching it cannot hold ~46 (even a same-size two-card swap read 53.7%), and `BAL-13` proved the early bosses cannot open offsetting headroom (they are not the completion bottleneck — act 3 is, and it is climber-spread-protected). So — with Evan's sign-off — `CARD-15` shipped as a **deliberate, dated re-pin to ~52** plus a joint balance pass: the pool is a global lever, so it lifted the ROUTE-8 **direct** line into a +7.8 free win (retuned `dClear -1→0`, now −2 vs guide) and sank the **Onsighter** under its 5% floor (bought back `firstTurnPower 2→3`, to 8.8%). Everything green at the new pin — drift, ROUTE-8, climber-spread and the journal guard all re-verified. `BAL-13` had shipped a version earlier as a band-neutral feel win (The Priest and The Hourglass now kick into a real second phase near the top, matching the act-3 bosses; arc, not difficulty). `UI-1` reworked the climb screen — the route now reads as cool **stone** against your warm **paper** cards (they were one tan wash a shade apart), a cleaner pump meter (the muddy 45° hatch replaced by a solid tint with a colour-blind-safe dashed cap), a real type scale, and carved section headers (THE ROUTE → YOUR MOVES → YOUR HAND) — pure presentation, so the pinned band could not move. `ENG-25` then made the 14-card Opposition mechanic visible to the tuning sim, which had scored it as dead in **both** halves: the drafter (`cardValue`) had no `opposes` term and the policy (`autoPlay`) filled one hand lane at a time against an empty partner, so a pair never formed. Both fixed — the drafter builds toward the pair, the policy seats it — and the on-band re-pin **held**: n=300 46.7, n=600 46.5, dead on the ~46 pin and tighter than the 44.7/47.2 it replaced (opposition made visible is a balanced archetype, not a hidden lever); the NARR-11 journal guard was raised n=250 → n=600 the same way ROUTE-8/CARD-9 were, once the property was verified monotonic there. `CARD-15` (the frozen 22-card reward pool) is the one on-band item left; the rest are off-band or preview-only. Three surveyor leads were discarded on disk verification (the "dead bail path" — `PSYCHE_BAIL`/`result:'bail'` live at `App.tsx:649`; the intentional `EXPOSED_*` no-ops). **v9.85 — the third audit is fully shipped (10 for 10). All three audits closed — thirty items, thirty guards.** The first two audits shipped in full (v9.56: 10, v9.66: 10), and now the third (four-way survey, every claim verified on disk; one surveyor lead discarded on verification — the "exposure psyche cost is dead" claim, live in `App.tsx:639`). `META-10` fixed the mastery deeds (a `purist` predicate that could never match, and two deeds that read the 20-deep rolling history and so could un-earn themselves — now backed by durable `archWins`/`mutatorWin` records). `VIS-7` gave every map node the non-colour `▲/▼` forecast cue. `A11Y-8` rebuilt the accessibility tree (polite log, real headings, modal climb sheets). `CARD-13` then gave curses teeth — a curse dumped on a lane now sharpens that hold (`hex`), so a curse costs a position, not just a draw — **and in building it, uncovered that `spawn()`/`makeDeck()` had been silently dropping the `restChip` field, so `CARD-11` (Chalk the Hold) shipped DEAD**; the same fix resurrects it, and a new tripwire test asserts `spawn` carries every field a card defines so this class of bug can't recur. `CARD-12` gave the singleton effects breadth (`Pounce`/`Bail Out`/`Latch And Look`) and — while doing so — exposed that the traverse map line was a quiet free win the noisy n=150 `ROUTE-8` guard had been missing; the traverse was retuned to a real endurance trade and the guard raised to n=500. `RUN-12` gave the weekly ladder its first hook (an `In Season` deed and a share-the-week button). `SKIRM-6` gave the Circuit a boon at its deep zones (10/14), so the endless deck can break a rule, not just grow. `UX-19` finished the text-size work (scroll lists scale with `--fs`, cap to `dvh`). `ENG-24` then gave the top of the flow bar a payoff for decks with no momentum/dyno to spend it on — dialed in (`FLOW_HIGH`), you **read the coming holds**. The audit's obvious fix (a high-flow clock relief) is the exact thing `ENG-23` measured at +7 to +13 points and cut, so the reward is information (band-neutral, like `ROUTE-12`'s read), never the clock. And `CARD-14` closed it out: the four-item consumable stub grew to seven with a grip-cut (`Tick Stick`), a skin repair (`Skin Salve`) and a psyche restore (`Pep Talk`), so the trading-post one-shot line stops repeating fast. **Notably, three of the ten (`META-10`, `CARD-13`, `ENG-24`) surfaced latent bugs or a documented trap the audit's literal suggestion would have re-introduced — the ledger and guards earned their keep.** The standing backlog is now the older pre-audit items: `ENG-21`, `BAL-13`, `AUD-2`, `NARR-9`, `ENG-9`, and the ship items. **Both phase gates still sit with Evan.**

## Phases & gates

| # | Phase | Scope | Gate |
|---|-------|-------|------|
| 1 | **Design lock** | Core loop spec, ~40-card starter list, 10 Act-1 boulders, name, art-direction sheet | Evan signs the one-page loop spec |
| 2 | **Combat prototype** | One boulder fully playable — pump/flow/projecting, ~20 cards, DOM+CSS hand | One battle is fun for 10 minutes |
| 3 | **Run loop** | Act 1 node map, deckbuilding (add/remove/upgrade), camps, shop, skin/psyche across a run, win/lose | A full Act 1 run, start to finish |
| 4 | **Narrative & events** | Journal pages, dialogue, 15+ events, skirmish v1 | The story lands from play alone |
| 5 | **Full campaign** | Acts 2–3, conditions fully online, The Lost Line finale (beta-gated), 2nd archetype, meta-unlocks | Campaign beatable, ~3–5 hr |
| 6 | **Polish** | Audio, tutorial-through-play, sim-driven balance pass, juice | Stranger-ready |
| 7 | **Ship** | Pages TWA → Play Store, itch.io, Capacitor, devlogs | Live |

**MVP cut line:** bouldering only (no rope systems), Act 1 only, one archetype, ~40 cards, minimal skirmish.

Four-lens survey (engine/balance · content/narrative · run-loop/meta · a rendered UI pass), every load-bearing claim opened on disk. `UI-1` (new family — full-screen UI overhauls) was the headline; `ENG-25` cleared the first on-band item with the pin held; `BAL-13` gave the flat early bosses real arcs (band-neutral); `CARD-15` refreshed the reward pool as a deliberate re-pin to ~52 plus a joint balance pass (ROUTE-8 direct line + the Onsighter retuned to hold everything green). > **The seventh set (v10.49+) — "it still feels like the gameplay needs more to it."** Evan's words, and the diagnosis behind these ten is arithmetic rather than taste: **248 cards against seven wall abilities, every one of them a static modifier to the same turn's sum.** Every turn asked the same question and only the hand changed the answer. Ten items, all aimed at the same gap — a decision that outlives the turn it was made in. Order set by Evan: **HOLD-1 first (shipped v10.49).** Four new families registered by this set: `HOLD` (the wall's abilities), `SEQ` (sequences), `FEET`/`LANE` (the board's geometry), `INFO` (what the player is told), `PUMP`/`COND` (the clock and the conditions).

| ID | Item | What & why | Status | Pri | Depends on |
|----|------|-----------|--------|-----|-----------|
| HOLD-1 | Holds that change while you are on them | The seven wall abilities were all static, so WHICH hold you left never mattered — only how many. A Greasy hold now **sweats up** while you leave it hanging (+1 Grip a turn, cap 3), keyed on the ability so the board's own GREASY label is the rule, stopped by brushing, telegraphed on the hold and in the tutorial. **Measured 44.2% against the 44.3% pin; the lever proved live at 4x (42.8%).** | ✅ v10.49 | P1 | — |
| HOLD-2 | The three holds on the board ignore each other | **Shipped, and the design the row implies was measured dead before it was built.** A hold that eases when its NEIGHBOUR CLEARS fires on **97.7%** of turns — over 45,488 campaign turns both hand holds are up on **97.2%** and something nearly always clears — so "the neighbour went" is not a condition in this game, it is the default, which is the near-constant shape CARD-20 already paid to learn. **What varies is the ORDER: both hand lanes are clearable on 34.7% of turns.** ENG-18 made the order the player's and it has only ever mattered for blow-outs. So `Chained` — a **flake** gives **2 Grip** to whoever lets the other hand go first — and the cost is the rule that already exists: the lane you send first may blow before the second resolves, taking its opposition and its `weight` with it. **Keyed on the order and not on the clear**, because a dyno's clear is an RNG roll and a rule firing on it could never be previewed exactly; UX-4's 100%-accurate preview is a pillar guarded in three places. One predicate serves `resolve` and `previewLane` (the ENG-19 shape), and **the policy sets `s.order`** or the ability measures at zero — ENG-25 for the sixth time. **BAND-NEUTRAL, AND THE DECOMPOSITION THAT SAID OTHERWISE WAS NOISE.** Three arms at **n=3000**: 58.9 without the flake, **59.0** with the flake and the ability stripped, **59.1** shipped — all inside 0.2, so the hold type is free and the ability is below what the band can resolve. The same three arms at **n=900** read **61.9 / 57.0 / 60.1**, and that 4.9-point story — "the hold type costs 4.9, the ability gives back 3.1" — was reported before it was resolved and is retracted here with its numbers. **The band at n=900 cannot resolve a texture change**; this is the third instance in one session, after CARD-20's carrier buy-back (80.4 → 76.5) and ROUTE-8's traverse (+4.4 → −0.6). **WHAT IT IS WORTH IS A RATE, NOT A BAND DELTA:** a flake is on a hand lane on **7.1%** of turns — a characteristic, the weight FEET-1 settled on for Solid — and the chain gives on **99.7%** of those. That second number is the honest limit of the claim: **the policy always takes the discount**, so as MEASURED this is a discount with a skill floor rather than a dilemma. The trade is real in the rules and only bites when going second costs you an opposition partner, which the policy does not weigh; a policy that prices that is a later ticket. Guard: the predicate (second gives, first does not, a foot neither grants nor claims, a brushed flake loses it), the preview and the resolution agreeing on a hold set to turn on the order alone, the policy's ordering clause, and the weight ceiling. **6 injections — one of which found a dead branch in the ticket's own code**: an `i > 1` check beside a `1 - i` lookup, redundant and each short-circuiting the other, so neither could ever be broken. One explicit mechanism now. 202/202 core, 132/132 slow. | ✅ v10.77 | P2 | HOLD-1 ✅ · CARD-20 ✅ |
| SEQ-2 | Sequences are a side quest | **Five defects, three shipped.** Two plan cards were named after other cards bar the case of "the" (and the built deck held the *common* twin); a plan died to one bad draw, so the four were ranks 57–60 of 61 bonuses and the valuation was right to refuse them — `SEQ_GRACE = 1` now; and the odds model was the wrong shape with an off-by-one. **Band 44.2% on the 44.3% pin.** My row's premise was wrong: a running plan is already legible (`A11Y-9`'s banner). | ✅ v10.50 | P2 | — |
| SEQ-3 | A plan is still not worth buying, and cannot be bought | **The three-part design held, and the missing piece was a measurement nobody had taken.** SEQ-2 found a real inconsistency — `seqValue` priced a payout with `bonusValue`'s coefficients and never applied `BONUS_WEIGHT` to them, so `draw` was worth 2.2 here and 7.0 next door — then measured that fixing it made the game *worse*: plans went from ranks 57–60 of 61 bonuses to ranks 7–34, and with one purchasable, completion fell **44.3% → 40.3%**. The 3.2× was cancelling an unmodelled cost: a plan **constrains the play while it runs**. What was missing was **how often each condition is met anyway** — measured over 88,609 climb turns of the real campaign: **`clear` 83.0% · `norest` 39.3% · `rest` 60.7% · `feet` 95.2%.** So the cost is not one number: three turns of keeping your feet on is nearly free, two turns of *not* resting takes the play you'd have made on three turns in five. Priced per-condition (`SEQ_BASE`, `SEQ_CONSTRAINT = 9`, derived as a denied rest at `shed 2 × 1.4 × BONUS_WEIGHT`), the weight applies without inflating anything — because the plans it inflates most are the ones that cost most to run. Purchasable via the **post shelf, not the reward pools** (SEQ-2 measured pool dilution at 44.2% → **26.6%**: four cards into pools of eight and four removes the old options rather than adding one), **stage-picked** so it perturbs no roll — the precedent the kit and the rack beside it already set — and **gated on the deck**, so a plan you cannot run is never offered. **Measured after: the shelf showed a plan on 1,900 visits, 423 affordable, 226 bought; 33.8% of runs end holding one; across 3,322 plan turns 1,170 paid, 929 spent the slip and 140 broke outright.** Band **42.1% against the 42.6% baseline** — −0.5, inside the 0.9pt SE, where the same three changes without the constraint priced measured −4.0. 192/192 core, 117/117 kept, 8/8 injections. | ✅ v10.62 | P2 | SEQ-2 ✅ |
| DECK-5 | The builder is blind to any card whose value depends on the finished deck | **Premise dead — measured, not argued.** The row rested on `Link It Up` scoring −1.2 against an empty deck and +25.0 finished. That figure does not reproduce: against the deck it wants it is **+6.0**, and against the deck the builder actually makes, **+4.8**. Swept all 219 collectable cards for the gap between an empty deck and a finished one: **only 2 move by 3+**, and **only 4 price negative against an empty deck** (`Link It Up`, `Quiet Feet`, `Take It All In`, `Find The Rest`). In the built deck those four score **4.8 · 2.5 · −3.0 · 0.2** against a built-deck **minimum of 11.8** — so the builder is not blind to them, they are genuinely worse than everything it chose, and excluding them is correct. DECK-4's synergy cap removed most of what deck-dependence there was in `cardValue`. All four are plan cards, which is the row that *was* real: **SEQ-3**. | ❌ premise wrong | P3 | DECK-4 ✅ · SEQ-3 ✅ |
| FEET-1 | The feet lane is one lane | **The row was wrong twice.** The choice of foot was already a real trade (38 cards, 25 profiles, zero dominated pairs; lane filled 77.5% of turns). What was missing: Support only ever reads 1 or 2, and the wall could only push it **down** — `Featureless` zeroes it on 28.9% of footholds, one weather and two windows subtract, and the only positive dSupport was a shoe. The plain foothold — the one entity on the board with no ability and no text — is now **`Solid`: a settled foot pays +1 Support**. Both marks keys list the footholds at last. **45.0% against the 44.3% pin** after reweighting the easy pool. | ✅ v10.51 | P2 | ENG-32 ✅ |
| RUN-14 | The map is a corridor | **The row was wrong: branches differ in kind at 18 of 26 stages.** What was true is that `ACTS` is static, so the map was byte-identical in every run ever played. `tierNodes(s, tier)` now makes a stage's contents a property of the run — **16 of 26 stages vary, 2000 seeds give 2000 shapes** — swapping only the non-climbing nodes. **43.8% against the 44.3% pin.** Two versions measured and thrown away (dropping a node: 41.2%; swapping any node: 39.2%).  **AMENDED v10.65 (NARR-22):** the swap could also take the two kinds the pool cannot hand back — the **trail node** (11 a run became 10.56) and the **FA node** (3 became 1.93) — and the trail-node half cost **nine points** of the known ending in this one version. The rule is now *the swap may only take what it can give back*. The map still varies; it varies the support and not the story. It was also inflating camps 9 → 10.55, which had been propping the Alpinist up by ~1.4 points.| ✅ v10.52 | P2 | ROUTE-16 ✅ |
| GUARD-10 | The band drifted for eight versions and the guard could not see it | **The failure this exists for happened, while every ticket was being honest.** COND-2 −0.5 · COND-3 +0.5 · COND-4 −0.1 · COND-5 −0.4 · LANE-1 −1.7 · DECK-4 −0.2 · SEQ-3 −0.5 — every one inside its own standard error, every one reported band-neutral in good faith, and the sum walked the campaign from the **44.3 it was pinned at down to 42.1**. That is BAL-14's compounding, which the ledger guard exists to catch, and it did not catch it because it *cannot*: it reads `campaign 300` where the SE is ~2.9, so its tolerance has to be **±6**, and ±6 cannot see a 2.2-point slide. Buying that resolution in simulation is ~9,000 extra campaign runs on a slow suite already running 11,300. So the number is bought **once, by hand, at n=3000** by whoever ships the version and written into `sim/band.mjs`; the guard over it is arithmetic and runs in the FAST suite on every `npm run check`. **The teeth:** the newest ledger entry must be the current `package.json` version, so **a release without a measured band fails** — and the failure message hands you the exact command. Plus: the sample must be ≥1500 (a cheap reading cannot be passed off as a dear one), and the tolerance must be **smaller than the ledger's own widest step**, so a tolerance wide enough to pass the drift it was written for is itself a failure. Applied to the history above, a pin of 44.3 at ±2.0 would have failed at v10.62 — one ticket later than ideal, but an argument instead of an accumulation. Backfilled with all eight measured bands. Band **46.4%**, unchanged, which is what a ticket that touches no game rule should look like. 193/193 core, 118/118 kept, 6/6 injections — two of which collided on a shared anchor and were caught by GUARD-9's own meta-check.  **AMENDED v10.65 (NARR-22):** the ledger carries an `ending` column too, under the same teeth, because the known-ending rate had been sliding the same way and nobody had written a number down. **AMENDED v10.66 (BAL-18):** the ledger carries a third column, the five-climber ladder at n=2000, and a **column, once started, cannot be dropped** — checking only the newest entry left a hole you could reverse into. The ladder is also the one column whose recorded value gets **re-measured**, which the band never can.| ✅ v10.64 | P1 | — |
| SIM-9 | The policy every measurement is taken through cannot plan | **Shipped as ONE CLAUSE, and two of the row's three claims retracted by measurement.** The row said the policy cannot hold a card for next turn, cannot spend a turn setting something up, and cannot value knowing what is coming. Measured before designing anything: **74.6% of failed burns die of pump and the 30-turn clock binds on 0.0%** of 2,780 — the binding resource spent as if it were the free one — so "spend a turn" is the whole ticket, and the other two claims are not real. HOLD A CARD: a placed hand card **stands 1.09 turns and 83.7% blow the turn they land**; among turns with two clearing candidates only 4.9% differ on the next hold, and greedy is accidentally right on 84% of those. There is no second turn to hold for. VALUE WHAT IS COMING: **zero, even for a policy that plans** — a rest depth read off the upcoming holds moved nothing at `readAhead` depth (79.8) or with a full oracle (79.4, against 79.9 flat, paired n=1200), because holds arrive only when the one in front clears, so the policy always SEES the hold it is choosing for. The consequence (reads need an UNCERTAINTY-limited policy, not a planning one — the sim reads exact grip through the WOBBLE) is dated onto INFO-2's row; the card-lifetime number onto CARD-20's. WHAT SHIPPED: `autoPlay` shakes out a lane nothing in hand clears once pump reaches `SHAKE_AT` — swept 0-7 at paired n=1200: 77.2 / 79.3 / 79.6 / **79.9** / 77.8 / 75.8 / 73.4 against greedy's 58.8, so 4 — and the gate is deliberately the only clause, because every smarter rule measured WORSE on the same seeds: rest-only-when-3-Power-short 72.8, rest-only-while-a-clearing-card-exists-in-the-cycle 60.5 (backwards — the gain IS pacing the holds nothing clears, interleaving cheap grinds with recovery), stop-at-dusk 79.8 (nothing). Worth **+21.1 points of session send** mid-ladder, **+8.2 on V8-V9** (12.3 → 20.5), **+13.6 on roped** (21.0 → 34.6 — the biggest, because a caught fall costs the pitch). THE BAND CANNOT FEEL IT AND DID NOT MOVE — **60.3% at n=3000 against the 60.0 pin** — because the built deck's two rests are FEET cards (Knee Bar, No-Hands Rest): a hand rest is in hand on **6 of 7,610** no-clear lane decisions, and the paired arm read 59.8 against 59.9 at n=900. The row's "it would also move the band" was wrong for a legible reason, and the band conversation it asked for closes with the pin untouched. The LADDER is where the lever reaches (every climber loadout carries Shake Out x2), and **all five climbers rose** at n=2000: Onsighter 8.6 → 14.2, Boulderer 9.8 → 13.1, Comp Kid 8.0 → 9.7, Trad Dad 9.7 → 10.3, Alpinist 11.3 → 11.4 — floor cleared by 7 SE, spread 1.46x. (At n=400-600 the Trad Dad and Alpinist read as DECLINING, ~1.5 SE each, and two gates were nearly designed to fix it; both readings were noise. The handoff's own warning, hit again and written down again.) Ending **82.1 against the 82.5 pin**. AND TWO PREDICTED RECALIBRATIONS DID NOT HAPPEN, measured rather than assumed: **CARD-9 held** (wind lift inside its 0-4 window — the wind arms ride the built deck the branch cannot reach; the handoff predicted a WIND_PUMP retune and none was needed) and **the builder is NOT underbuilding** the mechanic the policy can now spend — swapping the built deck's No-Hands Rest for a Shake Out reads **51.8% against 60.3**, 8.5 points worse (the swap also frees a rare slot, so the pure rest-kind share is smaller, but the sign is not in doubt), so the feet rests the builder picks are measured right and `cardValue` gets no shed term. The whole slow suite passed first run, 132/132. Guard: `node sim/run.mjs policy` A/B — `REST_AT=99` is the pre-SIM-9 policy exactly, the knob kept the way SHARP_AT is — asserting a **+10 floor under the measured +21.1 FIRST**, then the mechanism (6.91 vs 2.37 shake-outs/session), then the knob. The order is a finding the injections bought: written instrument-first, every arms-level mutation tripped the shake-out comparison and **the lift assertion had never once failed** — the LANE-3 shape, one failure guarded twice with one copy standing in front of the other — so the claim now sits where every arms-level failure lands and the instrument assertions behind it say WHY a lift died. 4 injections — and designing the fourth found the property is ROBUST TO WHICH REST RULE: rest-when-you-could-clear still pays +12.9, and always-rest-one-lane read **82.0 against the shipped 79.9** at paired n=1200 (~1.7 SE — not a measurement yet, and outside the sweep family; noted, dated 2026-08-22, for whoever tunes the policy next). Only resting at the WRONG PUMP kills the lift (plan arm +0.1 with the gate inverted — an injection that also drags `REST_AT=99` along with it, `pump < 99` being always true, which is what exposed the shielding above). | ✅ v10.71 | P2 | INFO-2 · CARD-20 · LANE-3 |
| QA-1 | The v10.45 tutorial fix had never been checked on a real phone | **Checked, on a Z Fold 6 running the installed PWA — and it found two bugs, one of them a single line of CSS that broke scrolling.** Reported as *"the scrolling only works if I scroll on top of the COMMIT and TAKE IT BACK row"*. **ONE: TWO SCROLL CONTAINERS.** The sheet said `html,body{overflow-x:hidden}`, and `overflow-x:hidden` forces the OTHER axis from `visible` to **auto** (CSS Overflow: `visible` computes to `auto` when its partner is `hidden`). So that one declaration made **both html and body scroll containers** — measured in Chromium, before and after: `[hidden auto]/[hidden auto]` became `[clip visible]/[visible visible]`, two vertical scrollers down to one. DEV-3's `overscroll-behavior-y:contain` — which is right, and stops an over-flick pull-to-refreshing a climb away in a standalone PWA — then blocks a drag in the INNER box from chaining out to the viewport. On a layout where the overflow lands on the viewport rather than on body, every drag in the page does nothing while a drag on the **fixed** control bar, hit-tested against the viewport scroller, scrolls normally. Fixed with **`overflow-x:clip` on the root only**: `clip` clips the axis *without* creating a scroll container, which is the entire reason it exists, and the root is where `overscroll-behavior` reaches the viewport from. **TWO: UX-19's SPACER SAT IN THE MIDDLE OF THE PAGE.** It reserves the fixed bar's measured height, and it was rendered **before** the advisory stack and the log — so the MATCHED box, COMING UP, FROM THE GROUND and the last three log lines sat under the bar with no way to scroll them clear. That is the empty gap in the second screenshot and the text peeking from behind the bar in the first. **UX-19's hit-test could not have caught it:** its own comment says the overlap was "found by hit-testing, not by looking at it", but none of those boxes exist on the first frame of a climb, so at the moment it measured there was nothing after the spacer to hide. Verified in Chromium at four viewports, before and after: `log` under the bar at full scroll → nothing. **THREE: the hand strip now declares its axis.** `.hand` is a horizontal scroller with `overflow-y:hidden` and had `touch-action:auto`, so a browser may claim a vertical drag that starts on a card for a scroller that cannot move vertically. `pan-x` says the strip owns horizontal and the page owns vertical. Chromium passes it through by choice, not by rule — **which is why the reported symptom could not be reproduced locally** at 390×844, 390×700, 360×640, 313×700 or 1.3× text scale. **Carve-outs and self-inflicted wounds, recorded.** GUARD-8 caught this ticket's own new guard taking a window off an `indexOf` — the exact rule it exists to enforce; it uses `region()` now. The first "nothing follows the spacer" check fired on the sheet overlays' `.row` children, so it is scoped to the climb screen's own flow content. Backticks went into a CSS comment inside a template literal **twice**, once in a comment that says "No backticks in here". And SHIP-4's injections, written one version earlier, **rotted on the very first bump** because both anchored the literal version string — the same mistake GUARD-10's release anchor made twice; they are anchored off the markup now. 196/196 core, 131/131 slow, 4/4 QA-1 injections. **Still not verified:** that the reported symptom is gone on the actual device. | ✅ v10.68 | P1 | UX-19 ✅ · DEV-3 ✅ |
| LANE-3 | `cardValue` carried an urgency that inflated the bar it was judged against | **Shipped, and the reason it was parked turned out not to exist.** The row's original mechanism was wrong and was corrected when it was built: the valuation's `synergy` and `tagOf` terms are **ZERO in both decks**, and the real cause is the **feet urgency** — a feet card is worth **+7** to a deck under a quarter feet and **+1** to one over, a sevenfold cliff at a hard threshold. `bestOffer` measured its candidates against the deck's own mean *through the same function*, so **a deck short of feet inflated the bar it declined against** and the deficiency made the fix harder to buy. The concentrated deck holds 3 feet in 15 (share 0.20, +7 each) and rates **15.31**; the spread deck that beats it by 10.9 points holds 4 in 15 (0.27, +1 each) and rates **13.93**. The urgency alone is **1.13 of that 1.38 gap — 82%**. Fix: the urgency becomes `needBonus`, added to CANDIDATES only, and the bar is computed **bare**. The urgency itself stays — removing it is worth +16.9 points and is LANE-4's question. **It was parked for two releases on a number that was an artifact.** The stated cost was "~2–3 points of NARR-21's known-ending rate, and that guard is already hanging by a tenth of a point". Both halves were the broken instrument: the cost was read off the **wins ratio** that falls when the game improves, and the tenth-of-a-point margin was the 18-point gap NARR-22 closed. Re-measured on the careers band: **63.8% against 62.9% — +0.9, positive.** Band **45.2%**, identical. Ladder inside **0.3** on every climber. **Two errors in the parked code, corrected rather than shipped.** Its comments gave the spread deck as **13.93** in one place and **13.66** in another; only 13.93 yields the 1.38 gap both of them claim, so 13.66 was the typo — resolved by arithmetic, not by re-running. And the `OFFER_BAR` note claimed the multiplier had been "re-tuned to put acceptance back where it was" when the constant is **0.8**, exactly the literal it replaced. Nothing was re-tuned: I predicted restoring acceptance would restore the band, measured 0.86 and 0.88 at **52.2% and 53.7%**, and was wrong — acceptance and the band are not the same lever. Recorded as what happened. **And a red guard that was a duplicate, not a regression.** The drift test also asserted `pcts[0] < full - 5` under the message "reading his journal is worth N points" — word for word the same claim, and the same message, as the NARR-11 guard four tests above it, which measures at n=600 where this reads n=300. It failed at **5.0** against a bar of *more than* 5, failing by exactly nothing. The launch's real ending cost is **−0.9 at n=720 — a draw**; the guard was firing on the slice, not the game. Resolution bought — NARR-22's own precedent, 60→240 once before — ENDING_N is 720 now, the guard reads it from band.mjs so ledger and guard cannot disagree, pin 76.7, tolerance 5 (~2.3 SE, still four points inside RUN-14's step). Ladder inside noise of v10.71 on all five. 198/198 core, 132/132 slow, 6/6 injections — one catches the launch firing off a set FOOT (this row's own objection smuggled back), and one each for the two `set` boundaries. | ✅ v10.72 | P2 | CARD-19 ✅ · SIM-9 ✅ |
| PUMP-1 | Pump is a single rising line | **One of three claims held.** Pump falls on 23.5% of turns (two-way already); pump-out IS the only failure that fires (32.2%, daylight 0.0%); and the second clock existed and never ticked — `TURN_CAP` 30 against a mean burn of 5.7 and a longest-ever of 19. **Measured: there is no room for a second pressure** (a cut from turn 8 costs 3.0 points, because a smaller hand compounds into the pump clock). So the day CLOSES from turn 12 — the hand shrinks each turn, re-arming the pump clock a grind was beating. **43.9% against the 44.3% pin.** | ✅ v10.55 | P3 | — |
| COND-2 | Conditions are set before you tie in and never move | **The row was right, and narrower than it reads.** Weather is rolled once per burn and never moves — but the reason is structural: `WEATHER[...].dContact` is **baked into every card's contact at `startBurn`**, so shifting `s.weather` mid-burn leaves every card in hand carrying the old number (the ENG-26 divergence). ROUTE-6 had already solved this correctly with the weather WINDOW, which touches only the live-read modifiers (`dBite` in `biteAgainst`, `dSupport` in `powerAgainst`) — so the window is the mechanism, not the weather. What was actually broken: only **2 of 37 routes** carried one, and every one was a **one-way switch** — it shut at `at` and stayed shut to the top. Measured over 435,320 climb turns: **2.17% of climb turns inside a live condition**, 7.98% even on a route that could have one. Windows now **pass** (`until`, checked in `windowOf` so resolve and the preview read one clock), and are **announced** when they do (`clear`, required wherever `until` is). The game's harshest window — the Hanging Slab's ice band, which its own note always said *lets go in the afternoon* — became temporary, which paid for mild `dSupport -1` windows on **Sun Dagger**, **The Blowhole** and **Whiteout Slab**. The finale keeps the one permanent window: a set-piece you fight to the top through. **Turns inside a live condition 2.17% → 4.97%**, routes with a window **2 → 5** (4 temporary), 6,344 windows climbed out of and every one announced. (Of 6,416 pass lines, ~72 are followed by a rewind — you clear the height and blow the burn on the same turn. Pre-existing ordering, shared with `— THE WINDOW —` and the phase lines, not a COND-2 regression.) **43.5% against the 44.3% pin** (−0.8, inside the 0.9pt SE), 121/121 balance and 190/190 core. | ✅ v10.56 | P3 | ROUTE-6 ✅ |
| COND-3 | The weather itself still cannot move | **The row was right; my estimate of the cost was wrong in both directions.** `WEATHER[...].dContact` was applied once, in `startBurn`, and written into every card as it was dealt — so the sky could not move during a burn without leaving every card in hand carrying the number it was dealt with (ENG-26). I had priced the unbake as touching "every card display, the preview, `cardValue` and the drafter": **wrong** — `cardValue` and the drafter read LIBRARY cards and must not change, and the in-burn readers are only **4 in the engine and 5 in the UI**. What I had missed is worse and is the real reason the number was baked: **`resolve` wrote the remaining Contact back into `contact`**, so the stat and the wear were one field. Moving the weather to a live read without splitting them charges it again every turn a card holds on — measured, **40.8% against 43.5%**, caught by the negative control. So `contact` is the stat, **`spent`** is the wear (the same split ENG-32 made between `settled` and `set`), and `contactOf(s, c)` is the one live formula, beside `powerAgainst`/`biteAgainst`/`supportNow`. **The unbake alone reproduces the campaign exactly — 43.5%, same act-by-act deaths, same turns** — which is the proof it is a refactor and not a change. Then the payoff: a window may carry `dContact`. **The Chimney** (act 2) gets a temporary one — the back of a chimney is where the water runs — and it is **band-neutral at 43.5%**. The finale's window gets one too, because journal page 3 has said from the start that *the crux is a waterfall*, and **its `dBite: 1` is gone to pay for it**: Bite+Contact together measured 42.4% (−1.9 from the pin, over 2 SE — a real move), the swap measures **45.0%** (+0.7, inside 1 SE). So on the finale a point of Bite is worth ~2.6 points of completion and a point of Contact ~1.1, and zeroing the weather's Contact term entirely is worth **6.3** (43.5% → 49.8%) — which is why the window's share of it is one point, on one stretch, on two lines. 190/190 core, 111/111 kept, 6/6 injections. | ✅ v10.57 | P4 | COND-2 ✅ |
| COND-4 | `drizzle` is the only weather whose text lies | **I wrote this row and it was wrong.** The literal fact holds — `drizzle` carries `dContact: 0` — but the conclusion I drew from it does not. Measured (`sim/run.mjs conditions`, send% over four mid-ladder routes): **crisp 89 · still 57 · freezing 45 · humid 42 · drizzle 41 · hot sun 31.** Drizzle is already the **second-hardest sky in the game**, and it is 33% of alpine forecasts, where 30% of runs die. Its text is accurate to its terms: `sloperGrip: 3` is *slopers are off* and `dSupport: -1` is *your feet are going nowhere* — humid owns hands-greasy (`dContact: -1`), drizzle owns feet-and-slopers, which is a deliberate split, not an oversight. **Shipping the row's proposal measures 41.2% and takes drizzle to 28% send** — the hardest sky in the game, past hot sun — so a Contact point there costs 3.8 campaign points. Not shipped. **What the measurement did find is next door: `forecastScore` disagrees with it.** That function is a difficulty claim — the map paints the node and says *in nick* / *out of nick* off its sign, and the reward policy picks the best-conditioned climb off its order — and it carried a flat **−2 for `sloperGrip`, as heavy as two whole points of Bite**, while never reading `dSupport` at all. VIS-3 had already measured slopers as near-worthless in a comment (*"only 6 of 231 cards care about slopers"*) and the score weighted them hardest anyway; the result was **humid rated the worst sky of the six while it measures fourth of six**. The measurement also *vindicates* the part I did not touch: a point of Bite costs ~30 points of send% and a point of Contact ~14, so the long-standing 2:1 weighting is right and is kept. Now `-dBite*2 + dContact + dSupport`, which ranks crisp > still > {freezing, humid, drizzle} > hot sun against a measured 45/42/41 for that cluster — both ends exact, one honest tie. Every sky's SIGN is unchanged, so nothing the player is shown flips. **44.9% against 45.0%** — band-neutral, and the honest reading is that the mis-ranking cost the policy almost nothing, because the three skies it was confused about are within four points of each other. The value is the signal being true, not the band. All six texts audited against their terms: five exact, one loose (crisp's *"rubber sticks"* pays in Bite, not Support). **It also tripped CARD-9, and the control cleared the ticket:** changing which climb the policy walks toward turned that guard red, but at v10.57 — *before* the change — the Second Wind lift already measured **+0.7 at n=900 and +0.1 at n=3000**. A difference of proportions at n=900 carries a ~2.3pt standard error, so a true lift of ~0.1 passes a `> 0` floor about half the time: **the guard had been green on a coin flip since v10.55**, and this ticket re-rolled it. The cause is PUMP-1's daylight clock — past `DUSK_AT` the hand shrinks every turn, and a burn that *starts* at 45% pump is a short burn, so the two multiply. Bar untouched; the **item** repriced on the dial this contract has always used, `WIND_PUMP` 0.45 → **0.30**. Lift now **+1.3 at n=900 and +1.0 at n=3000** — inside the bar from both ends and, unlike before, the two sample sizes agree, which is the real test of whether an item helps. Pinned band untouched at 44.9%, because the default arm spends zero winds. 191/191 core, 111/111 kept, 123/123 with the ledger, 7/7 injections. | ✅ v10.58 | P4 | COND-3 ✅ |
| COND-5 | The forecast verdict ignores half the forecast | **The row was right and my example was backwards.** `forecastScore` was handed `{weather, rock}` and read only the weather, while the map printed `⛰ granite` directly beside the ▲/▼ verdict. Measured the missing half properly — a new `sim/run.mjs rock` mode, send% per rock **per style**, because `conditions` averages the rock over four routes that happen to be mixed/crimp-ladder/compression with no slab, power or jug haul: **compression** 37/42/37/30/33 · **power** 16/24/21/20/22 · **mixed** 19/32/27/26/28 · **crimp ladder** 10/16/18/18/18 · **slab** 6/12/11/9/11 (granite/sandstone/limestone/gneiss/basalt). I wrote *"granite is sharp — punishing on a crimp ladder, fine on a jug haul"*: granite is in fact the **harshest rock on four styles of five** and simply **absent on the fifth** — compression has no crimp weight for its `boost` to land on. And the case that really needs the style is the other way round: **sandstone is the kindest rock overall and the fourth-kindest on a crimp ladder**, because its `bite` lands on exactly the holds that style is made of. So the term is **derived, not fitted** — `buildRoute` multiplies the style's hold weights by the rock's `boost` and deals holds carrying its `grip` and `bite`, so the expected cost of a hold is that same combination, with **no chosen coefficients**. It agrees with **18 of the 22 pairs** the measurement is clear about (>4pt), and is right about both flat-term failures. Centred on the mean rock for the style, because *"no rock"* is not something you can draw and the choice being made is between this tier's nodes — which also keeps the sign of the verdict meaning what it always did. Scaled by `ROCK_WEIGHT = 0.7`, from the measurement: a model unit is ~10 points of send%, a weather unit ~14. **The mechanism fires hard:** over 3000 seeds and 78,000 tiers, **50.0% of tiers offer a choice of climb**, **56.4% of those were tied on weather alone** — settled by node order — and **the rock now decides 22.2% of them**. Band **44.5% against 44.9%** — neutral, which is the honest result: the forecast is now *true* on a decision the player makes constantly, without making the game easier or harder. Also collapsed the four duplicated tint-and-arrow ternaries on the map into one route-aware helper, and VIS-7 (the colour-alone guard) was repointed and **strengthened** — it now also asserts the shared tag still contains the ▲/▼ cue, since one helper is now the single place it could be lost. 192/192 core, 111/111 kept, 7/7 injections. | ✅ v10.59 | P4 | COND-4 ✅ |
| LANE-1 | Three lanes, no relationship | **The premise was wrong twice over.** Measured over 86,251 climb turns of the real campaign: there are **five** hand-to-hand cross-lane terms and **six** on the feet-to-hands axis, and a hand-to-hand rule is live on **56.8% of turns** — opposition 63.5% counting both sides, Squeeze 21.4%, support 86.1%, Featureless 26.4%, Two-finger 15.5%, campusing 4.6%. What IS true is narrower and sharper: **the two rules carried by CARDS are dead** — `fx: 'guard'` fires on **0.03%** of turns and `fx: 'weight'` on **0.12%** — and the cause is not value (`Untouchable` prices at 22.3 against the built deck's max of 22.1) but availability: **only 4.8% of runs ever hold one of those eight cards.** The three cross-lane rules that DO fire are the three that need no draft — a hold ability, a board state, a hold. So: (1) the two nameless terms became a family, **core tension**, with a specialist (`Front Lever`) — CARD-19 named opposition on exactly this argument and left these two behind, and its own guard demanded every card with a `synergy` or `opposes` term be nameable while ignoring `fx`; (2) `weight` was the only cross-card term priced at **zero** in `cardValue` and is now priced at the board it is actually paid on (`WEIGHT_BOARD = 1.86`, measured); and — honestly — **neither moved the incidence** (0.04% → 0.03%), because availability is the gate, which is DECK-5's row, not this one. (3) So the ticket's own rule lives on the **board**: **matched hands** — both on the same kind of hold, a card on each, **28.2% of turns**, read by nothing before this. It pays a breath (shed 1) and it takes your opposition away (a seated pair is *alone* while you are square to the wall, biting on 14.2% of turns). **The two-sidedness is measured, not decorative.** Every version that only gave something: both hands covered for a Bite point **+5.0** against the pin, second hand only **+2.1**, the shed alone **+2.4**, shed + a softened cost **+1.8**; the pure cost **−1.1**. The parts are strongly **non-additive** (shed +2.4 and cost −1.1 combine to −1.5, not +1.3), so it was searched rather than inferred. Shipped at **42.8%** against the 44.3 pin — 1.6 SE, and the ledger's drift guard accepts it. Two fixture bugs of my own found on the way: the shed fixture started at 0 pump (startBurn resets it) so it measured 0 against 0, and it compared a jug against a pinch, measuring their abilities instead of the match. Also fixed a real divergence the ticket created and the guards caught: `matched` first read `GameState`, and `spit` takes a card off mid-turn, which split the preview from resolve on 34 of 1750 turns; and the shed sat below the lane loop, where resolve has already added pump, which split them on 315. 192/192 core, 113/113 kept, 125/125 with the ledger, 10/10 injections. | ✅ v10.60 | P3 | — |
| LANE-2 | The loadout builder's family nudge is costing the game ~11 points | **Confirmed, corrected, shipped, and it forced a re-pin.** First the correction: this row cited DECK-5's `Link It Up` −1.2/+25.0, which is **dead**. What survived — the builder snowballs on whichever family it starts with — is true and expensive. Raced three decks built by the **same value function and the same structural gates**, changing only `v += 3 + have * 2`, at n=3000 (SE ≈ 1.3): **as shipped** → 9 of one family, three duplicate pairs, **42.1%**. **Removed** → biggest family 4, no duplicates, **45.5% (+3.4)**. **Inverted to a penalty** → 7 families, biggest 3, **53.0% (+10.9)**, deaths down in every act. Monotonic across three settings of one expression, and **`cardValue` prefers the worse deck** (15.31 vs 13.93 for the one that beats it — the valuation carries the same bias, which is LANE-3). **Removed, not inverted**, on Evan's call: the +3.4 is the clean single-variable claim, the +10.9 conflates which cards got picked (two spread decks measured 7.5 apart). **THE BAND IS RE-PINNED ~44 → ~45 (v10.63)**, because every difficulty decision from v10.23 to v10.62 was calibrated against a builder mistake — and the shipped band had *also* drifted 44.3 → 42.6 across COND-2..SEQ-3, each step inside its own SE and compounding, which is the BAL-14 failure mode the ±6 tolerance was loose enough to miss. Removing the bonus moves the band back **through** the old pin. **AND IT BROKE DECK-4, one ticket after it shipped:** DECK-4's payoff keyed on a family of `COMMIT_AT = 8`, reachable only because the bonus handed you 9 for free — removing it took the payoff from **94.3% of runs to 11.2%**. So `COMMIT_AT` is retired and the gate is now **merit**: the specialist for your largest family, substituted only when it beats the card it displaces. Back to **92.2%**, it can never make the shelf worse, and there is no threshold to recalibrate next time the builder changes. 192/192 core, 118/118 kept, DECK-4 8/8 and LANE-2 2/2 injections. | ✅ v10.63 | P1 | LANE-1 ✅ · DECK-4 ✅ · DECK-5 ❌ |
| DECK-4 | The deck is a pile, not a plan | **The opposite was true, and worse.** Measured over 600 finished runs: **every single run** ends with **11 cards of one family** and about **4.5 families** big enough to pay a specialist, and only 0.68 cards per deck have no family at all. Decks are heavily shaped. What was broken was the **payoff**: the drafter was offered a specialist **2,394 times and took it ZERO times**, and 1,841 of those offers arrived with **none** of that specialist's family in the deck. The deck's shape paid Power on **4.7% of runs**. Root cause, and it was not the price or the odds: **`REWARDS` is a curated shelf of 27 cards holding exactly TWO of the game's twelve specialists** (Crimp Specialist, Pocket Poacher) — so for **nine of the eleven families** a deck can commit to, the card that pays for the commitment **was not obtainable at all**, and the two that existed were offered uniformly whether you were building crimps or not. Fixed by letting the deck's own commitment fill one offer slot: at **`COMMIT_AT = 8`** cards of a family — measured, the point where a specialist's weak body (1/5) finally beats an ordinary 3/7 reward card: 6.5 at zero of its family, 8.7 at three, 10.9 at six, 12.4 at eight — the shelf offers you that family's specialist, searched across **every card** rather than the curated pool. **Specialists reaching a deck: 11.2% → 94.3% of runs. The shape pays on 4.7% → 94.0% of runs.** Two things had to be paid for. The synergy bake was **never balanced**, because nothing could ever collect it: uncapped it measured **+6.0** points, so it is capped at **`SYNERGY_CAP = 1`** and `cardValue` is capped identically (it was pricing +4 Power the rules would never hand over). And the tailored card is itself worth **+3.1** — being shown the card your deck wants *is* the reward — so it **displaces the best card on the shelf** rather than dropping into a spare slot. **42.6% against the 42.8% baseline** at n=3000, band-neutral. **A process correction:** the first six comparisons in this ticket were run at n=600, where the campaign SE is ~2.0pt, and one dial read +2.0, −2.8 and −0.5 at three adjacent values — non-monotonic noise I briefly took for signal. Everything shipped here is measured at n=3000. 192/192 core, 114/114 kept, 8/8 injections. | ✅ v10.61 · gate reworked v10.63 | P3 | — | **v10.63 (LANE-2):** the `COMMIT_AT = 8` gate only ever worked because the loadout builder's family bonus handed you 9 of one family; with that bonus removed the payoff reached 11.2% of runs instead of 94.3%, so the count is retired for a **merit** gate — the specialist for your largest family, substituted when it beats the card it displaces. Back to 92.2%, and it can never downgrade the shelf. How often it fires depends on which specialist the family maps to (70.8% of shelves for crack, 1.0% for crimp — LANE-3).

> **The sixth set (v10.7+) — Evan's brief, not an audit.** Ten items to a shape he set: **three on coming back tomorrow**, three on the game you actually play, four brand new. Order set by Evan: DAILY-1, DAILY-2, DAILY-3 first — **all three shipped (v10.7, v10.8, v10.9). The daily trio is closed; seven items remain.**

| ID | Item | What & why | Status | Pri | Depends on |
|----|------|-----------|--------|-----|-----------|

A rung ladder at 3/7/14/30 paying kit into a carried `larder` plus cosmetic titles, and one forgiven miss a week. **Off-band by construction** — the larder is handed over in the App, so `newRun` (and the harness) never sees it. | ✅ v10.7 | P1 | — |
| DAILY-2 | The daily is one number | Two objectives a day on the same seed, announced before you tie in, paying score and XP. Needed two new per-burn counters (`rests`, `cruxFree`) — the note about there being no rest counter was correct. **Off-band** (score is unspendable; the XP rides the daily, which the harness never plays). | ✅ v10.8 | P1 | DAILY-1 ✅ |
| DAILY-3 | Nothing is coming tomorrow | A four-week **season** — running total, days climbed, a four-column board, and a title off the total it finished on — plus **tomorrow's conditions** on the menu (the tease was indeed free; the key was already there). Titles reuse DAILY-1's list. **Off-band** (cosmetic title, unspendable total). | ✅ v10.9 | P1 | DAILY-1 ✅ |
| ENG-21 | The route acts on you | Three moves that engage the climber rather than the stone: `spit` (the hold rejects a card it will not hold), `pin` (no shake-out in that lane), `stance` (the Support you never placed). On-band; shipped with a joint balance pass — `WIND_PUMP` 0.4 → 0.55 to hold CARD-9's contract. | ✅ v10.10 | P2 | ROUTE-6 ✅ |
| CARD-17 | Cleanse at a cost | A bonus curse can be **written off** mid-climb — exhausted for the burn for its printed cost + `CURSE_TAX`. Verified they were worse than dead first (a played bonus recycles to the discard). Visible to `autoPlay` per ENG-25, and surfaced on the card in hand. **Measured band-neutral** (paired n=2700: 53.0 vs 53.3). | ✅ v10.11 | P2 | CARD-16 ✅ v9.93 |
| ENG-32 | The feet lane has no depth | Measuring first showed the lane already works **39%** of all holds — the depth was hidden, not absent. Support is **earned** now (a rushed placement gives the hands less), floored so it bites the solid placements, exempt on turn 1, and the lane reads what it is doing. Shipped with a `quickFeet` buy-back for the Comp Kid and `WIND_PUMP` 0.55 → 0.45. **n=2700 51.7%, on the ~52 pin.** | ✅ v10.12 | P2 | — |

Fixed: it scores the clear *and* what the foot will pay once planted, so ENG-32's trade is priced. Worth **+1.9 points of measured completion with no game rule changed** — a better ruler, not an easier climb — and the final model reads n=2700 **52.7, still on the ~52 pin, so no re-pin was needed.** It did re-calibrate ROUTE-8 (both lines retuned) and CARD-9 (`WIND_PUMP` 0.45 → 0.22). | ✅ v10.16 | P2 | ENG-32 ✅ |
| AUD-2 | The game is silent between sounds | Beds per place — forest, desert, alpine wind, a camp stove, the post, the van's radio — all **synthesised** on the existing WebAudio layer (Tone.js never was a dependency; that row was wrong for seven versions). **The finale is silent on purpose.** `bedFor` is a pure engine function so the decision is testable; its own switch. | ✅ v10.13 | P3 | — |

Off-band (a skirmish). | ✅ v10.15 | P2 | SOCIAL-1 ✅ |

Image and paste read one projection so they cannot drift. **A browser found the sheet rendering in the wrong component**, drawn correctly and invisible; A11Y-8's guard counted sheets by a literal `2`; and the comment-strip rule bit a fifth time, so `stripComments` is shared now. | ✅ v10.32 | P3 | SOCIAL-1 ✅ |

> **The SEVENTH audit (v10.18).** Eight lenses, several never used before: content reachability · card-text truthfulness · rendered extremes · **invariant fuzzing** · card conservation · phase reachability · the guard suite again · mode/state leakage. **Six came back clean, which is the headline** — this is a shorter list than any previous audit and it is short honestly rather than padded. Every claim below was verified on disk or by running it.

| ID | Item | What & why | Status | Pri | Depends on |
|----|------|-----------|--------|-----|-----------|
| MODE-1 | Modes leak into each other | Each mode-start set only its own flag. A daily after a challenge quoted somebody else's code; an abandoned daily then an FA **banked the FA's score as the daily** (581 vs ~353). Fixed with one `modeReset()` every start spreads. | ✅ v10.18 | P1 | — |
| GUARD-8 | The guard suite is still fragile | Fixed-length and unchecked-anchor source windows, the GUARD-3 class again. Fixed structurally: one validated `region()` (37 windows converted), `ok`/`eq` refusing a message-less assertion at call time, and a meta-guard that reads all three suite files for the three shapes. Found two more the audit missed, one of them vacuous. **The audit's "2 message-less assertions" were false positives** — a line-based scan cannot read a two-line call. | ✅ v10.19 | P1 | GUARD-3 ✅ |
| SAVE-6 | `loadGame` did not clamp array lengths | Every array now goes through one clamp with **derived** caps, plus a scan so a new field cannot be missed. Two caps only the writer knew are shared (ENG-19). **The larder was growing without a ceiling in normal play** — capped where it grows too. And the fix reproduced SAVE-5's own NaN failure mode, so every cap is asserted to be a finite number. | ✅ v10.27 | P3 | SAVE-5 ✅ |
| CARD-18 | Four singleton mechanics | Each now has a sibling that is a trade, not a reskin (248 cards). Found: **two of my four were strict upgrades**, and **`restChip` had no drafter term at all**, so Chalk the Hold has read below the take-it line for eleven versions. Band 52.7 → 53.4, and a three-arm measurement proves it is pool reshuffle, not difficulty. | ✅ v10.20 | P2 | CARD-12 ✅ |
| SIM-7 | The measured starting deck is stat-greedy | Opened into a live bug and shipped as one: **`buildLoadout` ignored the slot rules**, so BUILD ME ONE handed a full collection ten rares against a limit of one — a deck that completed 18.0% against the starter deck's 28.1%. Fixed (44.3%), guarded structurally and measured in the slow ledger. | ✅ v10.22 | P2 | ENG-25 ✅ |
| SIM-8 | The pinned band was measured on an illegal deck | **Re-pinned ~52 → ~44.3**, agreed and dated. The band rides the engine's `buildLoadout` now, so one builder and a deck a player can hold. Only ROUTE-8 needed retuning (direct `dCrux` 3 → 2, restoring the intent its own note states); CARD-9 and the climber spread untouched. | ✅ v10.23 | P1 | SIM-7 ✅ |
| GUARD-9 | The negative tests were thrown away | Every ticket gets injections; they found the original bug every time **and three assertions that could not fail**, and all of them lived in `/tmp`. Now `sim/mutants.mjs`: **52 kept injections**, each naming the failure it expects. The anchor half is guarded on every `check`; the message half needs a run, and the header says why. | ✅ v10.24 | P1 | GUARD-8 ✅ |
| BAL-16 | The climber floor could not be measured | The guard was a coin flip: the Comp Kid read 5.2% at n=600 against a real 5.8%, and 5.8% clears a floor of 5 by **1.7 SE** — unresolvable at any affordable sample. Fixed by resolving the floor where it lives (`ARCH_ONLY`, two-pass, margin reported in SEs) and buying the climber back to 6.6% on the axis it died of (`dPsyche`, psyche deaths 19% → 1%). **Floor untouched.** | ✅ v10.25 | P2 | — |
| ROUTE-15 | Six routes had no signature | The four lines you meet **first** were four of them — the sameness ROUTE-5 set out to kill, concentrated where a new player decides whether this game has texture. > **Seventh-audit clean bill** (recorded because these are the lenses that would have found something): **content reachability** — all 244 cards in a pool, zero orphans, no dead `CardDef` fields, all 12 gear / 16 boons / 9 mutators / 31 signatures / 20 talks / every event outcome key live · **card-text truthfulness** — zero real mismatches across 244 cards (the LINES slip was a one-off from SIM-6's retune, now guarded by ROUTE-14) · **rendered extremes** — 402×874 and 320×700 at 1.0× and 1.4× text: no overflow, nothing escaping, nothing clipped · **invariant fuzz** — 1,200 climbs and 6,101 resolved turns across random routes/weather/rock/mutators/boons/gear with no crash, no NaN, no negative pump · **card conservation** — exact over 400 climbs · **phase reachability** — all 28 phases reachable (a first pass flagged 7, a false positive from a `go('…')` helper).

| ENG-21 | The route acts on your turn — **shipped v10.10** | All four original `RouteMove`s only mutated holds. Three that engage the climber shipped at v10.10: `spit`, `pin`, `stance`. Board/lane only, no per-turn pump term, and the 28-combo preview sweep round-trips at zero. | ✅ v10.10 | P2 | ROUTE-6 ✅ |
| BAL-13 | Act 1 is frictionless — **answered v10.33: no dial fixes it** | The row's number was wrong for many versions: "kills 3%" says act 1 is not *lethal*, where the complaint is it cannot *go wrong*. Measured, act 1 puts **4.3%** of runs under skin pressure against act 2's **30.2%**, touches psyche on **0.6%** against 11.3%, and takes **0.4 of 3** camps. **Five levers screened against all three bars at once** (pressure, band, spread) — the first time any attempt checked more than one. Removing recovery does nothing (the cost never accrues); a flat approach cost *raises* the band because the policy trades climbs for camps (**the slack absorbs the pressure**); and once the slack is cut, **three independent mechanisms — skin, grip, attempts — each drop a climber under the 5% floor**. Weather is differential by construction (`ignoreWeather`). **Structural, not tunable:** act 1 is the only stretch where every climber is at full resources, which is why it is frictionless *and* why pressure there separates the roster hardest. Changing act 1's **shape** is the only route left, and that is a design call. Pinned in the ledger so it cannot drift unnoticed. **A SIXTH LEVER SCREENED v10.75 — LENGTH — and it fails for a reason the first five did not have.** Act 1 cut **9 stages → 7**, same routes, same camps, shops, projects and boss (the two dropped stages' climbs re-seated so no route leaves the game). Screened against all three bars: act-1 skin pressure **4.1% → 5.6%** against act 2's ~20%, so **it does not make act 1 bite**; the band **61.9% → 58.3%** (n=900, a screen); act 1 **155 → 146 turns**. THE MECHANISM IS NEW AND IT IS WHY LENGTH CANNOT WORK: **shortening scales the cost and the slack together** — fewer climbs to be hurt by AND fewer camps to recover at — so friction is very nearly invariant to it, and what you lose is runway rather than safety. **It is also the first lever that does NOT break the roster:** spread **1.92x → 1.66x** and the lowest climber **7.8 → 8.3**, where skin, grip and attempts each dropped a climber under the floor. So it is a live option as a PACE change and a dead one as a friction fix. **Decided with Evan (2026-08-22): park it** — no act-1 change ships, because it does not fix what the row is about and would cost a band re-pin to buy two minutes. Recorded so the seventh person does not re-run it. **What is still untried:** a cost act 1 can charge in something the roster differs on LESS than skin, grip and attempts — cash or deck slots — which is speculative and unmeasured. | 🚧 → 📌 v10.33 · v10.75 | P2 | — |

| PERF-2 | The 190KB question — **answered v10.35: it does not matter** | The row was stale in both numbers: React is **38%** of the bundle (190 KB raw / 59 KB gzip against 497 / 157), not "most of 333KB", and the game is what grew. Bytes were never the question — measured under CPU throttling, a **mid-tier phone has the game up in 378 ms and repaints a turn in 50 ms**; low-end 605 ms and 110 ms. React's share of the boot is bounded at ~90 ms, against hand-rolling a renderer under four thousand lines of App.tsx. `npm run perf` keeps it runnable and the size is pinned, because the answer holds at this size and not at any size. | ✅ v10.35 | P3 | PERF-1 ✅ |
| ROPE-2 (second row) | Ropes beyond Act 3 | **Answered, and half of it had already shipped.** The row named two open things; **the multi-pitch finale variant went out at v10.48** (`specOf`: the finale you roped is three pitches) and the row stayed ⏳ for twenty-six versions anyway. Pinned by assertion now rather than re-argued. **WHAT A ROPE IS WORTH — the number a rope-first act needed before anybody authors one, and nobody had taken it.** Four act-3 lines that are not roped as authored, roped by hand, same seeds, same fourteen-card deck, 1,600 sessions an arm: as authored **8.2%**, roped with no rack **8.3%**, unroped carrying a rack **1.8%**, roped carrying a rack **10.2%**. So **the rope alone is worth +0.1 and the rope with a rack is worth +8.4** — the belays buy nothing and the whole value is in the CLIPPING (runout reset and `CLIPPED_POWER`), which is why a third pitch adds **+0.1** over a second. **A rope-first act is therefore not a difficulty change but a DECK-CONSTRUCTION one:** it would force a rack, and the rack is what pays. That reframes the row's own ask, and it is the thing to design against. **AND THE PROVISIONING NOW FOLLOWS THE RUN.** Three places asked *is there rope on this trip* — the post's rack, the clip term in `cardValue`, the hint the card prints — and all three read the static `ACTS` table, while **RUN-15 made which line fills a climb slot a property of the run**. One `ropeOnTrip(s)` walks the run's own map, cached per (run, act) because `cardValue` is called thousands of times inside `buildLoadout`. **Measured before fixing, and the drama is retracted: the divergence is LATENT, not live** — `MAP_SWAP_IN` is camps and shops, so act 3 keeps its two roped projects and **0.0%** of 3,000 seeded runs have no roped node. What does vary: **4.7%** of runs are offered no roped CLIMB, and the roped share swings run to run against a static **0.286**. So today it changes what a clip is WORTH; the moment a roped climb lands in an act with no roped project it is the difference between provisioning that works and **a hint that lies to the player**. The rack comment's own promise — *"adding a roped line to another act provisions it without anybody remembering to"* — is true again. Guard: the finale variant asserted, the derivation shown to disagree with the table on real seeds (and to agree with itself), a ropeless act reporting no rope, and all three consumers pinned to the one function. 5 injections. **Two of them misfired first and both were defects in the test-side work, recorded because they are the reusable lessons**: one patched the same line an existing mutant anchors on (two mutants cannot share a line — the second reads as GUARD-9 rot instead of as itself, so the older one moved to where the answer is DECIDED), and one was defeated by the cache evicting past 64 entries mid-loop, so the guard's seed loop is bounded at 50 and says why. 201/201 core, 132/132 slow. | ✅ v10.75 | P2 | ROPE-1 ✅ · RUN-15 ✅ |
| SHIP-3 | Pages → TWA → Play Store | **Everything that does not need a Play account is done and in the repo:** a real maskable icon (the plain one would be cropped by a round mask — the bottom hold sits 208px from centre against a 205px radius), a privacy policy whose every claim was checked against the built file, the manifest fields Bubblewrap and Play read, a Bubblewrap config guarded against disagreeing with the web manifest, and `assetlinks.json` emitted on demand and **never stubbed**. Found on the way: **`.nojekyll` is load-bearing** (Jekyll drops dot-directories, so assetlinks would 404 and the TWA would silently keep its URL bar), and **`build-html.mjs` is not idempotent** — half a build doubled the service-worker registration. **What remains needs an account, a key and a device**, and is written down in `ship/README.md`. **AUDITED v10.76, forty-two releases on, and the account-independent half had rotted in two places — both silent, both fatal at upload.** **(1) THE PACKAGING POINTED AT THE WRONG ADDRESS.** The repository is `RCJLabs/Sandbagged` and `package.json` says the site is `https://rcjlabs.github.io/Sandbagged/`, while every packaging file said **`/sandbagged/`** — the TWA's `startUrl`, `fullScopeUrl`, both icon URLs, the web manifest URL, the manifest **`id`** and the checklist. **GitHub Pages paths are case-sensitive**, so on the reading the repo itself supports that is a TWA which opens a 404, a scope covering nothing, and an asset-link check — the thing the whole `.nojekyll` note exists to protect — pointed at a URL that is not the site. *Not verified live: this environment cannot reach github.io, so the fix follows the two statements of the address that exist in the project. If the site really is served lower-case or moves to a custom domain, `homepage` is the single line to change and everything derives from it.* **(2) THE VERSION PLAY SHOWS SAT TWENTY-SEVEN RELEASES BEHIND** — `appVersionName` 10.48.0 against a shipped 10.75.0. That is **SHIP-4's failure one layer out**, on a hand-maintained file nothing touches between Play uploads. The build syncs it now, and the guard pins the MECHANISM rather than the value, because `npm run ship` runs the suite *before* the build that does the syncing and an equality check would fail every release in that window. Guard: every TWA URL and the manifest `id` derive from `homepage`, the checklist's every github.io URL must match it, and the build must still derive the id and sync the version. 4 injections — **two misfired first and both were gaps in the guard rather than the fix**: one asserted the emitted artifact while the injection edited the generator (both are asserted now), and one used `includes`, which a second correct URL elsewhere in the file masked. Off-band by construction: `src/engine.ts` and `sim/run.mjs` byte-identical to v10.75. 201/201 core, 132/132 slow. | 🔨 v10.34 · audited v10.76 | P1 | — |
| ART-3 | Store art | Feature graphic and cover drawn (v6.7). **Screenshots still outstanding and cannot be faked** — they have to come off the real build on a real device. **Correction (2026-08-15): the stated blocker is gone.** `SHIP-1` shipped at **v5.4** and `SHIP-2` at **v4.4**; neither has a row in these tables, so this dependency read as unresolved for thirty versions when it was satisfied. What actually remains is a device and a store presence, which is Evan's call, not a build task. | ⏳ | P2 | SHIP-1 ✅ v5.4 |

## Open decisions

- ~~**NAME-1 — the name**~~ — **resolved v0.5. The game is _Sandbagged_; the campaign mode is _The Lost Line_.** Slug `sandbagged`, URL `rcjlabs.github.io/sandbagged/`. Collision-checked: nothing by that name on Steam or itch, and the climbing-game shelf (Klifur, Crux PX, White Knuckle, Peaks of Yore) holds no card battler. Known cost: in *gaming* culture "sandbagging" means deliberately underperforming — roughly the inverse of the climbing sense — so general search will surface Smash and Dead by Daylight threads. Accepted.
- **Grade display:** V-scale vs Font. Default V (audience), revisit at localization.
- ~~**Beta persistence**~~ — **resolved in DES-1.** Three tiers: banks across the 4 burns of a session · dies when you leave the node · only journal pages persist across runs. Meta-progression stays narrative, never a stat drip.
- **Dyno commitment check:** a ~65% roll that ends a burn. Unsimmed and possibly punitive — resolve in ENG-1 against a live build, not on paper.

## Core loop constants (DES-1 v0.2, simmed 2026-07-27)

**3 lanes = limbs** (left hand · right hand · feet). Holds have **Bite/Grip**, moves have **Power/Contact**, resolution is simultaneous. Pump is health *and* mana.

**Updated v0.4** — supersedes the constants in the DES-1 doc.

`PUMP_MAX` **11** · `HAND_SIZE` 5 (fanned) · clock = **+1 flat per turn, +1 per unanswered hold** · `SUPPORT` +1 Power · `CAMPUS_BITE` +1 · flow −1 tax at 2+ · `BETA_GRIP` −1 · `ATTEMPTS` 4 · **exhaust-on-blow** (Anchor exempts) · Bite bumps at half rate by grade, Grip at full.

Ladder across three deck tiers (15 / 21 / 30 cards), all monotonic: Chossmaster V2 91/99/100% · Peeler V2 71/96/100% · Wasp Nest V4 4/46/81% · **The Priest V5 0/24/48%**. Burns 3.5–9.8 turns, 2.4–8.0 exhausts per burn, zero deck-outs.

**Costing law:** 1 Power ≈ 4 Contact. Contact is a *threshold* stat — every row jumps at C6→C7 (survives two typical hits) then saturates flat. Cost Contact by hits-survived, never by raw points.

**Three rules the sim proved, not opinions:**
1. **The clock is `HANG_TAX`.** +1 pump per uncleared hold at end of turn. Without it, blocking all three lanes takes zero damage and the fight never ends. With it, every lane you clear slows the clock — tempo and survival become one axis.
2. **Flow must never touch Power.** Support +1 and flow +1 stacked on base-2/3 Power made moves one-shot every hold: 100% flash at every grade. Any flat bonus larger than ~⅓ of a base stat is suspect. (v0.1 hit the identical trap with its flow discount.)
3. **Resting costs you sends.** A lane spent shaking is a lane not clearing, and the clock keeps ticking. That's the push-your-luck axis, and it means rest cards must stay tempo-negative.
4. **Defense must buy clock control or it buys nothing.** The original clock (+1 per *uncleared* hold) made deleting doubly rewarded and defense actively bad — Power beat Contact 5–7:1. Taxing *unanswered* lanes instead, plus a flat tick to stop turtling, halved that to 4:1. Any future clock rule has to leave a reason to hold a lane.
5. **Grip is superlinear; Bite is underweight.** Grip multiplies turns × holds-on-board against the clock, so a style Grip delta of +2 made a V3 harder than a V4 — style Grip deltas stay at ±1. Meanwhile Bite reaches the player only through unblocked lanes, so it lands as card attrition (3.4× spread across styles) rather than pump. `ENG-3` fixes the second half.

## Design baseline (locked 2026-07-27)

**Pitch.** Slay the Spire's run structure meets climbing, where **the route is the opponent** — routes fight back with pump, fear, and conditions. You don't kill an enemy; you top out.

**Pillars.** The route is the opponent · push-your-luck turns · falling is learning · climbing-culture authenticity · everything looks torn from the journal.

**Combat core (v0.2 — lane combat).** A **3-lane board**, lanes are limbs: left hand · right hand · feet. The route plays **holds** (Bite/Grip); you answer with **moves** (Power/Contact); they trade **simultaneously**. Grip to 0 = hold worked, +1 progress. Contact to 0 = you came off it. Survivors persist and keep trading. An unanswered lane bites straight into **Pump**, which is both your health bar and your mana — bonus cards cost pump, max pump = fall. The **feet lane** grants Support (+1 Power to hand lanes); leaving it empty is campusing (+1 Bite). **Flow** buys time, not muscle (−1 hang tax). **Projecting**: worked holds return at −1 Grip and later burns reveal the route.

**Resources.** Pump + Flow (per-climb) · Skin (run HP) · Psyche (mental currency, restored at camps) · Cash/rack (economy) · Daylight/attempts (projecting limiter).

**Cards.** **Moves** carry Power/Contact and a lane tag — crimp grip, open hand, lock off, gaston, drop knee, mantle, deadpoint, dyno (hands); heel hook, smear, kneebar, flag (feet, Support). **Bonus cards** cost pump and add effects — chalk up, breathe, silent feet, try-hard scream. **Gear** (shoes as equipment slots, chalk, brush, tape, pads) and **beta** (reveal the hold deck, cut a crux's Grip, plus cursed *sandbagged beta*). **Shake Out** occupies a hand lane and deals 0 Power — resting costs you a limb.

**Routes.** A route is a **deck of hold cards**. Hold types are stat profiles: jugs are cheap to clear and gentle (2/3), slopers are tanky but harmless (2/6), pockets and sharp crimps hurt (4/4, 4/5), cruxes are walls (4/8). Styles — slab, overhang, compression, crimp ladder — shift the hold-deck weights and demand different decks. A **conditions layer** modifies every climb (crisp = −Bite, heat = +Bite on slopers, rain = slopers unplayable). V-grades V0–V17 scale via a graduated stat bump and crux count; the finale is graded **"?"**.

**Campaign — _The Lost Line_.** Branching node map per act — climbs, camps (rest + dialogue), training (upgrade/remove), events, trading posts, boss climb. Acts: forest boulders → desert towers → alpine wall → **The Lost Line**. The first ascensionist vanished decades ago; their scattered journal pages are lore *and* the finale's beta cards.

**Skirmish.** Any unlocked route, any deck, chasing score/speed; daily seeded routes later.

**Art.** The whole UI is the journal — paper texture, ink, hand-drawn topo lines, cards as sketches. Cohesive and cheap for a solo dev.

**Tech.** The Dirtbag playbook: React 19 + TS + Vite 6, single-file `App.tsx` with engine/content split into typed tables, one-file HTML build, GitHub Pages → TWA → Play Store, Capacitor at feature-complete.

## Changelog (shipped)

### v10.55 — PUMP-1: the daylight clock had never once run out (2026-08-19)

**The seventh of the ten, and the row's three claims came apart differently under measurement.**

**"One direction" is wrong.** Pump **falls on 23.5%** of climb turns and is flat on 18.3% — it is a two-way meter already.

**"One failure" is right.** Over 10,749 burns: sends **66.9%**, pump out **32.2%**, roped falls **1.0%**, daylight **0.0%**.

**And the game already had a second clock that does not tick.** `TURN_CAP` is 30. The mean burn is **5.7 turns**, the median 6, the 99th percentile 11, and the longest that has ever happened is **19** — eleven turns of headroom past the worst case ever recorded. No burn has ever ended on daylight. The guard on it asserts the constant's *range*, so it passed for versions while the mechanic did nothing.

#### The measured answer to the row is: no second pressure, but a working backstop

Four attempts, all n=3000 against a 44.1% base:

| attempt | band |
|---|---|
| flat −1 card from turn 8 (the 90th percentile) | **41.1%** — −3.0, far outside the error |
| flat −1 card from turn 10 | **43.6%** — band-neutral, but reaches only 1.14% of burns |
| compounding from turn 10 | **43.1%** — −1.0, marginal |
| **compounding from turn 12** | **43.9%** — band-neutral ✅ |

**The first attempt is the lesson.** A smaller hand *compounds* — fewer plays, fewer clears, more hang tax, more pump — so it is a death-spiral accelerant rather than a second axis. The difficulty of this game is already concentrated in the long burn, and **anything that bites often enough to be a dimension costs three points.**

**So the day closes instead of stopping dead.** Past `DUSK_AT` you draw one card fewer for every turn the light has been going. Turn 12 sits past the 99th percentile of every burn measured, so it never touches a legitimate climb.

**And the mechanism is the nice part: dusk does not add a third way to fail — it re-arms the first one.** Measured on the guard's own grind (rests in both hand lanes every turn, holds nothing can work): hand 5 at dusk, empty by turn 17, pump 1 → 13 over the next five turns, burn over at **22** against a wall at 30. The pump clock the grind was beating by resting finally does its job.

**Telegraphed, per `ROUTE-6`'s rule:** the banner says THE LIGHT IS GOING the turn before, THE LIGHT HAS GONE after, and how many cards the dark is costing — because a hand that quietly comes up short reads as the deck being broken rather than as the day ending.

**Two bugs of mine, both caught by measuring rather than assuming.** The first guard fixture named its holds `crux`, which carries the Committing tax and converts bite to pump, so the "grind" died on turn 2 and would have proved nothing about daylight. And `DUSK_AT` was missing from the sim barrel, so my first incidence probe compared `s.turn > undefined` and reported the mechanic firing 0.00% of the time — the same omission `FEET_STATS` had in v10.51.

**Band: 43.9% (n=3000) against the 44.3% pin.**

(190 core + 120 balance incl. the ledger; **6 injections kept and negative-tested** — one of which exposed an assertion of mine matching `DUSK_DRAW` in the *import line* rather than in the sentence it was about.)

### v10.54 — CARD-19: the language cards talk in could not name half of them (2026-08-19)

**The sixth of the ten. The row said *"248 cards, and synergy is one tag-count term"* — half right, and the wrong half is the interesting one.**

**Cards DO talk.** `opposes` is a real card-to-card mechanic — a hand card is worth `OPPOSE_PAIR` (+2) beside another opposition card and `OPPOSE_ALONE` (−2) on its own — and measured, **it fires on 19.1% of turns**, with both hands played on 90.7%. So the pairing works and is common. What was broken was the **language**.

**`tagOf` is the only vocabulary the game has for "cards of a kind"** — synergy counts them, the builder nudges on them, the hints name them — and it derives a tag from **a substring of the card's own name**. Two holes, both measured:

**One: four of the ten specialists did not count themselves.**

| card | exists for | its own tag was |
|---|---|---|
| `Air Time` | dynamic | *(none)* |
| `Crack Rat` | crack | *(none)* |
| `Old Hands` | rest | *(none)* |
| `The Specialist` | crimp | *(none)* |

None of those four names contains a keyword, so each was permanently one short of its own count. A card whose entire reason to exist is a style is a card of that style; `synergy` is read first now and the name heuristic is the fallback.

**Two: `(none)` was the largest tag in the game** — 58 of 248 cards, and **47.5% of every hand move**: Sidepull, Undercling, Gaston, Layback, Cross-Through, Iron Cross, Mantle, Match, Static Reach and fifty more, invisible to the one term that makes cards talk.

**Most of that is honest and was left alone.** A Mantle is not a crimp move; inventing a hold style for it would be lying to make a number bigger, which is the opposite of the fix — and the guard asserts that some cards are *still* untagged so this cannot quietly become "every card is a style". But the **opposition** moves are a real family the game already plays, and they were the only family with no name in the table. They have one now, `oppose`, derived from the flag rather than authored, and taken **after** the word scan so no card that already had a hold style loses it. Untagged: 58 → **47**.

**And the family can now be built for.** Every other style has a specialist that counts its kind; opposition had a mechanic across two lanes and nothing to commit a deck to — which is the difference between a combination and a plan. `Body Tension` (uncommon, 1 Power / 8 Contact, Opposition · +1 Power per 3 opposition cards) is deliberately low-Power and high-Contact: not a stronger Sidepull or a cheaper Cross-Through, but the card that holds on while the pair does the work.

**A knock-on worth recording:** `Crack Rat` now values **18** against the deck `BUILD ME ONE` produces — the highest of all ten specialists — because that deck is `crack:9` and the card finally counts itself. **The builder still does not take it**, for the same greedy-prefix reason the sequence cards are never taken (`DECK-5`).
