`npm run perf` keeps the measurement runnable — it serves `docs/` itself, because over `file://` the service worker never registers and the numbers would describe a different app. **The size is pinned** at a generous ceiling (roughly a third above today): the answer holds at this size and not at any size, so it is a tripwire for a dependency arriving, not a budget to manage. If it fires, re-measure rather than reverting to a guess.

**And the repeated-fragment lesson landed three times in one ticket.** `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` and `[1, 4, 6]` each appear **twice** in the perf script — once for the boot sweep, once for the turn — so an assertion checking for either *anywhere* would pass with one of them removed, which is precisely how you end up quoting a phone number measured on a desktop. Both are counted now. A third instance was the same shape in `.gitignore`. **Count when the fragment repeats** — that is now three tickets running (`ROUTE-15`'s tagger writes, `NARR-15`'s three solo modes, and these).

One more of the anchor lesson, too: the SKIRM-9 guard first windowed the subtitle on `'<div className="sub">{st.circuit'`, so switching the branch off moved the **anchor** and GUARD-8 reported a broken guard where the guard was fine and the code was wrong. **An anchor must not contain the thing it is testing** — second time in three versions, after A11Y-9.

(173 core + 116 balance; **6 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.34 — SHIP-3: everything for Play that does not need an account (2026-08-16)
**Packaging, not porting** — one self-contained HTML file with no external requests. Everything that can be done without a Play account is now in the repository; what is left needs a Google account, a signing key and a device, and is written down in **`ship/README.md`** rather than half-built.

**What the build now emits**, all of it verified rather than assumed:

- **A real maskable icon, as its own file.** Android's safe zone is the centred circle of 80% diameter and the artwork does not fit it — the bottom hold sits about **208px from centre against a 205px radius** — so the previous manifest, which relabelled the plain icon as `maskable`, would have had it shaved by a round launcher mask and lost the contour lines entirely. `icon-maskable-512.png` is the same artwork at 80% on the same field.
- **A privacy policy** (`docs/privacy.html`). Play requires the URL for every app, including one that collects nothing. **Every claim on it was checked against the built file**: the only `fetch` in `index.html` is Vite's modulepreload polyfill, which never fires because every asset is inlined; the only one in `sw.js` re-requests the app's own files; storage is `localStorage` alone; and the sole external strings in the whole bundle are React's licence URL and the SVG namespace. A guard now fails if a tracker or a new host appears, because the page would become a false statement on a store listing.
- **Manifest fields Bubblewrap and Play actually read** — `id`, `lang`, `dir`, `categories` alongside what was there.
- **`assetlinks.json`, on demand and never stubbed.** The fingerprint only exists after the first upload under Play App Signing, so the build writes the file **only** when `SANDBAGGED_SHA256` is a real one and **throws** on a malformed one. A placeholder would be worse than nothing: it serves, verification fails silently, and the only symptom is a URL bar nobody can explain. Both paths were exercised.
- **`ship/twa-manifest.json`** — the Bubblewrap config, checked against the web manifest by a guard so the two cannot name different icons or modes.

**`.nojekyll` turns out to be load-bearing** and nobody knew: GitHub Pages runs Jekyll without it, **Jekyll drops directories beginning with a dot**, so `.well-known/assetlinks.json` would have 404'd and the TWA would have kept its URL bar with no diagnosable cause. It was already emitted for other reasons; it is now guarded for this one.

**A latent build bug, found by an injection failing for the wrong reason.** `build-html.mjs` is **not idempotent**: run it without a fresh `vite build` and the PWA head goes in twice against the one `</head>` — two manifest links and **two service-worker registrations**, racing each other's `controllerchange` (DEV-7). **Nothing shipped was ever affected** — `npm run ship` always rebuilds first, and v10.30–v10.33 were checked and are clean — but it happened in this working tree while iterating on the manifest, which is exactly when a person runs half a build. It refuses loudly now.

**And the comment-strip lesson landed for the fifth and sixth time, in two new syntaxes.** A rule commented out still contains its own text, so `/writeFileSync\(join\('docs', '\.nojekyll'\)/` matched `// writeFileSync(...)`, and `/ship\/\*\.keystore/` matched `# ship/*.keystore`. Both injections walked straight past the assertions written for them. The generator is read through `stripComments` now, and `.gitignore` is parsed into its **active rules** — which is the difference between guarding a signing key and guarding a sentence about one.

**The checklist guards its own claims.** `ship/README.md` tells Evan the keystore is gitignored; when I wrote that it was not true, and the rules were added to make it so. A guard now checks the README's package id against the TWA config, and the ignore rules against the file — because that sentence is exactly the kind that is true when written and false a month later, at the cost of committing a signing key.

**Screenshots are still absent and still not faked** — from the manifest, the listing and this entry. They have to come off the real build on a real device, which is `ART-3`, unblocked since v5.4 and waiting on hardware.

(171 core + 116 balance; **9 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.33 — BAL-13: act 1 is frictionless, and no dial fixes that (2026-08-15)
**No game change. The deliverable is an answer, the measurement that produced it, and a pin so it cannot be lost again.**

**The row had the wrong number for many versions.** "Act 1 kills ~3% of runs against act 3's 34%" says act 1 is not *lethal*, where the complaint is that act 1 cannot *go wrong*. Those are different claims with different fixes, and the death rate cannot tell them apart. A new `acts` diagnostic measures what an act actually costs:

| | act 1 | act 2 |
|---|---|---|
| lowest skin reached | 6.9 ± 1.7 | 5.3 ± 2.4 |
| skin ever ≤3 | **4.3%** | 30.2% |
| psyche ever ≤1 | **0.6%** | 11.3% |
| camps taken (of 3 offered) | **0.4** | 1.4 |

**Act 1 is not merely non-lethal, it is FRICTIONLESS.** You never spend the resources, so no act-1 decision is a decision — camping is not the answer to a problem you never have, which is why five sixths of the camps go untaken. Psyche, the resource the whole game is themed on, is touched on **one run in 160**.

*(The first cut of the diagnostic measured state at the act boundary and read skin 8.9/9 leaving act 1 — which looks like "act 1 costs nothing" but actually says "you can always camp back to full", because the boundary value is post-camp. The trough is the number that answers the complaint, and it is a different number.)*

**FIVE LEVERS, SCREENED AGAINST ALL THREE BARS AT ONCE** — act-1 pressure, the ~44.3 band, and the climber spread. Every previous attempt checked one bar, which is how v9.36 shipped a route-length change that took the spread from 1.4× to 2.9×.

| lever | act-1 pressure | band | spread | lowest climber |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline | 4.3% | 45.1% | 1.46× | 5.7% |
| fewer camps | 3.8% | 45.5% | — | — |
| no between-act top-up | 4.3% | 44.3% | — | — |
| flat 1-skin approach | 28.7% | **51.1%** ↑ | 1.61× | 11.3% |
| …plus one camp fewer | 53.3% | 42.1% | **4.45×** | **4.0%** |
| +1 grip in act 1 | 10.9% | **39.2%** | **3.78×** | **2.3%** |
| one fewer burn *(control)* | 2.2% | 39.9% | 1.78× | **2.7%** |

**Removing recovery does nothing at all** — the cost never accrues, so there is nothing to fail to recover from. **A flat approach cost raised the band by six points**, which is the tell: the greedy policy answers a cost by taking **all three** act-1 camps (3.0 ± 0.0 against 0.4) and trading climbs for camps, coming out ahead. **The slack absorbs the pressure.** Nine stages carrying three camps, two shops, events, a project and an FA means any cost you add just moves the player onto a node they were skipping.

**Take the slack away and act 1 finally bites — at spread 4.45× with a climber on 4.0%.** And that is the result: **three independent mechanisms — skin, grip, attempts — each put a climber under the 5% floor the moment act 1 has teeth.** A fourth, weather, is differential **by construction** and needs no run to rule out: `archOf(s).ignoreWeather` means one archetype ignores it outright.

**The reason is structural, and it is the thing to read before trying a sixth.** Act 1 is the only stretch of the game where every climber is at **full resources**. That is exactly why it is frictionless, and exactly why pressure there separates the roster hardest — the differences compound across nine stages with nothing yet spent to recover from. **A dial cannot fix this.** Changing act 1's *shape* might, and that is a design decision rather than a tuning pass, so it is Evan's.

Act 1 being soft is now a **measured, deliberate property** rather than an oversight, and it is pinned like the band is — in both directions, so a future ticket that accidentally gives act 1 teeth fires the guard and lands on this evidence instead of rediscovering it. That evidence cost about forty thousand simulated runs.

(170 core + 116 balance; **4 injections kept and negative-tested**, one of them in the slow ledger. `src/engine.ts` and `src/App.tsx` are byte-identical to v10.32 — the screening harness patched and restored them, and that was verified rather than assumed.)

### v10.32 — ART-4: the day comes out as a picture (2026-08-15)
**SOCIAL-1 gave the daily a result you can paste anywhere, and what it gave was text. An image is what people actually post** — and the grid was already a picture, it just had no way out of the app. This is the last item on the sixth set.

**The delivery was the hard half, and the row said so.** `<a download>` is the wrong answer for the device this game is played on: iOS has never handled downloading a blob from a web app properly, and inside an installed PWA there is nowhere for the file to land that the player can see. **So there is no download link.** Two native routes instead:

- **`navigator.share` with the file** — Web Share Level 2, the real share sheet, which is the actual "post it" the ticket is asking for. Behind `canShare({files})`, because a browser can have `share()` and refuse files.
- **and where that is missing, the picture itself**, on screen, to press and hold. That is how you save an image on a phone. No download, no permission, nothing for iOS to drop.

**The picture and the paste are one result rendered twice, so they read off one projection.** `shareCard` is new and both use it (ENG-19); a guard asserts the grid the image draws is the same grid `dailyShare` prints, across a fall, a partial and a top-out. The canvas draws **boxes rather than the ▪/▫ characters**, because a glyph that renders as tofu is a broken *image* rather than a broken line — so the count is what has to match, and that is what is checked.

**The card height follows its content.** Fixed at 1350 it left a slab of dead paper under a two-objective day, which reads as an unfinished layout rather than as space. The palette and the font are read off the live stylesheet rather than copied, for the same reason the numbers are.

**THE BUG A BROWSER FOUND, and only a browser could have.** The fallback sheet went into the **climb screen's tree**, which returns earlier than the one its button is on — so the picture was drawn, correctly, 1080 wide and 142 KB, and **had nowhere to appear**. Every guard passed, because every guard was reading the right source in the wrong component. Fourth ticket running that the render caught what the code did not.

**A modal that cannot be escaped is worse than one that never claimed to be.** The new sheet said `aria-modal="true"` and was not wired into A11Y-8's focus/Escape effect, which tells a screen reader the page is inert while stranding a keyboard user behind it. It is in the effect now — and **A11Y-8's guard counted `2`**, the number of sheets that existed when it was written, so a third *correct* sheet failed it while a third *broken* one would have passed. It counts against the sheets themselves now, and additionally asserts focus and Escape reach every one.

**The comment-stripping rule bit for the FIFTH time, and this time the stripper was the fault.** Three guards rolled their own as a per-LINE filter on lines that *start* with a comment marker — and this project writes block comments whose continuation lines are bare indented prose, so the sentence *"`<a download>` is the wrong answer"* sailed through and failed the assertion forbidding a download link. **`stripComments` lives in `sim/guard.mjs` now**, strips blocks as blocks, and replaced all three hand-rolled copies. An injection also showed the grid guard could be evaded by writing `'\u25aa'.repeat` instead of the literal mark; it matches any `.repeat(` now.

`tsc` caught a temporal dead zone on the way — the sheet state read by the A11Y-8 effect was declared below it. That is SAVE-6's shape, and the reason the state is declared with the other two sheets rather than beside the drawing code.

**Verified in a browser** at 402×874: the button sits with the other two shares, the picture draws, focus lands in the sheet, Escape closes it, and it reopens with a fresh image, so revoking the last URL does not break the next one. **Not render-verified: the partial grid** — a day with points but no top-out is not reachable by a crude driver on the route the real date rolls, so the empty-box branch is covered by the projection guards and an injection rather than by a screenshot.

Off-band by construction: a share is a projection of a result already decided, and the guard asserts it never touches the rng.

(169 core + 115 balance; **11 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.31 — DEV-7: an update never takes the player off the screen they are on (2026-08-15)
**Reported from play: "I am on the main menu, I tap to enter and get in and then it randomly goes back to the previous menu."** It did, and it was the update path.

**The chain, all three links already documented and none of them wrong on their own.** DEV-2 reloads an open page when a new service worker takes over, because `skipWaiting()` + `clients.claim()` do **not** reload, so an installed PWA that is rarely cold-started would run the old build for days. It gates that reload on `__SB_BUSY__`, which meant **"mid-climb"** — the two phases the save skips, because a reload there throws the boulder away. And `loadGame` restores the save but **not `phase`**, deliberately: SAVE-4 exists precisely so a reload cannot reopen the post, the van and the daily.

Put together: **a reload from any screen except a climb was permitted, and every one of them lands the player back on the splash.** Mid-climb it costs a boulder — guarded. Three levels into the books it costs your place, and it arrives unrequested a couple of seconds after launch, which is exactly what "randomly" describes.

**Reproduced before touching anything**, with two builds over HTTP: install, ship a new `sw.js`, reopen, tap into THE BOOKS — and a navigation fires and the splash is back. `controllerchange` was measured at **about 1.7 seconds after load**.

**That 1.7 seconds is the fix.** The flag now means **"the player has tapped begin"**, so the only reload that can ever happen is one nobody can see: the splash is up, the page reloads, the splash is still up. It keeps DEV-2's entire point — the build lands in the session that downloaded it, no relaunch needed — because a second and a bit is the *normal* case for still being on the splash, not a lucky one. Mid-climb is covered a fortiori: you cannot be climbing before you have begun.

**It also retires the poll.** The old flag went true and false as the phase changed, so waiting four seconds and asking again made sense. `booted` is one-way — nothing un-taps the splash — so if it is busy at `controllerchange` it is busy for the rest of the session, and an interval that can never fire is just an interval running for ever. One check now, and the build lands on the next launch's splash instead.

**Verified against the reproduction, both ways round:** tap in immediately and race the worker, or wait until it has already taken over. **No navigation after the tap in either case**, and `__SB_BUSY__` reads `true` the moment the player taps in.

**Guarded against the tempting wrong fix.** The obvious alternative is to make the loader restore `phase` so a reload puts you back — that is the SAVE-4 hole, and there is an assertion and an injection that fail if anyone reopens it. The answer was to stop reloading at the wrong moment, not to start restoring a screen the save was never meant to reopen.

DEV-2's own assertion moved rather than relaxed: it pinned "the flag is mid-climb", which is the thing that was wrong, and now pins the splash gate **plus** a negative on the old meaning. GUARD-9's patch-path check was widened to `scripts/`, which is source by every measure that matters here — DEV-2's guard already reads `build-html.mjs` as the source of truth for the worker.

(168 core + 115 balance; **6 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.30 — A11Y-9: a screen reader hears the turn, not the last line of it (2026-08-15)
**The row said "the newest blocks are static divs — consistency, not a break." Reading the screens found something worse, narrower, and definitely a break.** The climb screen has had a screen-reader live region since A11Y-2, and it announced **one line**:

```
<div className="vis-hidden" role="status" aria-live="polite">
  {st.log.length ? st.log[st.log.length - 1] : ''}</div>
```

**A turn writes a mean of 4.8 log lines.** Measured over 297 turns of real play: one line on 2% of turns, four to six on two thirds of them, nine on one. So a screen-reader player was getting **about a fifth of the game** — and not a chosen fifth. The last line is whatever the engine appended last, which is routinely the route's move for **next** turn, or a phase banner, rather than the outcome of the move you just made. The visible log renders `slice(-3)`, so a sighted player was two lines a turn ahead of a blind one.

**What goes out now is every line added since the last render** — the account of what just happened. No three-line cap on it: that cap is a small screen's space constraint, not a judgement about what matters. Verified in a browser against the visible log side by side; the gap it closes is stark. One real turn announced *"Open Hand works the sloper. **The Mirage — you read the next 1 hold off it.** Open Hand blows. ▸ Something is flaking off the feet hold. Hanging. +1 pump."* where the screen showed the last three and the old region would have read only *"Hanging. +1 pump."* — the signature read, which is a thing you paid for, was inaudible.

**The rule the ticket settled on, instead of "make every static div announce":** a live region is for something that appears **while you are already on the screen**. Three qualified and said nothing — the sequence banner, the phase-approaching banner, and NARR-15's curse block, which arrives when you arm TAKE TWO. Nineteen other `.spot` blocks are present at mount, where a live region is noise rather than help, and they were left alone.

**The phase banner is the deliberate exception.** The phase writes a log line, so the region above already reads it — announcing the banner too would say it twice. Its **partner clause** is the one thing there that is *not* in the log and cannot be, because NARR-14 keeps partners out of `resolve` (v10.29). So the clause announces and the banner does not, and both halves are guarded.

**Two windows in my own new guard were wrong, and the injections found both** — the same family as the last two versions: the sequence window closed on `{(() => {`, two banners further down, so it **swallowed the phase banner and passed on a live region belonging to that**; and the phase-banner anchor contained the very attribute list under test, so adding a `role` moved the anchor and GUARD-8 reported a broken window instead of the assertion reporting the real fault. An anchor must not contain the thing it is testing.

**Left unresolved on purpose, and stated rather than quietly settled:** the project already puts `role="status"` on three blocks that are present at mount (SKIRM-7's circuit partner, and two on `sessionEnd`). Under the rule above those are noise; the counter-argument is that screen readers announce little on a view swap. That is genuinely arguable, it is not what this ticket was about, and changing it would be removing announcements rather than adding them.

(167 core + 115 balance; **9 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.29 — NARR-15: the moments they were standing through in silence (2026-08-15)
**The backlog row said "the partner speaks at four moments" and was two tickets stale — there were ten.** What was true is that the game had *built* five more moments and walked somebody through all of them without a word. Two of the five sting:

- **WALKED.** SKIRM-7 gave you somebody who spends the entire circuit saying *go again* or *that is enough*. SKIRM-8 gave walking off its own screen. **Nobody joined the two up** — they pushed you there and then said nothing when you arrived.
- **CLAIM.** The first-ascent screen's own words are *"nothing to compare it to and **nobody to ask**"* — and there is somebody standing right there. Having an opinion about what you just did **is** the partner mechanic.
- **CURSE** — taking one on purpose is a decision, on a screen that offers it in as many words. **PHASE** — a route changing under you is the loudest thing that happens mid-climb.

**And a fifth the row did not list, found by reading the screens instead of the row: the Circuit has TWO endings and both were silent.** Walking off goes to `sessionEnd`; running out of skin goes to `runEnd`, whose partner block was gated `!st.circuit`. That gate was **right** — `top` and `died` are trip lines, pads carried down, *next time you go first* — but it left the mode's **ordinary** ending, the one most sessions get, with nobody in it at all. It has `spent` now.

**NARR-14's guard refused my first draft, and it was right to.** The `phase` line was written into `resolve`'s log, and the rule is that **resolution never reads the partner** — because a partner the resolution can read is a partner somebody can later make matter. Not a guard that predated its own answer (the ROUTE-15 case); a deliberate architectural rule. The line lives on the climb screen's phase banner instead, which was already there and is pure render.

**Two older guards pinned an expression where they meant a property**, and both are now stronger rather than merely widened: NARR-14's read the whole `partnerSays` call, so adding a *third* outcome to that screen broke it with nothing wrong — it pins `won ? 'top' : 'died'`, which is all it was ever claiming. SKIRM-7's pinned the **gate** `!st.circuit`, the only way to say "not a death line" while the circuit had nothing of its own to say; it now reads which moment the circuit branch actually chooses, which the old test could not have told you.

**THE RENDER CAUGHT TWO THINGS, AND THEY WERE THE SAME THING TWICE.** The walk-off block shipped **rendering nothing at all**: my gate was carefully written not to read `circuit` — which SKIRM-8 clears on the way out — and then called `partnerFor`, **which read it for us**. Teaching `partnerFor` about `result: 'walked'` fixed it and immediately **woke a block that had been silently dead on that screen**: the send/fall line, which then said *"Feet." One word, and she is right* on the one ending where nothing fell. That is the third ticket running where a browser found what reading the code did not, and the second where the find was a per-route line on the walk-off (SKIRM-8 caught two others).

**A hole in a guard that predates this ticket, found by an injection for a different one.** `ok(/spec\.tutorial && tip/...)` is a **substring** of `{false && spec.tutorial && tip`, so switching the tutorial banner off entirely left it green. Two of my own new assertions had it too. **Anchor a JSX gate on its opening brace** — the same shape as ROUTE-15's tagger regex one version ago, so it is now written down twice.

**Two more assertions of my own were vacuous and the injections found both:** an unanchored gate (above), and `/skirmish: route, inRun: false/` tested anywhere in the file — the FA, the daily and the challenge all contain it, so removing the one it was about left it matching one of the other two. It counts all three now, and covers modes it was not even claiming to.

Still text and still out of resolution: the partner table may not grow a mechanical field, `resolve`/`autoPlay`/`startBurn` may not name them, and the FA grade is asserted to stay yours. Which FA matters and is now asserted: the map node keeps the trip's state so somebody is with you, while the menu FA sets `inRun: false` because you drove out alone.

**Verified in a browser** at 402×874 — `walked`, `curse` and `spent` all read correctly with no overflow. **Not driven: `claim` and `phase`**, which sit behind most of a campaign (a trip FA needs a map node and a send; only four routes have phases and all are act 2–3). Both carry a source guard, a behavioural guard on what `partnerFor`/`partnerSays` return for that state, and an injection.

**Found and NOT fixed, logged rather than bundled:** the CIRCUIT OVER screen's subtitle reads *"Act 1 · the forest · stage 1 · deck 15"* — trip furniture on a screen that is not a trip, the same class SKIRM-8 named twice on the walk-off. Out of scope for a ticket about who speaks.

(166 core + 115 balance; **17 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.28 — ROUTE-15: the first lines in the book have a feature too (2026-08-14)
**ROUTE-5 added named features to kill "a route is a stat line", and it left the four lines you meet FIRST without one.** 31 of 37 named; the six bare ones were the four earliest (Warm-Up Rail, The Sit Start, Mossback, Peeler), the tutorial and the finale. The sameness ROUTE-5 set out to kill was concentrated exactly where a new player is deciding whether this game has any texture in it.

**They are TAGGED, not placed, and that distinction is the whole ticket.** `signature` runs `placeSig`, which *rebuilds* the hold from the feature's base type and applies its stats — band-active, and not something to do to the four routes a beginner learns on. ROUTE-12's generated path instead writes `{ ...hold, sig: id }` onto a hold that is already there, from an RNG keyed to the **route** rather than the run: no number moves and no run entropy is consumed. `sigTag` extends that to scripted lines. **Measured: 44.4% against the ~44.3 pin.**

**What they DO is ROUTE-12's answer, and three existing guards are what made me find it.** Written stat-less, the four tripped *"is just its base hold with a name on it"* and *"still does nothing but sit there"*. The project has a stated, guarded rule — **a signature must do something** — and ROUTE-12 had already answered what: it pays a one-time **`read`** when first worked. Information, which is free, because resolution never consults `readAhead`. On the four earliest lines that is the read mechanic teaching itself at the point where you own the fewest cards that can do it. The older guard's `dGrip || dBite || ability` **predates** that answer, so it was widened to accept `read` — and an injection proves the widening is not a weakening: removing a `read` still fires it.

**And the widened guard got a converse that is the part which can actually go wrong:** a tagged feature may not carry stats, because the tagger does not rebuild the hold — a `dGrip` there would be a number that silently does nothing and a reader of the table would believe it.

**THE BUG THIS TICKET SHIPPED INTO ITS OWN WORKING TREE.** The first cut of the tagger ran "which feature" and "whether to tag" through one expression, so a generated line got `tagged = null`, and `null || undefined` is falsy — **every grind line silently lost its feature**. My own 60-seed probe of the four scripted routes passed the whole time, because it never looked at the circuit. **ROUTE-12's guard caught it**, which is the argument for keeping old guards that seem redundant. It is kept as the first injection.

**Two more holes the injections found in guards I had just written:**
- the band-neutrality assertion wanted to see *one* spread anywhere in the tagger, and the tagger writes to the hold list **twice** — rebuilding the first site left it green. It counts the writes and checks every one now;
- `local` and "not in the generated pool" looked like the same assertion. They are not — one guards the flag, the other the derivation — and there is now an injection for each.

**The render caught what the code did not, again.** The Sit-Down's note read *"You start from the floor, on your backside"* — but the holds are **shuffled**, so a positional note lands four moves up. The other three were position-free by luck rather than rule; it is a rule now, guarded and injected. The line's own name carries where it is; the feature only has to be a hold on it.

**Two carve-outs, both from content, neither for convenience.** The **tutorial** is authored hold by hold (`spec.holds`) to teach one thing at a time, and a named feature with a note to read is a second thing. **The Lost Line**'s own note is *"No chalk. No tick marks. No trail. Exactly as he left it."* — a named feature means people have been there and talked about it, which is the one thing that route is about not being. That is almost certainly why ROUTE-13 left it out, and if it ever gets one the name should come out of **his pages**, which the player may or may not have read.

The four are marked **`local`**: their notes are about one specific place, so unlike the rest of the pool they are never tagged onto a generated circuit line. That also leaves `GEN_SIG_IDS` byte-identical, so the circuit names the same features it did before.

**Verified in a browser** at 402×874: both act-1 features render in the guidebook on the first screen a new player sees, no clipping, no overflow.

(165 core + 115 balance; **13 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.27 — SAVE-6: a save cannot claim an array longer than the game can make (2026-08-14)
**SAVE-5 was one unvalidated NUMBER** — `xp: undefined` beat freshRun's 0, `gainXp` returned NaN, `while (NaN >= xpToNext(level))` was never true and levelling froze for ever — and it was reachable from a one-field import code, not only a future version bump. **The arrays were the same hole:** `owned`, `history`, `journal`, `established`, `trail` and a dozen more were taken at whatever length the file claimed. The audit's example was harmless (500 `seasonWeeks`, of which the board reads four); the ones that are not are **`history` and `owned`, which are rendered in full** — and everything accepted is written straight back out, so the file grows.

Every array in `loadGame` goes through **one clamp** now, and the caps are **derived** wherever the game already knows the number — you cannot own more cards than exist, or tick more acts than there are — so they cannot drift from the thing they bound. A guard scans the loader for any array still taken at the file's word, so a new field cannot be missed.

Two caps existed only where the **writer** could see them and are shared now (ENG-19): `established` was a bare `.slice(0, 40)` inside `claimStep`, and the larder had none at all.

**That larder is the find beyond the audit's claim.** It is the only array in the save that grows without a ceiling **in normal play**: `bankDaily` appends a kit item every time the streak ladder pays one, and the only thing that takes any out is starting a trip. Keep a habit up for months without tying in and it grows for ever. Capped where it **grows** as well as where it loads, or the file bloats regardless of the loader.

**And I reproduced SAVE-5's exact failure mode inside the fix for SAVE-5's shape.** `LARDER_MAX` was first written as `KIT_MAX * 6` two thousand lines above `KIT_MAX`; esbuild hoists the declaration rather than throwing, so it evaluated to **NaN** — and `slice(-NaN)` returns the whole array, so the cap silently did nothing. `tsc` would have caught it had I run it between the two edits, and it *did* catch the same class one edit later. Every cap is asserted to be a finite positive number now, and there is an injection that makes one NaN.

**Three fixture and guard faults found by the injections, all the same family:**
- the crafted-save fixture was 100 entries against a largest cap of **248**, so removing the `owned` clamp was indistinguishable from keeping it;
- a negative assertion about source matched `slice(0, 40)` **in its own comment** explaining what it replaced — fourth time that correction has been needed, so the rule is now written down: *a negative source assertion reads code, not prose*;
- **GUARD-9's own guard let a two-element patch through**, which destructures to `file=<the needle>` and made the runner try to open it as a filename. Patch shape is asserted now.

Off-band by construction: the harness calls `newRun` and never loads a file. (164 core + 115 balance; **9 injections kept and negative-tested**.)

### v10.26 — SKIRM-8: the good outcome gets a screen (2026-08-14)
**RUN-13 added the circuit walk-off to history because nothing in the game acknowledged the one outcome this mode has that is not a failure. SKIRM-7 gave you somebody standing there who nudges you toward it. And pressing the button set `phase: 'menu'`** — so the good ending dropped you on the guidebook with no screen at all, while every fall got a full account. Found while wiring SKIRM-7 and logged rather than bundled.

It ends on **`sessionEnd`** now, which already carried a WALKED AWAY heading for a bailed skirmish and needed only the circuit's own numbers adding: how many lines, which zone, whether it beat your best, and a **`circuitShare`** in the same no-server shape as the daily and the week.

**`result: 'walked'` is the marker**, and a distinct result from `'bail'` — a bail is giving up on one boulder, this is the good ending. It is `result` rather than a flag because every run start sets `result: null`, so it cannot leak into the next attempt the way a mode flag could (MODE-1). `circuit` and `skirmish` are still cleared on the way out for exactly that reason; `circuitScore` is not a mode flag, it is the score, and the screen reads it — with `result` deciding whether this ending was a circuit **at all**, rather than a stale score being taken as one.

RUN-13's guard pinned `phase: 'menu'`, which is the thing this ticket found wrong. It asserts the new exit and two more properties, so the reason it existed still holds.

**Two things the RENDER caught that reading the code did not:**
- The route header would have named **a campaign route you were never on** — `skirmish` is cleared, so `specOf` falls back to `ROUTES[routeIdx]`. Gated.
- Worse, and only visible on screen: **"Burns used 3/3. Got 6 of 5"** — one line's burns against a hold count already passed. Every per-route number on that screen is behind the same gate now, and guarded.

I also **cut two assertions of my own that could not fail**: `result !== 'bail'` and `result !== 'send'` are both implied by the `eq(result, 'walked')` above them, so neither can ever be the thing that fires. The intent is recorded as prose instead. That is the fourth vacuous-assertion class this month and precisely why GUARD-9 exists.

Off-band by construction: the Circuit is not measured at all — `sim/run.mjs` does not contain the word, asserted since SKIRM-7. **Verified in a browser** by driving the real build into the Circuit, sending a line and walking off: heading, score, zone, best and share all read correctly, nothing clipped, no horizontal overflow at 402×874.

(163 core + 115 balance; **10 injections kept and negative-tested** — one of which showed an assertion matching the wrong one of two gates on the same screen.)

### v10.25 — BAL-16: a floor nobody can measure is not a floor (2026-08-14)
**The climber-spread guard says no climber completes less than 5% of campaigns, and it was passing on a coin flip.** The Comp Kid read **5.2%** at the n=600 that guard runs, against a real **5.8%** — the minimum of five estimates whose SE is ~0.9 reads about an SE low. And 5.8% clears a floor of 5 by **1.7 SE**, which no sample this project can afford turns into a claim (2 SE wants n>1900 on one climber; 3 SE about 3400).

**An unmeasurable property is a different problem from a false one**, and the fix was neither the floor nor a shrug.

**The guard** resolves the floor where the floor lives: a coarse n=600 pass for the spread ratio, then a **fine n=2000 pass on the two lowest** climbers through a new `ARCH_ONLY`. That costs 4,000 runs instead of the 12,500 fining all five would — the other three cannot hold the floor and there is no reason to pay for them (GUARD-6). And it **reports its margin in standard errors**, so "this guard cannot resolve this" is a loud failure rather than a flake. Cost ~+4,000 runs on the ledger, stated because GUARD-6 fought for that budget.

**The climber** was bought back to **6.6%, 3.2 SE clear**, on the axis it actually died of: psyche, **19% of its runs against 11–14% for everyone else**, because `dAttempts: -1` fails more boulders and `PSYCHE_MAX` is 3. Every existing dial was the wrong **size**, measured one at a time at n=2500:

| dial | Comp Kid completion |
|---|---|
| baseline | 5.8% |
| one skin | 4.9% — *nothing*, it just fails more boulders and dies of psyche anyway |
| one betaGrip | 5.8% — nothing at all |
| **one `dPsyche`** | **6.6%** ✅ |
| one Contact | 10.2% |
| one card of hand | 15.6% |

That last pair is worth remembering: **this climber is card-starved, not strength-starved**, and a single point of Contact or one card of hand swings it four to ten points. So it gets a dial of its own — and a characterful one: a comp kid does not lose heart when a boulder beats them, they go again. Its psyche deaths went **19% → 1%**, so its constraint is attempts and route now rather than morale, which reads truer to "no patience" than a fragile head did.

**The floor did not move.** BAL-9 exists to forbid that, and its own note records the floor being lowered to 4 once *"to accommodate a drift rather than to fix it"*. `psycheMax` is one function rather than twelve copies of the constant (ENG-19), so a climber who carries more heart carries it at the start of a trip, off a send and out of a camp alike.

**Verified long-standing rather than recent drift:** v10.19 — before CARD-18 and before the SIM-8 re-pin — reads the same climber at **5.7%** (n=1500). `arch` mode measures each climber's *own* loadout, so no re-pin can reach it, which also confirms what v10.23 asserted about it instead of leaving it asserted.

Roster after, n=1500: **Boulderer 8.9 · Comp Kid 6.7 · Trad Dad 6.7 · Alpinist 7.7 · Onsighter 7.3** — spread 1.33× against a 2.2× ceiling. The campaign band is untouched at ~44.3.

**Noted for next time:** the whole roster now lives between 6 and 9%, and the floor of 5 was set when it spanned **3.3 to 29.8**. It is a much tighter fit than it was, and the next climber to drift will hit the same unmeasurable margin. That wants a dated decision about the floor eventually.

(162 core + 115 balance; **7 injections kept in `sim/mutants.mjs` and negative-tested** — one of which found this guard matching the word `ARCH_ONLY` in its own prose rather than in the command, the third time that correction has been needed.)

### v10.24 — GUARD-9: the negative tests, kept (2026-08-13)
**`test.mjs` opens by saying that fifteen one-off harnesses were written across this project, each proving something real, and every one of them was thrown away — and that this is why the kept suite exists.** The injections were the same story one level up. Every ticket here gets negative-tested, they have found the original bug every time, and every one of those scripts lived in `/tmp`.

`sim/mutants.mjs` keeps them: **52 injections** across GUARD-8, CARD-18, SKIRM-7, SIM-7, SIM-8 and GUARD-9 itself. Each breaks a specific thing a guard claims and **names the failure it expects to hear** — a mutant that merely makes *something* fail proves nothing about the guard you meant to test. `npm run mutants` · `npm run mutants:slow` · `node sim/mutants.mjs SIM-8` for one ticket's.

**The case for keeping them is that they found three assertions that COULD NOT FAIL**, each in a guard written minutes earlier, each reading green:

- **SKIRM-7** asserted `partnerFor(st)` appeared in the screen. It does — into a local — even when the block rendering it is gated off.
- **SIM-7** asserted a send rate that cannot see the bug *and points the other way*: the illegal deck sends 99% of one boulder and completes 18.9% of campaigns.
- **SIM-8** asserted beta cards were absent from a list the **test** had just built, rather than from the one the harness uses.

GUARD-8 made a fragile source *window* impossible. This is the other shape — **an assertion that tests the fixture instead of the claim** — and nothing static catches it. Running it does.

The runner edits source files and puts them back: snapshot first, restore after each mutant, restore on crash or Ctrl-C, and a **byte-compare at exit** that says so loudly rather than leaving a mutated tree.

**GUARD-9's own guard runs on every `npm run check`** and covers the half that rots silently: every mutant's **anchor** must still match its file exactly once, because the moment somebody edits that line the injection quietly stops testing anything. The other rot — a `catches` message drifting from what the guard prints — **cannot be checked cheaply, and the header says why rather than pretending**: most of those messages are template literals that only exist once a failing run has built them. That half is caught by running it, and it caught three while this was being written.

**Three self-references had to be handled, and are commented rather than left clever:** an injection that patches `mutants.mjs` quotes the line it targets, which puts a second copy in the file and kills its own anchor (split strings); the anchor check has to run **last** or it masks the assertion every such injection was written to test (found by running them); and the interrupt injection could not fail while its own `catches` still spelled the signal name.

**Not gated on `ship`, deliberately — and this cuts against GUARD-6, so it is stated rather than glossed:** the fast set is ~9 minutes against ship's ~5, and what it protects is *guard authoring* rather than release regression. Source does not drift into vacuity between releases; somebody edits a guard. The half that does rot on its own runs on every check.

(161 core + 115 balance; **52/52 injections caught**, including GUARD-9's five against itself.)

### v10.23 — SIM-8: the band was pinned on a deck no player can hold (2026-08-13)
**THE PIN IS RE-SET, ~52 → ~44.3** (n=2700, PAGES=14). Deliberate and dated in the CARD-15 sense: *not a drift being accommodated — a wrong reference deck replaced.*

run.mjs had its own deck builder — sort every card by `power * 2 + contact` — and it capped rares and uncommons **but not beta rarity**, while `copyLimit('beta')` is 3. So six of the fifteen measured slots were `Beta · Going Alone` (4/8) and `Beta · Being Frightened` (3/7), cards `buildable()` refuses outright. **Every "the band held" in this ledger since the pin was set leaned on that deck.**

The band now rides **`buildLoadout`** — the engine's builder, the one behind BUILD ME ONE, with the slot rules SIM-7 gave it. One builder (ENG-19), and the number describes a deck a player can actually arrive at. Drift tolerance is **±6 (38–50)**: about 2 SE at the n=300 that guard runs, still tight enough to catch a BAL-14-shaped 10-point slide either way.

**What the honest deck cost, measured: 53.4 → 44.3.** Of that, **+10.5 was the six illegal cards alone** (the same sort restricted to legal cards reads 42.9); the rest is *shape*. The game's build carries **half the raw Power** (20 against 40) and wins on consistency instead — two copies each of Hand Jam, Fist Jam and Arm Bar, three rests, three techniques.

**One guard needed retuning — the same one SIM-6 had to move.** ROUTE-8 says a line you pick is a choice and not a trap: no more than 8 points below the guide. At n=1500 the guide reads 45.1 and the direct at `dCrux: 3` reads 38.1 — **−7.0 against a −8 floor**, margin 1.0 on a 1.8-point difference SE, which is a guard passing on a coin flip. The direct is **`dCrux: 2`** now and reads 43.1, so **−2.0, five points clear of either bound**. That *restores* the documented intent rather than inventing one: the ROUTE-8 note itself says the direct should land *"~2 under the guide"* and that cruxes are *"almost free"* — both were true of a deck with twice the Power, and its own next sentence explains the rest: *"a cruxy line that a powerful deck eats and a weak one walls on"*. The line's prose moved with its number.

**Two things recorded because I walked into them:**
- **`dClear: -1` on the direct reads +1.2 and looks like a fix.** CARD-15 removed exactly that, for exactly this drift — a short line skips endurance. The comment above `LINES` is what stopped me shipping it.
- **At n=500 the direct at `dCrux: 2` read +1.0; at n=1500 it reads −2.0.** The difference SE at 500 is 3.1, so the first number was one SE of nothing. ROUTE-8's own arms run at n=500, which resolves its −8 floor and **not** its +3 ceiling.

**Nothing else moved.** CARD-9's lift reads **+1.8** against its 0–4 window, comfortable at both ends. The climber spread is untouched **by construction** — `arch` mode sets `LOADOUT = undefined` and measures each climber's own loadout, so this re-pin cannot reach it; the Comp Kid's **5.2% against a floor of 5** is a pre-existing graze worth somebody looking at, not this ticket's doing. The acts curve and the journal guard hold.

(160 core + 115 balance; **8 injections negative-tested** — one of which found the **third vacuous assertion of the session** in my own new guard: it rebuilt the harness's owned-card list locally instead of reading it, so widening run.mjs's filter back to "anything but a curse" left it green.)

### v10.22 — SIM-7: BUILD ME ONE was handing you an illegal deck (2026-08-13)
**`copyLimit` caps copies of one card. `RARE_SLOTS` (1) and `UNCOMMON_SLOTS` (3) cap how many rares and uncommons the WHOLE loadout may hold** — and they exist for a stated reason: *"without this a full collection fields eight distinct rares and every route sends at ~100%"*. The card picker enforces them in three places. **`buildLoadout` — the builder behind BUILD ME ONE — enforced neither.**

With a full collection it fielded **ten rares against a limit of one** and four uncommons against three: a loadout the picker would have refused at every step after the first. Measured over campaigns it completed **18.0% (n=2700) against 28.1% for the starter deck it offers to replace.** The button that says *"not sure where to start? let it pick"* was making you nine points worse. Obeying the rule takes it to **44.3%** — the fix is worth about **+26 points** to anyone who presses it. Reachable exactly as a player hits it: the button is `disabled={full}`, reads **BUILD ME ONE ▸** when empty, and sits next to **EMPTY IT**.

**Off-band by construction.** `buildLoadout` is called from the App and from these guards, never from the measured campaign, which still rides run.mjs's own `buildBest`. `DECK=builder` measures the game's builder on purpose, and the slow ledger now asserts its deck beats the starter deck by a real margin — **the guard that was missing**.

**Why it survived.** The two DECK-1 guards check deck *shape* and never the slot rules, and the one send measurement uses a beginner's collection of starters and commons — **which contains no rares at all**, so the bug could not appear in it.

**Triage, recorded so nobody repeats it.** The structural caps are *not* the cause: `MAX_TECH` 3→1 read 20.8, `MAX_RESTS` 3→2 read 17.9, both 20.0, `MIN_HANDS` 8→9 read 18.9, bonus saturation from the first card 20.2 — all against a baseline of 18.9. Adding `power * 3` to the build read 34.1, which is what pointed at the real cause. Do not tune the caps; the rule was simply missing.

**Two findings about instruments, both of which cost me a wrong assertion first:**

- **A single-climb send rate cannot see this, and points the wrong way.** The rare-stuffed deck sends **99%** of an early boulder against the commons-built deck's 93% — *better* — while completing 18.9% of campaigns against 45%. Which is exactly what `RARE_SLOTS` was written for: one climb rewards raw stats, a campaign is attrition, and fifteen singleton rares draw badly and carry almost no shed. **Both send-rate assertions I first added were vacuous and are removed rather than kept**, with the reason written into the guard.
- **The pinned band itself is measured on an illegal deck.** `buildBest` caps rares and uncommons but not **beta**, and `copyLimit('beta')` is 3 — so the measured deck carries three `Beta · Going Alone` (4/8) and three `Beta · Being Frightened` (3/7), **six of fifteen slots**, in cards `buildable()` refuses outright. Measured, those six are worth **+10.5 points**: the same stat sort restricted to legal cards reads **42.9**. So the honest band for a legal deck is ~43 stat-sorted or ~44 as the game builds it, against a pin of **~52**.

(159 core + 115 balance; **7 injections negative-tested**, including reinstating the original bug and drifting the picker away from the builder.)

### v10.21 — SKIRM-7: the Circuit had nobody in it (2026-08-13)
**The Circuit runs `inRun: false`** — which is correct, it is not a campaign — **and every feature that gated on `inRun` therefore skipped it silently.** By v10.20 it was the least-served mode in the game: no partner (NARR-14), no ambience bed (AUD-2), nothing between lines but a zone name. It is also the **longest single sitting there is**, so it is the mode that most wanted somebody standing there.

**A partner.** `partnerFor` admits `circuit` as well as `inRun` — widened **by exactly one mode, asserted**, so a daily or a project still gets nobody. The Circuit's `runSeed` is saved and restored with the rest of it (DEV-3), which is the only reason resuming one keeps the same person instead of handing you a stranger.

**An opinion, about the one decision this mode has.** Rather than reuse the campaign's moments, partners get two new lines — `again` and `enough` — and a **threshold**: the circuit score from which *this* person would rather you walked off. Kit never wants to stop (14); Moss wants to stop almost immediately (3). A fixed per-partner push would be static, and deriving it from the zone would make it the game's opinion rather than theirs — a threshold is both, and where it falls is who they are. **RUN-13 established that walking off is the GOOD circuit outcome and that nothing in the game ever said so;** somebody saying it is the cheapest honest way to.

**A place.** `bedFor` gives the Circuit a bed keyed to **its own zones** rather than an act it does not have, so the sound says how deep you are — and it covers the between-lines screen, which is the Circuit's map. Keyed off `circuitZone(n).floor` rather than a second copy of the ladder (ENG-19), and guarded so **adding a zone fails loudly** instead of sounding like the warm-up for ever. Into the Dark shares alpine with The Business on purpose: there is no fourth synthesised bed, and inventing one is oscillator work AUD-2 deliberately left outside the tested part of the game.

**Scope carve-out, on the record.** The audit item also listed "no season or daily hook" and that is **deliberately not done**: the Circuit is *endless*, so feeding its score into a bounded four-week season turns the season into a farmable grind test when it is meant to measure a habit. The carve-out is **asserted**, so reversing it is a decision rather than a drift.

**NARR-14's guard had to be widened, and that is the part worth watching.** It forbids any partner field outside a fixed allowlist — a partner is TEXT and nothing else — and `enough` is a number. Widening an allowlist to admit your own field is exactly how a guard gets quietly relaxed, so it ships with the assertions that make it safe: **every value `partnerPush` can return must be a key of `says`** (a field that selected anything but text would fail that), and `partnerPush` joins the API that `resolve`, `powerAgainst`, `biteAgainst`, `startBurn` and `autoPlay` may not read.

Off-band by construction and absolutely: `sim/run.mjs` does not contain the word "circuit", and that is asserted. **Verified in a browser** by driving the real build to the between-lines screen and rendering *both* branches (forcing every threshold to 0 for the second): both read correctly, nothing clipped, no horizontal overflow at 402×874. Also found on the way: `walkAwayStep` goes straight to the menu, so the good outcome still has no screen of its own — logged rather than bundled.

**13 injections negative-tested, and one of them found a VACUOUS assertion in this ticket's own guard** — it checked that `partnerFor` was *called*, which stays true when the render is gated off. That is the GUARD-8 class, in the guard I had just written, and the negative test is the only reason I know. (159 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.20 — CARD-18: four mechanics you would never meet twice (2026-08-13)
**`skinCost`, `read`, `latch` and `restChip` were each carried by exactly one card of 244.** That is not a small pool — it is a mechanic the game *has* and a player will almost certainly never see, which is CARD-12's pattern and CARD-17's failure mode. Each has a sibling now, and each sibling is a different card rather than a reskin:

- **`Take It All In`** reads twice as far as Sight the Line and hands you nothing back — off the clock instead of off a card.
- **`Rand Smear`** latches, and on a **foot** that is a different card from Iron Fingers: what it saves is the **Support**, which ENG-32 made the thing you are paying for.
- **`Milk The Jug`** rests more and works the hold less — the other end of Chalk the Hold's trade.
- **`Last Of The Skin`** buys the **clock** with skin, where the one existing skin card bought Power with it at rare.

Plus keyword entries for the two mechanics the game never named: **Reading ahead** and **Skin**.

**Two of the four shipped as strict upgrades in the first cut**, and both were caught before any measurement: Milk The Jug was **Shake Out with a free chip** and Rand Smear was **Precise Feet's exact stat line with a free latch** — which is the thing CARD-11's own guard forbids for its own card. They pay for their keyword now: the chip costs the anchor, the latch costs a point of Contact. A *general* dominance scan was tried and abandoned — it reports **2,759 pairs** across the table, because a higher rarity is supposed to dominate and two different `fx` values are not comparable — so the guard asserts the specific trade each card was measured against.

**`restChip` had no term in `cardValue` at all**, so for eleven versions the drafter scored a chipping rest on its shed alone and **Chalk the Hold read below the take-it line**. That is ENG-25's blind spot, third instance. Priced against a keyword already calibrated here rather than guessed: the per-card probe reads restChip at **+3.0pt per point** (exactly linear at 1 and 2) against `anchor`'s **+2.6pt**, and anchor is worth 3 — so restChip is worth 3 a point. **`read` deliberately gets no term:** the policy never consults `readAhead`, so valuing it would make the drafter buy what the sim cannot spend, which is ENG-25 run backwards. That is also why `read` counts as *situational* in the dead-card guard — and the guard now asserts the fact it rests on rather than taking it on trust.

**Band: 52.7 → 53.4 at n=2700, and the move is RESHUFFLE, not difficulty.** Measured in three arms on the same seeds rather than argued: **244 cards 52.7 · 248 with the keywords stripped 53.6 · 248 shipped 53.4** — so the pool change alone is **+0.9** and the four mechanics are **−0.2**, which is nothing. This reproduces CARD-11's v9.74 finding, and **the mechanism is named this time:** three *event* outcomes draw a card off `BY_RARITY(rarity)` — the whole table, not REWARDS — so `rng.int(73)` became `rng.int(77)` and every run downstream of an event diverges. The **~52 pin holds**, no re-pin, whole ledger green. REWARDS is untouched on purpose (CARD-15's dated carve-out) and that is now **asserted**, so a later change there gets measured instead of slipping through.

Harness: `CARDS_ONLY` filters the per-card probe, which was unrunnable at over nine minutes for the whole table and so had never been run. (158 core + 114 balance; **11 injections negative-tested**, including reinstating both strict upgrades and both original dead-field bugs.)

### v10.19 — GUARD-8: the guard suite reads itself (2026-08-13)
**The seventh audit's other P1, and the discipline turned on itself for the second time.** Half of this suite works by reading a window of source and asserting what is and is not inside it. That is a legitimate technique — it is how you assert "the screen does not reimplement this rule" or "this function grew a pump term" — but the WINDOW is where those guards rot, and they rot **silently**: a guard reading the wrong text still reads green. GUARD-3 fixed the instances it found and did nothing to stop the next ones, and three more were written during the very next ticket. So this one stops fixing instances.

There is now **one way to take a source window** — `sim/guard.mjs`'s `region()`, with `declBody`/`appFn`/`cssRule`/`tail` on top of it — and it validates itself: the opening anchor must exist, a closing bound must exist (otherwise it would quietly read to the end of the file), and the result must clear a size floor. **37 hand-rolled windows** across both suite files were converted, and the **22 manual `.length > N` "this moved out from under the guard" assertions** that were the previous answer are now the helper's job rather than each guard's.

`ok` and `eq` moved into the same module — shared, not copied (ENG-19) — and **both now refuse an assertion with no failure message.** That is enforced at CALL time rather than by scanning source, which is what makes it exact, and it produced a correction to the audit that opened this ticket: **its "2 assertions with no failure message" were both false positives.** A line-based scan reads `ok(E.claimCurse({ … })` as a complete call when it is the first line of a two-line one, and a regex literal containing a quote is enough to fool the comma counting. Every assertion in both suites executes, so with the runtime check in place, 157 + 105 green is proof there are none.

And the part that makes the class not come back: **`guardScan` reads all three suite files** for the three shapes — a hard-coded window length, a window taken straight off an `indexOf`, and the two-line form where a variable set from an `indexOf` is sliced later. A line that genuinely needs a raw slice (mutating a code string, a deliberate proximity lookahead) carries a `guard-8: allow` marker **and a reason**, so it is a decision somebody wrote down rather than a habit.

**Two windows were under-asking, and the repair found both — not review.** The BACK-parent census read a fixed 4,000 characters and was **silently leaving the `deck` screen out of the census entirely** (it has a parent, so no live bug — but the guard was not asking). And the persist effect's four negative assertions read a fixed **220** characters, which is the audit's own vacuous shape, in a guard the audit did not flag. Both bound at the real edge now.

**Negative-tested eight ways:** each of the three fragile patterns reinstated (including one in the *other* suite file, to prove the scan is not test-core-only), a message stripped from an assertion, `ok()`'s message check removed, and each of `region`'s own three checks gutted in turn. Plus one honest control: gutting `guardScan` itself passes, which is the one thing a self-checking suite cannot defend against.

No engine content changed, so the completion band is **untouched by construction**; the ledger records that rather than a measurement. (157 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.18 — MODE-1: no mode leaks into another (2026-08-13)
**The seventh audit's first finding, and it was mine — two versions old.** Five flags say what KIND of attempt this is (`daily`, `challenge`, `circuit`, `onProject`, `skirmish`) and each mode-start set only its own, so they leaked into each other. Two of the leaks were live and user-visible, both verified by running them rather than reasoned about:

- Climb a challenge, then the daily, and the end-of-burn screen rendered its **challenge block on a daily result** — quoting somebody else's code and their terms.
- Abandon a daily to the menu (`daily` is session state and stays `true`; it is not saved) and then climb an FA, and `endSession` **banked the FA's score as your daily**. Measured **581 against a real daily's ~353**, corrupting the score, the streak and the week together.

**This is SAVE-2's shape exactly** — a state built by hand-copying the fields somebody remembered — so it gets SAVE-2's shape of fix: one place that names all of them (`modeReset()`, alongside `carryOver`), and every start spreads it. The circuit's *two* entry paths (start and resume) both reset and then put `circuit` back. `startLostLine` already inherited clear flags through `freshRun`, and now says so explicitly so every start reads the same way and the guard needs no exception.

Guard: `MODE_FLAGS` and `modeReset` agree about what a mode is; `freshRun` agrees with `modeReset` (or a new run and a reset run are different things); **`carryOver` never carries one back in**; every one of the seven starts goes through `modeReset()`; both circuit paths do. Plus the two broken behaviours asserted on the engine: a reset attempt writes no challenge answer, and `bankDaily` cannot fire on an attempt that is not the daily — with a positive control so that assertion cannot pass by `bankDaily` being dead. **Negative-tested five ways**, including reinstating each original bug. (156 core + 114 balance.)

Verified at the engine and state level and by the structural guard; **not** driven through the UI's bail-to-menu flow, which the driver could not navigate.

### v10.17 — NARR-14: somebody is out there with you, and they wanted something else (2026-08-13)
**Thirty-seven routes and nobody belayed.** The game had a spotter (a hint system wearing a person) and TALKS (people you meet once and leave behind), but nothing that was *with* you — so a trip meant to read as an expedition read as a solo campaign with dialogue in it. Four partners now come out with you: **Wren** (belays like she is being timed, has opinions about your feet), **Ade** (thirty years here, knows where the water is), **Moss** (would rather look at the rock than climb it) and **Kit** (nineteen, terrifyingly strong, no idea how lucky that is). They speak when you tie in, at the fire, after a burn, and at the end of the trip.

**A partner is text and nothing else, and that is the design rather than the shortcut.** ROUTE-10 and ENG-24 both established that this project pays narrative in *information* and never in power — and after four consecutive on-band tickets the last thing the ledger needed was a fifth. So a partner cannot make you better at climbing. Their one mechanic is an **opinion**: each of them would take a particular line, they say so *before* you choose, the line they favour is marked on the choice, and they have something different to say depending on whether you took their advice. Somebody wanting something other than what you did is the whole feature.

**Who you get is derived from the run seed, not stored.** UX-5's contract is that a run is fully determined by its starting seed, so a shared seed hands the other person the same partner — and deriving rather than storing means no new save field to drift and no chance of a reload changing who is standing there.

Guard: every partner is a person with something to say at all eight moments and no repeated lines; **every line has an advocate** (a weaker "at least two differ" form passes while a whole line has nobody who would argue for it, and a line nobody argues for is one the player never hears a reason for); no partner carries a field a partner should not have; the partner table contains no mechanical term; and **nothing in `resolve`, `powerAgainst`, `biteAgainst`, `startBurn` or `autoPlay` reads the partner API** — matched on the API rather than the word, because autoPlay's opposition comments call the other hand lane a "partner". Plus: the same seed always draws the same person, 200 seeds cover the whole roster, and nobody turns up to a skirmish, a daily or a challenge. **Negative-tested seven ways.** (155 core + 114 balance.)

**Two things the render caught that review did not**, both worth recording:
- The direct line still read *"four more cruxes"* next to a panel showing **3** — SIM-6 retuned `dCrux 4 → 3` the version before and left the sentence behind. Fixed, and **`ROUTE-14`** now checks every line's prose against its own numbers (count words must match a value the line carries; "more"/"fewer" must match the sign). Tuning a number and leaving the sentence is the easiest mistake in this file.
- The run-end guard sliced a **fixed 3,200 characters** from the screen, so adding the partner's closing line pushed a needle out of the window and failed for the wrong reason. It bounds by the next phase branch now — the property it always meant. That is the second fixed-window guard this session has had to repair (the first was SAVE-2's adjacency window at DAILY-1).

### v10.16 — SIM-6: the tuning policy can see the feet lane (2026-08-13)
**A better ruler, not an easier climb, and that distinction is the whole ticket.** `autoPlay` chose the feet by **max Support** and nothing else, so it could not see that a foot *works its hold*. It scores the same two things the hand lanes are scored on now — does this clear, and what does it leave behind — with Support priced through the shared `supportNow`, so ENG-32's settling cost is in it rather than assumed away. **Worth +1.9 points of measured completion with no game rule changed** (paired n=2700: 51.7 → 53.6 on the first value model). The final model reads **n=2700 52.7 — still on the ~52 pin, so the re-pin this ticket was flagged as needing never happened.**

**A correction to my own ticket, which is why it is written down here.** I logged SIM-6 claiming every band number "under-measures a third of the climb". That conflated two things: the **lane** is ~38% of all clears; the **blindness** was worth ~1.9 points. Only the second was a measurement error.

**What it really broke was the calibration of two other guards**, because they were measured through the same weak policy — and that is the transferable lesson. A competent policy pulls the guide line further ahead of the constrained ones, so `ROUTE-8` read the traverse at **−9.8** and the direct at **−7.8** against its −8 floor. Both were retuned (traverse `dCrux −2 → −3`, direct `dCrux 4 → 3`) rather than the guard being relaxed, because ROUTE-8 is a statement about the *content* and the better measurement is the one to believe. And a better policy needs an extra burn less, so `CARD-9`'s lift went **negative** at the old handicap; `WIND_PUMP` came down **0.45 → 0.22** to keep the wind a leg-up. That took two rounds, because refining the policy's value model moved the wind again — **if you touch the policy, expect to re-read CARD-9.**

**The guard caught two things in me, and both are the kind that ship silently.** The first value model scored only the placement turn — and because ENG-32 floors a fresh foot at one Support, it literally could not tell a Flag (Support 2) from a Rock Over (Power 3): they pay the hands identically on the turn they land and only separate once planted. The guard failed and forced the better model, which values a staying foot on what it will pay bedded in. Second, one assertion passed only by **hand ordering** — `reduce` keeps its accumulator on a tie, so with the Support foot first it stayed green even with Support not being scored at all; proved by injection, fixed by putting the strong foot first.

Guard: the deciding case (a workable foothold the reachable foot takes and the Support foot does not); the inverse (nothing workable, so Support wins); no foothold at all; Support scored through the shared function, not a second copy; a planted foot is never thrown away, since the policy only fills an empty lane — which is what makes ENG-32's "leave them alone" trade available to it; the harness still plays only through the shipping policy. **Negative-tested six ways.** (153 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.15 — SOCIAL-2: challenge codes — a problem, and the terms it goes on (2026-08-13)
**SOCIAL-1 let you paste a result, and that was the whole social surface** — people could compare numbers, but nobody could climb *your* thing. A challenge code is the other direction: a **seed and one objective**, short enough to read out over a phone, and it needs no server either. The objective is what makes it a challenge rather than a link — a seed alone is "try this problem", where a seed plus *"and do it without shaking out"* is something to beat. The objectives are DAILY-2's, so a challenge asks for something the game already knows how to judge, show and tick.

**One generator, two doors.** The daily already had exactly the right problem generator, so `dailyRoute` and `challengeRoute` now both call one `problemFrom(seed, note)` rather than the second copy of the tables that would have drifted (the ENG-19 lesson) — and DAILY-2's own route snapshot is what proves the refactor changed nothing.

**The check character is the load-bearing part, and the guard caught me getting it wrong.** The first cut summed `seed % 36`, which ignores every digit above the last — so **210 of the single-character typos in the guard's sweep read back as a valid, different problem**, which is the exact failure the check exists to prevent: two people compare scores on two different boulders and neither can tell. It is **ISO 7064 MOD 37,36** now, which *provably* detects every single-character substitution and every adjacent transposition — the two mistakes a person actually makes reading a code aloud. The guard demands **zero** on both sweeps and gets it. (The 37th symbol is why the check alphabet carries a `*`: 37 is prime and base36 has only 36 digits to spend.)

A second gap the injections found in the guard itself: the "a code from a newer build is refused" case originally used one *guessed* check character, so it was refused at the checksum and never exercised the bounds test at all — removing the upper bound left it green. It tries all 37 symbols now, so only the bounds test can refuse it.

Off-band by construction: a challenge is a skirmish, and the harness climbs the campaign. Verified end to end in the real build — MAKE ONE produces a code, the tile resolves it into the problem and its terms (*Hollow Bulge · V6 · 9 holds. Take a crux with your feet off.*), a one-character typo disables the tile, and the valid code climbs. Guard: round-trip across the seed range and every objective; case and spacing tolerated; rubbish refused; a challenge naming an objective this build lacks is dropped on load as well as on read; the answer says whether the terms were **met**, not just the score; the grid is one mark per hold; it survives a save. **Negative-tested seven ways.** (152 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.14 — VIS-8: the climb screen was broken for four versions (2026-08-13)
**Reported off a phone with a screenshot, and it was mine.** `.board` has named the climb screen's lane rows — a three-column grid — since v0. The season board added by `DAILY-3` at v10.9 was *also* called `.board`. One stylesheet, one global namespace, so the later rule won: both **THE ROUTE** and **YOUR MOVES** were forced into a 30px-tall, 230px-wide flexbox, and the hold cards overflowed upward across the header, the conditions line and the progress count. Renamed to `.wkboard`; the climb screen measures correctly again (rows 366×120, slots 118×120, nothing escaping, no horizontal overflow).

**It shipped through v10.10, v10.11, v10.12 and v10.13 and nothing caught it, which is the part worth fixing.** `tsc` cannot see a CSS collision. No guard rendered the climb screen's *layout* — the ones that touch it read `innerText`, which is unaffected. And in the worst of it: while checking a card caption for `CARD-17` I took a screenshot **of this exact breakage** and read straight past it, because I was looking at the words and not the picture.

So the tripwire is generic rather than a patch for this one name: **a bare class may only be declared once in the stylesheet.** `.wrap` is the documented exception — its second rule sets a custom property (`--fs`, the text-scale root) and nothing else, so the rule is precisely "twice is fine only if one side is variables only". The same guard also asserts the **stylesheet is not truncated**, because a stray backtick inside the CSS template literal silently ends it and discards the rest — which has now happened twice (`DEV-3`'s comment, and this ticket's own first draft, caught within a minute by this guard's own length check). Negative-tested three ways: reintroducing the original `.board` clash, injecting a backtick, and clashing an unrelated pair. (151 core + 114 balance.)

**Process note, since four versions is not a near miss:** a rendered check that reads text is not a rendered check. The climb screen now has a geometry assertion behind it — rows, slot sizes, child-escapes-parent, page overflow — and that is the check to repeat whenever the stylesheet is touched.

### v10.13 — AUD-2: the game has a sense of place, and the finale has none on purpose (2026-08-13)
**Eight one-shot effects and nothing sustained**, so the game had no sense of where you were. It has beds now: the forest floor barely moving, the desert towers dry and thinner, the **alpine wall** where the wind is a character, a stove and a low hiss at a camp, the trading post's canvas and generator, and the van's radio as a detuned pair heard through the panel — no words.

**The finale is silent, and that is the feature.** "Silence is the loudest thing you can do with a boss" — so `bedFor` returns `none` on the finale and that outranks the act it sits in. Act 3 is *not* otherwise silent, which is what makes the silence say something; a guard asserts both halves, because a bed playing over the finale would throw the whole idea away.

**Synthesised, not sampled** — and the roadmap's own long-standing error is why that is worth stating: a row claimed for seven versions that *Tone.js is already a dependency*. It never was. `package.json` is React and React-DOM, and the build is one inlined HTML file with no external requests, so the beds are built on the same two primitives the effects use — looped noise through a moving bandpass, plus held oscillators — with a 3-second noise buffer so the loop does not read as a loop, and a slow LFO on both filter and level for the gust. A guard now fails if anything fetches or decodes an asset, or if an audio library appears in the dependencies.

**The decision is a pure function in the engine; only the oscillators live in the screen.** That split is deliberate and it is the answer to the obvious problem with this ticket: I cannot hear a test. `bedFor(s)` is total over every phase, act and in-run combination, so nothing can ask for a graph that does not exist, and the part that can be *wrong* is testable even though the part that can be *ugly* is not. `bed()` is idempotent, so a render-driven effect can call it every state change for free; exactly one place stops a running graph; and a hidden tab is silenced so a backgrounded PWA is not left droning.

**Ambience gets its own switch.** A sustained drone you can only silence by killing the effects too is one people mute at the OS instead. It is a player setting, so it survives a save and a new run.

Verified in a real browser as far as it can be: entering act 1 builds exactly the forest graph (1 noise loop, 1 bandpass, 2 LFOs — `air()` with no hum), a screen change adds **zero** new sources, and no console or page errors. **Stated limitation: I confirmed the graph runs and makes signal, not that it sounds good — that judgement needs ears on a device.** Guard: the three acts are three distinct places; the map matches the wall; camp, post and van are their own; the menu and any skirmish or daily are silent; every phase resolves to a real bed name; both switches gate both call sites (an earlier version of this assertion passed with the main effect ungated, because the visibility handler also matched the regex — caught by injecting exactly that); one bed at a time; the setting persists. **Negative-tested seven ways.** (150 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.12 — ENG-32: the feet lane is a trade, and it says what it is doing (2026-08-13)
**Measuring first showed the ticket's premise was half wrong in the useful direction.** "Feet are a lane you fill, not a decision you make" — but instrumenting a campaign showed the **feet lane works about 39% of every hold worked**. A third of the climb was already happening down there and nothing on screen said so, and `autoPlay` picks feet by Support alone, blind to it.

What was actually missing was the **trade**. A foot paid its full Support the instant it landed, so moving your feet cost nothing and leaving them cost nothing either — and the card pool already carries the shape of a trade (Support 2+ averages 1.0 Power, Support 1 averages 1.6) that never bit. **Support is earned now**: a foot placed this turn gives the hands less than one that has stood a turn, and because a high-Power foot tends to *work* its hold and leave the wall, it never settles — so stepping up costs you the Support that standing still would have paid. Lane-local, and it rides `powerAgainst`, which the preview and resolve already share. The lane also **reads** now: *HANDS +1*, and *SETTLING (+1 NEXT TURN)* when standing a turn would actually pay more.

**Getting there took three corrections, and all three are the same shape: a global change landing on something's IDENTITY rather than on a player's choice.**
1. **Charged flat it was 2.0 points** (paired n=2700: 53.0 → 51.0) and took two other guards with it — the weakest climber to 4.3% against its 5% floor, and CARD-9 to a *negative* lift. One point of Support, withheld everywhere, is a difficulty change and not a texture change. A **floor** that never takes a foot below 1 Support puts the cost on the solid placements only.
