2. Freshness first read **`settled`** — which the Trad Dad's own signature (`settleMax: 0`, "nothing you place ever settles") pins to zero for ever. That climber paid the settling cost every single turn as a side effect of being itself. `set` is a separate field now: **planted is not settled.**
3. Charged on **turn 1** it taxed every burn's *start* — and a Second Wind burn is nothing but opening turns, because it begins part-pumped. That is what inverted CARD-9 to −1.3. Exempt on the opening turn: there is no previous turn you could have kept your feet from.

**Two knock-on fixes, both precedented.** The cost still landed hardest on whoever moves their feet most, so the **Comp Kid** (85 turns a run against the Trad Dad's 133) was bought back with `quickFeet` — feet it trusts the moment they land — rather than the 5% floor being lowered to meet it, which is the CARD-15 pattern. And **`WIND_PUMP` went 0.55 → 0.45**, not back to 0.4: 0.55 measured a lift of −1.3 and 0.4 measured 3.8 against a ceiling of 4, so *both ends are coin flips* and the middle is chosen for margin. That retires the v10.10 ENG-21 nerf, which ENG-32 now does the work of.

Final: **n=2700 51.7%, right on the ~52 pin**, and the whole ledger green. Also caught on a render, not in review: the settling note keyed on `settled` and promised a Support-1 smear a point the floor means it can never gain — it is derived from the shared formula now, so the label cannot lie. Guard: both sides of the floor; planted-is-not-settled including at `settleMax: 0`; the turn-1 exemption; the buy-back reaches that climber and only that climber and not a skirmish; one Support formula with `powerAgainst` as its only consumer; the feet lane works holds and the preview says so; preview agrees with resolve fresh and planted. **Negative-tested eight ways.** (149 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.11 — CARD-17: a bonus curse is a tax you can pay off (2026-08-13)
**The four BONUS curses were the last dead cards in the game, and verifying it on disk showed they were *worse* than dead.** Bad Landing, Ego, Doubt and Sandbagged Beta carry a pump cost and nothing else — and `playBonusStep` sends a played bonus to the **discard**, so paying to play one bought you nothing *and handed the card back on the next reshuffle*. Strictly negative: the only correct play was to let it clog your hand for the whole burn. (The roadmap said "cost pump for nothing"; the recycling half was worse than recorded.)

They are a tax you can **pay off** now. Play one and it is **written off** — exhausted for the rest of the burn instead of recycled — for the cost already printed on it plus `CURSE_TAX` (1), so 2–3 pump out of an 11-pump meter. That is CARD-16's own deferred direction: a **removal** mechanic rather than an active per-turn effect, because a per-turn pump term compounds and this project has measured that five times. It is deliberately a *worse* deal than the camp's `cut`, which removes the card from the run deck permanently and for free — mid-climb you are paying for the timing, not the removal.

**The mechanic is visible to the tuning sim, which is the whole reason it is not still dead.** ENG-25's lesson was that a mechanic `autoPlay` cannot use gets measured as worthless; the `worth` heuristic had no curse clause, so the policy would never have written one off. It does now, and only with real headroom (`PUMP_MAX − 5`), because a write-off buys hand quality rather than progress. **Band-neutral, measured paired at n=2700: ON 53.0% vs OFF 53.3%** — a 0.3-point difference against an SE of ~0.96. (n=2700 because ENG-21 established that n=900 cannot resolve a question this size here.)

**And it is surfaced where it is used.** `cardHints` only renders on the draft screens, so the option would have existed mid-climb with nothing telling you — the same "technically present, practically dead" state the ticket exists to end. The card in hand now reads *write off · 3 pump* with *Tap: gone for this burn*, verified on a render in the real build.

Guard: the premise is asserted rather than trusted (each bonus curse costs something and does nothing else, so if one ever gains an effect this test fails rather than lying); a curse is exhausted and an ordinary bonus still recycles; the price is cost + tax and touches nothing else; the camp cut is still the permanent one; the hint quotes the real price; the card in hand says all three things; a MOVE curse keeps its hex and is not offered a write-off. **The policy clauses are pinned by a boundary fixture derived from the constants** — an earlier version used a merely-pumped fixture that the affordability gate already rejected, so it passed with the headroom rule deleted *and* with the surcharge left out of that gate. **Negative-tested eight ways.** (148 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.10 — ENG-21: the route acts on the climber, not just the stone (2026-08-13)
**All four original route moves did the same KIND of thing** — mutate a hold's numbers. The route could make the wall harder or easier and that was the entire vocabulary; it never engaged the climber, only the stone. Three moves that act on *you*: **`spit`** the hold rejects a placement it will not hold (the card comes back to hand, the lane goes unanswered) — judged on Power in the hands and **Support** on the feet, because every feet card is Power 0–3 so a Power rule would have spat nearly all of them; **`pin`** no shaking out in that lane this turn (the rest still costs you the card and the position, it just does not pay, and it breaks flow exactly as the noRest phases do); **`stance`** the gift — the hand lanes get the Support you never placed, and you are not campusing while you have it. Board and lane effects only, and no flat per-turn pump term anywhere in them.

**Two of the three were wrong on the first cut, and both failures are shapes this codebase keeps finding.** `stance` originally paid a **card draw and shipped dead** — `refillAndDraw` tops the hand back to `HAND_SIZE` at the end of every turn, so the extra card was absorbed the same turn it arrived (the CARD-11/`restChip` failure mode again). It pays in Support now, routed through `powerAgainst`/`biteAgainst`, which the preview and resolve already share, so exactness is automatic; the guard tells that sanctioned Bite/Support pathway apart from a flat clock term by asserting **no** pump change with nothing on the wall and exactly `CAMPUS_BITE` where there is. `spit` originally **destroyed** the card and the roll chose the **kind before the lane** (so spit could avoid the feet lane) — both were balance mistakes, and both were caught only by measuring against the shipped build.

**The measurement discipline is the story of this ticket, including two of my own wrong conclusions.** Moves fire on a *timer*, so net cost in the move table scales with how long a climb takes: burning the card broke `ROUTE-8` outright (guide 55.4 against direct 49 and traverse 47, both traps) and put an archetype on 5%. And swapping two RNG draws reshuffles every roll after it — that reshuffle **alone** moved the CARD-9 wind arm **+2.9 points** and read as ENG-21 breaking the ceiling. Before that was found, an earlier tuning pass chased **five weight configs at n=900 that all sat inside one standard error of each other** (55.2 / 55.4 / 55.6 / 56.1 / 56.4) and concluded ENG-21 made the game *easier*; a paired n=2700 pass showed the opposite sign (55.6 → 54.1). **n=900 cannot resolve a 1.5-point question here** — one arm's SE is ~1.7 — and that is now written into the ledger for the next person. Final: band **~0.8 harder** (n=900 53.7 → 52.9), lines, climbers and spread all holding.

**Joint balance pass, stated rather than buried.** A burn is now slightly less reliable, so a *retry* is worth more, and CARD-9's lift went 2.4 → 4.0 against its ceiling of 4. Trimming ENG-21 to 8% of the move table only reached 3.8 — 0.2 inside a ~2.3 SE, a guard passing on a coin flip rather than a fix (the GUARD-1 lesson). So the **item** pays: `WIND_PUMP` **0.4 → 0.55**, the dial BAL-15 established for exactly this reading, and the lift is back to **2.0**. A deliberate nerf to the Second Wind in service of the contract CARD-9 states.

Also closed a latent trap: `previewPump` built a post-move state and then called `previewLane` on it, applying the same move **twice** — unreachable today because every caller passes precomputed lanes, but a grease would have counted double the moment one did not. `afterMove` clears `routeMove` on the way out and there is one application path.

Guard: all seven kinds reachable and the weights a fixed, **non-negative** 100 in every condition; the draw order *and count* pinned by replaying the stream by hand; spit rejects under the threshold and not at it, per lane, and hands the card back; pin denies the shed and the CARD-11 chip in its lane and nowhere else; stance is worth exactly `STANCE_SUPPORT`, cancels exactly `CAMPUS_BITE`, is not double-paid over real feet, and never outlives its turn or survives a save; **the preview agrees with resolve on pump and on clears for every kind on every lane**; every kind telegraphs a sentence. **Negative-tested ten ways.** (147 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.9 — DAILY-3: four weeks make a season that finishes (2026-08-12)
**The weekly ladder reset into a void.** Every seven days the number went back to zero, nothing accumulated past a week, and nothing was ever *finished* — so there was no arc longer than a week and no reason to care about week three. A season is four of those weeks: a running total, the days you showed up, and the four weekly totals so the board has something to draw. On rollover the season that just ended pays a title off the total it finished on — *Weekender · Committed · Season Local · Crag Rat*, pinned to what a day is actually worth (a strong daily banks ~580 after DAILY-2, so a 28-day season tops out near 16,000 and the rungs sit at roughly 3, 7, 14 and 23 days of good climbing).

**Stated rather than hidden:** the title lands the next time you play, not at midnight on the last day. There is no clock running in a game with no server, and inventing one would mean lying about when it happened — so a season you never come back from stays unclosed, and closes when you do. Titles reuse DAILY-1's list, so the save gained no new shape for it, and when a streak rung and a season close land on the same day they are applied in turn rather than either overwriting the other.

**Tomorrow is posted on the menu.** `dailyRoute` and `dailyForecast` have taken a key since SKIRM-3, so what is coming has been computable all along and was simply never shown — the cheapest come-back hook in the game. The conditions are what you plan around, so those are revealed; the objectives are what you turn up for, so those are not. The board gives every week a visible **empty** track: without one, a season with two weeks left to climb rendered as a two-week board rather than a four-week one with work still to do (caught on a render, not in review).

Off-band: a title is cosmetic and the season total is a leaderboard number no run can spend. Guard: a season is exactly four epoch-aligned weeks, with all 28 days inside it and in the column its week claims — **the boundary is derived, after a first version of the guard assumed a calendar date started a season and failed for its own reason rather than the code's**; the ladder pays the highest rung reached rather than the first passed, and nothing under the first; a rollover pays off the total the season ENDED on and starts the new one clean; a season title never doubles and never displaces a streak title earned the same day; tomorrow crosses months, years and a leap day; the tease shows conditions and does not read the objectives; the season survives a save. **Negative-tested nine ways.** (146 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.8 — DAILY-2: the daily asks for two things, not just a score (2026-08-12)
**The daily was one number, so the optimal line was the optimal line every day forever.** Two objectives a day give the shared problem a shape: today it wants a flash and a cool head, tomorrow it wants you off the rests. They are announced **before** you tie in — that is the point of a daily, everyone solving the same stated puzzle — ticked off in the recap, and carried in the share, which is the part actually worth comparing: two people can both score well and only one of them kept off the rests.

Two new per-burn readings, counted where the thing happens in `resolve` and cleared by `startBurn` beside `cleared` and `peakPump`: **`rests`** (turns you shook out — using the game's existing `restedThis`, so an objective and a `norest` sequence agree about the same turn rather than inventing a second rule) and **`cruxFree`** (crux holds taken with nothing in the feet lane, read off the board you *handed in* rather than the one resolve has chewed on, so a feet card that blew does not look like a choice you never made).

**On the goal seed — corrected by a negative test, which is the note worth keeping.** The first version of this comment claimed that reusing the route's seed would silently change today's problem for everybody. That is false: `dailyRoute` and `dailyForecast` each construct their own `RNG` and share no mutable stream, and the injection proving it passed the guard. What reusing it would actually do is *correlate* the goals with the route — the grade and the objectives locked to one number, so every V4 day asks the same two things and a month of dailies reads as a short loop. Hashing `key#goals` keeps them independent, and the guard now pins that at the call site (asserting `dailySeed(key+'#goals') !== dailySeed(key)` would only have tested the hash function) plus a correlation check across a month.

Off-band by construction: the score is a leaderboard number no run can spend, and the XP rides the daily, which the balance harness never plays — the carve-out `claimStep` already documents. **Two existing guards were sharpened rather than loosened** where the new payout collided with them: SKIRM-3 recomputed `dailyScore(base)` and expected the week to equal 500 + that, and now asserts the week stacks exactly what the day *banked*, whatever it banked; DAILY-1 compared a banked state to an unbanked one and so read the objective payout as the ladder's, and is differential now — a rung day and an ordinary day differ by the rung and nothing else. **Negative-tested seven ways.** (145 core + 114 balance.)

### v10.7 — DAILY-1: the streak pays a ladder, and forgives one miss a week (2026-08-12)
**Coming back day after day paid nothing.** `dailyStreak` was a number on the save that exactly one deed happened to read: `bankDaily` granted no cash, no XP and no cards, so the game asked for a habit and gave nothing back for keeping one — and at collection-complete, XP is inert anyway, so paying it there would have been nothing too. The ladder pays at **3 / 7 / 14 / 30 days**: kit at every rung, a cosmetic title at the two long ones (*Regular*, *Local*).

**The payout is off-band by construction, and that is the design, not a caveat.** Kit lands in a **`larder`** you carry rather than in your hands; the App hands it over at the start of a trip, capped at `KIT_MAX`, with anything that does not fit staying banked for the next one. The balance harness calls `newRun` directly and so can never be handed a larder — the same pattern CARD-14 used to keep consumables off the guide line, and the same one `claimStep` uses. Nothing on the ladder pays cash, xp, a level or a draftable card, so the pinned ~52 completion band cannot feel a streak reward at any length.

**Missing a day is forgiven once a week.** An attempt two days after your last one keeps the streak and spends `graceWeek`; a second miss in that same week resets it, and a three-day gap is a gap regardless. One rest day, not an open door — the streak stays worth something to have.

The menu shows what the streak is *for* now: the length, the title, whether the rest day is spent, and either what is banked for the next trip or how far off the next rung is. Three new persistent fields (`larder`, `titles`, `graceWeek`) wired through `GameState` → `SaveData` → `saveGame` → `loadGame` → `freshRun` → `carryOver`.

Guard holds both halves: the rungs sit at 3/7/14/30 and pay only kit that exists and titles that never duplicate; no rung touches cash, xp, level, hand kit or the collection; the forgiveness is one miss a week; the handover is capped and keeps the remainder; all three fields survive a save. **Negative-tested three ways** — removing the grace, paying cash at a rung, and dropping the cap each fail it with the right message. Also fixed on the way: the DAILY-1 comment pushed `carryOver` past the 420-char window of the SAVE-2 adjacency guard, and the comment moved rather than the window (144 core + 114 balance guards pass).

### v10.1–v10.6 — the rest of the fifth audit, in order (2026-08-12)
Six ships, closing the audit 10 for 10.

**v10.1 `GUARD-1` — CARD-9 measures the lift and the mechanism, not an absolute.** The old form could fail for the wrong reason: `wind < 58` is an ABSOLUTE, and `wind` contains the base band, so it tripped when the game got **easier** rather than when the item got **stronger** — two failures needing opposite fixes, made indistinguishable (ROUTE-13 hit exactly that at v9.94 and was reverted for it). It asserts the **lift** now, which is what the ticket always claimed in words. The "is the harness really spending it" half was a 0.5-pt threshold under a difference of two proportions whose estimate had read 0.5, +6.0, +3.6 and ~1.5 on sample size alone; `run.mjs` **reports the winds it spends** now, so that is a deterministic integer. Sample tripled 300 → 900, spending exactly the runs GUARD-6 freed. It also promotes a four-version-old prose claim into an assertion: the default arm must spend **zero** winds, which is *why* the pinned band is kit-free. Ceiling of 4 sits between the unfixed item (+6.0, which BAL-15 called a skip) and the fixed one (+2.4 at n=900) — negative-tested by reverting `WIND_PUMP`, which takes the lift to 7.6 and fails with the right message.

**v10.2 `SAVE-3/4` — reloading is not an exploit.** `postOpen` reads `shoppedAt` and `vanOpen` reads `vanRaided` — the fields behind the code's own "once a stage" and "once a range" — and **neither was saved**, so a reload reopened the post with freshly rolled stock and the van with it. Both persisted now, with `trail`, `eventChose`, `reroll` and `line`. The daily promised **"One go"** and did not keep it (the day was stamped only at resolve, so quitting bought a fresh roll — with the streak and weekly ladder sitting on that number); a go is spent when it **starts** now. That needed its own field: `bankDaily` derives the streak from `dailyDay` being *yesterday*, so an early stamp would have silently reset every streak to 1. `dailyUsed()` is the single source of truth the menu, recap and start gate all read. Accepted cost, stated rather than buried: a crash also spends the day — the honest reading of one go.

**v10.3 `DEV-3` — the endless run is written down, and the page cannot be pulled away.** The Circuit sets `inRun: false`, so **not one line of an endless run was ever saved** — a twenty-line streak was pure memory. It gets its own save block (score, deck, skin, psyche, gear, boons, kit), the line regenerated from the count the way it was made; the tile reads BACK ON THE CIRCUIT and continues rather than restarts. And `overscroll-behavior` existed on exactly one element (the hand scroller, x-axis), so Android pull-to-refresh was live in an installed PWA and an over-flick could reload the app mid-climb, where nothing is saved.

**v10.4 `DEV-2` — the service worker cannot pin a stale or broken build.** Precache reused the HTTP cache, so an install could store the **previous** build's HTML under the **new** cache name and serve it forever ("the update never reaches the player"); nothing checked the response, so a captive-portal page or a deploy-window 404 was cached permanently — and the game being one inlined file, that **bricked the install**; and there was no update path at all. Fixed with `{cache:'reload'}`, an `res.ok` guard, and a `controllerchange` reload that is deliberately careful: once only, only when a controller already existed (so a first install does not bounce), and **only when not mid-climb** — the app publishes `window.__SB_BUSY__`, because the naive version would have caused the very bug DEV-3 just fixed. The inline snippet was extracted from the built HTML and run through `node --check`.

**v10.5 `DEV-1` — content is inset past the notch and the home indicator.** `viewport-fit=cover` is an explicit opt-**out** of automatic insetting and `env(safe-area-inset-*)` appeared **zero** times, so every screen's bottom edge sat in the gesture strip. The worst case is the irony: `A11Y-5` exists to put COMMIT under the thumb and was putting it in the OS swipe-up zone. Fixed at the page, the sheets and the sticky reach foot, and the last two `vh` units became `dvh` — the splash already knew, the rest of the file caught up.

**v10.6 `UI-4` — the map says what each stage ahead of you is.** `ActMap` was passed no node types, so every future stage was an identical circle with a bare number: the run's decision surface could not say stage 5 is a camp or stage 9 the boss. Half the idea already existed (past stages annotate themselves from the trail) — only the future was anonymous. Each kind now draws in the same ink shorthand, a plain climb keeps its number so the marked ones read, and the boss gets a **red cross** rather than a glyph because it is the thing you want to see coming from the bottom of the page. Guarded against the engine's own `NodeType` list, so a type added later cannot arrive as an anonymous circle.

### v10.0 — GUARD-2/3/4/6: the guards can fail again, and the ledger is gated on a release (2026-08-12)
The fifth audit's meta-finding, and the reason it went before more features: across four audits every ticket got a guard, but **some of those guards could not fail**, and the balance ledger they all lean on **was never actually run by a release**. **`GUARD-2`** — "no unicode escapes in JSX text" ran its regex against `engine.ts`, a file with **zero** `className=` in it, which a sibling guard actively forbids ever adding; the pattern could never match, so the check had been **vacuously green since v0.4** while all 571 JSX attributes sat unscanned in `App.tsx`. It reads the screens now, and both files, preserving the deliberate distinction (an escape in a *string literal* is fine — JS decodes it, and App.tsx has five, the trail marks — an escape in *JSX text* is the bug, because that is what shipped literally). **`GUARD-3`** — three guards die silently when an anchor is renamed: `slice(indexOf(x), …)` yields `''` and every assertion in the block is a negative, so an empty slice passes. The victims were the font-scale CSS half (*the very guard whose comment records the last time it silently matched nothing*), "an injury must never pay you anything", and the SIM-3 "the screen must not reimplement these rules" block. Each asserts its anchor before slicing now. **`GUARD-4`** — "a wild boon is a trade, not a gift" accepted `mods.dyno` as **both** the gain and the cost, so for Deadpointing (whose only mod *is* dyno) it was a tautology, and the real cost — the halved Contact in `startBurn`'s deck build — was asserted nowhere; each side needs a distinct mechanism now and the halving is pinned behaviourally on the deck the burn actually deals. **`GUARD-6`** — the band, the climber spread, ROUTE-8 and CARD-9 all live in `if (SLOW)` and **no script passed `slow`**, with no CI: `check` and `ship` both skipped them, so a release could be cut without the band being evaluated once — it had been holding only because a human kept typing it. Added `test:slow`/`check:slow` and put the ledger in front of `ship`. **Paid for the gate rather than just imposing it:** three guards read only the full-journal band but paid for all three, so `run.mjs` takes `PAGES=14` — **~4,800 of ~16,100 campaign runs removed** with the measured values byte-identical (each band re-seeds independently; verified 52.7% either way), and the header's "~20s" claim (a large part of why it got skipped) now states the truth: ~11,300 runs, about four minutes, measured at 230s. **Every repair was negative-tested** — deleting Deadpointing's `* 0.5`, renaming the CSS/`playBonus` anchors, and injecting the v0.4 escape bug all now FAIL where they previously passed. 105/105 fast, 114/114 with the ledger, green at the ~52 pin. New GUARD-6 guard pins the wiring itself. Left queued on purpose: **`GUARD-1`** (CARD-9's margin sits inside its own noise) wants the *assertion* restructured to measure the lift rather than an absolute — a change to what the guard claims, which is a separate decision from repairing guards that cannot fail.

### v9.99 — SAVE-1/2/5: the save system stops losing your game (2026-08-12)
The fifth audit's first three items, shipped together because they are one theme — the save system destroying or refusing the data it exists to protect — each with its own guard. **`SAVE-1`:** `loadGame` returned `{}` both for "this slot is empty" and "I could not read this", so a corrupt or newer-build save was treated as a new game and then **persisted over**; it returns `null` now, the state carries a transient `saveBlocked`, the persist effect refuses to write over a slot that failed to read, and the menu says so in red. One unknown card name in the run deck threw out of `spawn()` and took the whole save with it through that same catch — unknown names are filtered, so a renamed card costs you the card, not the campaign. **IMPORT** wrote the code, reported *"Imported. Reloading the slot."*, and never reloaded — the persist effect then wrote the stale in-memory game back over it, so the one feature whose job is not losing data lost it while claiming success; it reloads now. **"wipe this slot"** on the slot you were playing cleared only the disk and the in-memory game was written straight back, so the wipe resurrected. **`SAVE-2`:** `saveGame` dropped the run whenever `tier` ran past the act map — which it *always* does at a boss, since a boss is the last tier and starting a climb pre-writes `tier+1` as the "you abandoned it" state — so the save said "no run" from the moment you tapped into any boss; the tier is clamped now. And the persist effect was an allowlist of six phases excluding every screen after a send (`gear`, `pack`, `claim`, `epilogue`, `shop`, `event`, `circuitNext`), so closing the tab on the post-boss gear offer lost the whole act and on the **epilogue lost the campaign win itself** (wins, the ascent-style unlock, `archWins`, the last journal page, the XP, the history record); it is a denylist now — everything saves except the two mid-climb phases whose state is deliberately not in `SaveData`. Depending on the whole state also closed the psyche-refund hole (`WAIT A DAY` was refundable by reloading) as a welcome side effect, at no write-per-tap cost since mid-climb is where the frequent taps are. **`SAVE-5`:** `level` and `xp` were the only two fields loaded without a default, under a comment promising every field had one — spread let `xp: undefined` beat `freshRun`'s 0, `gainXp` computed **NaN**, and the level-up loop was never true again, so levelling froze permanently and every XP readout read NaN. Reachable, not theoretical: `importSave` validated exactly one field, so `{"level":1}` imported cleanly and bricked the save; both fields are defaulted and type-checked and the importer now checks the types it is about to trust. Off-band throughout. 104/104 + 140/140, 113/113 slow as insurance, and verified **end-to-end in a real browser**: start a run, reload, it resumes with xp reading 0/35 rather than NaN and no console errors. Two guard notes kept honest rather than convenient: "a save from a NEWER build is refused" asserted `Object.keys(...).length === 0` and now asserts `null` — the *stronger* contract; and "nothing builds a state from scratch without carrying you over" scanned FORWARD only, so hoisting the read into `const loaded = loadGame(slot)` tripped it even though the state below merges that value — the window is symmetric now, negative-tested by injecting a bare `freshRun` 7.5k characters from any `carryOver`/`loadGame`, which it still catches.

### v9.98 — ROUTE-13: the guidebook is named (2026-08-12)
The last item of the fourth audit, and the one that could not ship until the headroom existed — **it closes the audit 10 for 10.** `ROUTE-5` named eleven routes and stopped; the other 25 were stat blocks. They are named now — The Nave, The Black Glass, The Kiln Pocket, The Numb Crimp, The Coffin, The Board, The Whiteout, The Furnace and the rest — each a hold with a line about it, each paying a one-time read when first worked (`ROUTE-12`, band-free). **Deliberately still plain, and guarded as such:** the first four boulders (a named feature is noise while you are still learning what a crux is) and **THE LOST LINE — the line nobody has named.** Giving the finale a named hold would contradict the premise, so it keeps its anonymity. *Why it needed `BAL-15` first:* a signature **replaces** an ordinary hold, so a mild one makes its route *easier* (the v9.94 attempt measured +3 for exactly that), and a distinctive hold is precisely what a bought extra burn beats on the retry — at the old +6 Second-Wind lift the set pushed the kit run to 62% against CARD-9's 58% ceiling, which is why it was reverted. With the wind honest and the signatures pitched at `dGrip` 2–3 (inside the range `deathblock` already demonstrated): band **n=300 52.7 / n=600 53.3** (~52 pin held), CARD-9 **wind 56.3 < 58** with the lift still real. Both ledgers updated, and CARD-9's now records that its **~1.7 clearance is the binding constraint on the way up** — the next change that lifts the base hits it before the drift band, and the answer there is to look at what is inflating the retry, not to move the 58 (the harness is deterministic, so the margin cannot flake). 104/104 + 137/137, 113/113 slow. New ROUTE-13 guard: ≥30 routes named, the plain list exact so a new route cannot silently arrive unnamed, the finale's anonymity asserted twice, no signature past the demonstrated `dGrip`.

### v9.97 — ENG-28: a hold's ability is read through abilityOf, never its base type (2026-08-12)
Found while attempting `ROUTE-13`, and live in the shipped game since `ROUTE-5` let a signature override an ability. A signature may override the ability of the hold it is built on — **The Wet Jug is a jug that is Greasy, not a Rest** — and a brushed hold has none at all. `resolve` has always gone through `abilityOf`; three readers reached for `HOLD_STATS[name].ability` directly: `previewPump`'s Rest-shed (so on **Icebox Corner** clearing the signature hold made the *preview* shed a pump the resolution never did — an `ENG-26`-class break), `previewPump`'s Sharp-on-blow (the same divergence the other way), and **`coach()`** — the spotter shouted beta off the hold's base type, so it gave the wrong advice on any overriding signature and advised about brushed holds that have no ability left. All three go through `abilityOf` now. Preview- and prose-only, so the band cannot move — measured identical at 50.3 either side. Guarded **structurally**, because this class recurs by being the easy thing to reach for: only `abilityOf` may look an ability up by hold name, asserted by counting the base-ability lookups and pinning them to the two inside that function, plus a behavioural preview-vs-resolve check on an overriding signature.

### v9.96 — BAL-15: a Second Wind buys the go, not a fresh body (2026-08-12)
The balance-headroom session, and the finding was that **the item was wrong, not the guard.** A Second Wind's retry started on an **empty clock** — a complete extra attempt, carrying the beta you had just banked, which is strictly better information than the burn you just lost. Measured: **+6.0 points** of campaign completion off one cheap consumable (base 50.3 → wind 56.3). That is exactly the "skip" `CARD-9`'s own ceiling exists to forbid ("an extra burn is a leg-up, not a skip"), and with `CARD-15`'s re-pin to ~52 it had left only **1.7 points** of clearance under that ceiling — which is why `ROUTE-13` could not land. A wind burn now starts **part-pumped** (`WIND_PUMP` 0.4 of the meter), exactly as a caught roped fall already does (`FALL_PUMP`): chalk up, shake out, tie back in — but you have been fighting a while. The lift falls **6.0 → 2.0**, so clearance goes **1.7 → 5.7**. The base band is byte-identical either side (50.3 both) because the default sim buys no kit, so the drift pin is untouched — this is purely the kit path being made honest. **`CARD-9` itself is unchanged**: its ceiling and its lower bound both still stand, and the lift still clears the lower bound. 113/113 slow. New BAL-15 guard: spending a wind marks the burn it bought and raises the cap; that burn starts at exactly `floor(PUMP_MAX * WIND_PUMP)` while an ordinary retry starts at 0; the mark is spent, so the burn after it is not charged again.

### v9.95 — UI-2: a title card, and the menu rebuilt as tiles (2026-08-12)
Reported off a phone: the main menu was "boring and even not clear what goes to what". Both halves of that were **structural, not taste**. *The ambiguity:* every mode was the same full-width ink bar, and each caption sat in a **separate element below** its button — so "an unclimbed line · name it" and "endless" sat under a row of two buttons with nothing tying either line to either action. A mode is a **Tile** now: its ink mark, its name and its own caption inside the one element you tap, so ownership is unambiguous by construction. *The monotony:* four near-identical ink-filled bars left the page with no first read. **Exactly one tile takes the ink**, and it is whatever you are in the middle of — a resumable run outranks everything, then the teaching climb while it is unclimbed, then the expedition; everything else is paper with a hand-drawn `Ink` border (the border language of the board and the map, which was already the quality bar). Three section labels group it: THE EXPEDITION / ONE CLIMB, ON ITS OWN / BEFORE YOU GO. *Scannability:* one 24px ink mark per mode in the existing `CARD-4` shape vocabulary — a topo line to a summit cross for the expedition, a sun over a boulder for the daily, a flag for the FA, a loop for the Circuit, a pack and a book for setup. **And a title card in front of it all:** the hand-lettered wordmark, the pitch, a seeded ink ridge with the route dashed up it to a red ×, and TAP TO BEGIN. Not only decoration — a browser will not start an `AudioContext` until the player has touched the page, so this tap is the honest place to open one instead of having the first climb's sound silently blocked; it gates the render **in front of** the phase graph rather than being a phase, so the save format and the harness's phase walk are untouched. **Pure presentation — `engine.ts` and the whole sim harness are byte-unchanged, so the ~52 band cannot move** (band-neutral by construction, as `UI-1` was). 104/104 + 132/132, font-scale guard included. Two new UI-2 guards: every menu mode is a tile that carries its own caption, the old full-width-bar pattern is gone from the screen, the hero follows a resumable run, the marks are all distinct; and the title card gates every screen, says how to get past itself, carries its drawing, and opens the audio inside the tap.

### v9.94 — FA-1: THE FA is a real first-ascent mode, and the claimed grade is an economy (2026-08-11)
Two gaps in the game's namesake loop. **The mode was mislabeled:** the menu button THE FA ran `startSkirmish` — a plain graded boulder that never reached the naming/claim branch (a skirmish sets no `fa` and a standalone session is `inRun:false`, which returned to `sessionEnd` before the FA check), so the thing literally named THE FA never put up a first ascent. It now builds a real unclimbed line (`faRoute`, scaled to your level), and the FA-claim routing in `endSession` moved above the `!inRun` return so a standalone FA sends into the claim screen exactly like the in-campaign fa node. **The grade bought nothing:** undergrading paid the same as honest and overgrading only risked the Ego curse, so the deliberate sandbag — the game's identity — had zero positive incentive. The claim RULE is now an engine step (`claimStep`, extracted out of the App's `done()` per SIM-5): a **sandbag** pays quiet, lasting **XP** (a line stiffer than its grade earns a reputation and teaches you the most), a **spray** pays loud immediate **cash** but still risks Ego on your running average (`claimCurse`), **honest** pays a little XP; and the in-run claim now leaves a trail mark like every other stage advance. Off the campaign band by construction: the sim's claim policy (`run.mjs`) is an honest-claim stub that never calls `claimStep`, the in-campaign `endSession` FA payment is byte-identical (only relocated), and XP feeds the collection the drift guard measures at a full loadout. 104/104 + 128/128, 113/113 slow guardrails; band unmoved at ~53. New FA-1 guard: a standalone FA send reaches the claim; the record is written at the claimed grade; spray pays cash and sandbag pays more XP than honest than spray; standalone returns to menu, in-run to the map.

### v9.93 — CARD-16: no move-curse is inert — the last three get teeth (2026-08-11)
The fourth audit found 7 of 10 curses did nothing beyond being a weak card. `CARD-13` gave three of them a **hex** (the hold you dump the curse on gets +1 Grip); the other three MOVE curses — **Tweaked Pulley**, **Tweaky Finger**, **Cold Shut** — had slipped through. They now carry the same hex, so every curse you can place on a lane costs a position, not just a draw (an injured or numb hand cranks harder and the hold plays meaner). **Carve-out, honest and deferred:** the four BONUS curses (Bad Landing, Ego, Doubt, Sandbagged Beta) stay hand-clogging dead cards — an active effect there needs either a per-turn-in-hand hook (which compounds pump, the exact trap `ENG-23` measured and cut) or a new "cleanse at a cost" mechanic that makes curses removable (a real design shift), both bigger than this ticket. Band holds at the ~52 pin (n=600 53.7; curses reach the campaign via events but the drafter never picks them and the sim rarely plays them). 113/113 slow guardrails. New CARD-16 guard: every move-type curse carries `hex > 0`, so an inert move-curse can never creep back.

### v9.92 — RUN-13: a Circuit walk-off is recorded, the good outcome (2026-08-11)
A deliberate Circuit walk-away (`walkAwayStep`) banked your best score but wrote no history record, so "Every Trip" only ever showed the circuits you *lost* (`endSession` records those on a failure). The clean stop — the whole point of the endless mode — left no trace but `bestCircuit`. It now records as the win it is, with a circuit-appropriate cause (`walked off · N lines`). **Scope, deliberately narrow:** career "expeditions started" / "success rate" stay Lost-Line metrics — `wins` counts *finale sends* and the mastery deeds (`finisher`/`veteran`) read it, so folding the circuit into `runs`/`wins` would corrupt the success rate. The walk-off lives in history, not the campaign counters. (The surveyor's suggestion to bump `runs` for circuits was checked and declined for exactly that reason.) Off-band — history/display only, and the campaign band sim runs the Lost Line, not the circuit. 104/104 + 127/127; the walkAwayStep guard now asserts the walk-off writes a won-circuit record with the right cause.

### v9.91 — ENG-27: a roped fall pays the same fall-skin modifiers a boulder fall does (2026-08-11)
The caught-fall skin cost in resolve's roped branch ignored both fall-skin modifiers the boulder fall handler (App.tsx) honours, so on exactly the routes you fall most: **Long Game** and **Static** (`dFallSkin:1`) — their stated drawback, "a fall costs one more skin" — vanished, making them strictly upside on rope; and **Crash Pads** (`skinSave:1`), which eats the first fall of a burn, stopped working. The roped caught-fall now mirrors the boulder semantics: base loss still scales with the runout (`ceil(lost / RUNOUT_SKIN)`), `dFallSkin` adds to it, and `skinSave` (on burn 1, in a run — or a project) eats the fall entirely. Band holds at the ~52 pin (n=600 53.7; the guide line carries no kit, roped routes are act-3, boon-runs there are rare). 113/113 slow guardrails, climber-spread and drift included. New ENG-27 guard: a plain roped fall costs skin, Long Game costs one more, Crash Pads saves the first entirely.

### v9.90 — ENG-26: the preview reads the same board resolve does (2026-08-11)
The exact-preview pillar (UX-4/ENG-19) had two leaks, both preview-only — the resolution is untouched, so this is **band-neutral by construction**: the sim's policy (`autoPlay`/`cardValue`/the harness) never calls `previewLane`/`previewPump`, which are UI. **(1) The hang tax.** Resolve taxes holds whose *ability* is Committing (`abilityOf`), over the post-resolution board; the preview counted the `h.crux` *flag* instead — and the finale's **allCrux** phase promotes ordinary holds to `crux:true` while leaving their base ability (a jug stays a Rest), so the flag was set where the ability was not, and the preview over-reported the tax on exactly that phase. `previewPump` now keys on `abilityOf === 'Committing'` (which also subsumes the old `!clean` case). **(2) The grip.** Resolve chips a lane's hold (the Kneebar boon's `restChips`, a CARD-11 `restChip` card) or sharpens it (a CARD-13 `hex` curse) *before* that lane resolves, so `gripFor` sees the adjusted grip; `previewLane` read the un-mutated hold, so it disagreed on the target — and on whether that lane cleared — the turn a curse or a chipping rest landed there. `previewLane` now applies the same same-turn delta in resolve's order and with its clamps (rest chips floor at 0, hex does not). 104/104 + 126/126. New ENG-26 guard, deterministic and discriminating: a crux-flagged jug is not taxed (preview == resolve pump); Split Tip (Power 2 / hex 1) on a Grip-2 hold does not clear (it sharpens to 3 first); and Chalk the Hold works its own Grip-2 hold down to a clear — each matched between preview and resolve.

### v9.89 — CARD-15: the reward pool carries modern texture — a deliberate re-pin to ~52 (2026-08-11)
The reward pool (`REWARDS`) had been a frozen 22-card subset — the vanilla moves the game shipped with — so every mechanic added since was **undraftable as a climb reward** and a campaign deck could never express it. Added across common and uncommon: **tough** (Hand Jam), **friction** (Palm Press), an extra **static** (Static Reach), and the **synergy specialists** (Crimp Specialist, Pocket Poacher). The rare tier is left untouched on purpose — it is the dominant band lever (always-taken, high-impact; even peel/cycle rares the drafter gives no keyword bonus read 58% because they draw cards), and the strong rare-only keywords (echo/settle2/weight/momentum/guard) plus snap stay carved out for the same reason. **This is the audit's most important finding, and a deliberate, dated re-pin agreed with Evan.** The old ~46 band was *structurally held by the pool being thin* — a genuine refresh cannot hold it (even a same-size two-card swap read 53.7%), and `BAL-13` proved the early bosses cannot open offsetting headroom (they are not the completion bottleneck — act 3 is, and it is climber-spread-protected). So the pin moves to **~52** (n=300 48.7, n=600 53.0). The pool is a global lever, so enriching it rippled — and both ripples are bought back in the same pass so the whole suite stays green: the **ROUTE-8 direct** line became a +7.8 free win (a short line skips the endurance the richer pool bought), retuned `dClear −1→0` — same height, still +4 cruxes — to land ~2 under the guide; and the **Onsighter** sank to 4.8% under her 5% floor (the synergy specialists dilute a deck she cannot build around — no beta, fixed hand), bought back with `firstTurnPower 2→3` to 8.8%, back in the pack at a 1.45× spread (`dContact:2` was tried first and was the same cliff `dHand:2` is, 14.7%). The drift ledger is re-pinned to ~52 with the floor raised 40→44 to defend it. 113/113, slow guardrails included (drift, ROUTE-8, climber-spread, journal, acts-deadlier all re-verified). New CARD-15 guard: every reward name resolves to a real card of its tier, `rollOffers` never yields a blank, and tough/friction/static + a synergy specialist are draftable. This closes the fourth audit's last on-band item; the remaining six are off-band or preview-only.

### v9.88 — BAL-13: the early bosses are fights now (2026-08-11)
The fourth audit found The Priest (act 1) and The Hourglass (act 2) were single-phase — a slightly harder route — against the two- and three-phase act-3 bosses. They now kick into a real closing phase near the top: The Priest's **Guillotine** at 0.85 (the crack narrows to one hand while the clock keeps running — `dTax` + `lockLane`), The Hourglass's **Upper Tower** at 0.8 (past the waist it kicks back, nothing out right, clock running — `dTax` + `lockLane`). Even-handed clock/lane effects only, never `dBite`: the Summit Block ledger proved added Bite on a crit path charges the Alpinist (−2 Contact) and the Comp Kid (a burn) twice. **This is a feel/arc change, and it is measured band-neutral — which is itself the finding.** The early bosses are not the completion bottleneck (act 3 is), so giving them a real second phase, and in testing even +1 crux or earlier/stronger phases, leaves the band flat at ~46 (n=600 46.8, n=1000 48.6, against a 46.5/48.8 baseline) with act deaths ordered and unchanged (4/15/34). So the bosses now play as fights with a closing crux, but the *other* half of BAL-13 — act 1 being a formality — stays open, and is now known to be unreachable through the bosses: it needs a climber-neutral lever that touches neither route length nor the act-3 crit path. This also settled the plan for `CARD-15`: BAL-13 was meant to open headroom for a richer reward pool and cannot, so CARD-15's pin decision is unavoidable and it is parked on it. 113/113, slow guardrails (climber-spread and drift both hold). Guarded: the two early bosses carry ≥2 phases with a top-half closing phase and no `dBite` on the crit path.

### v9.87 — ENG-25: the balance sim can see opposition (2026-08-11)
The fourth audit's first on-band item, and the highest-value code fix in it. Opposition has been a real mechanic since `ENG-12` — a sideways pull (gaston, undercling, layback, the whole compression family) is **−2 alone and +2 with the other hand pulling back against it** — but both halves of the tuning sim were blind to it, so the pinned ~46% band had been measured on decks that *structurally excluded* the entire 14-card identity (common through rare). The drafter (`cardValue`) had no `opposes` term at all, so it scored those cards on raw stats and never built toward the archetype; and the play policy (`autoPlay`) filled one hand lane at a time against an empty partner, so `powerAgainst` returned the −2 *alone* value for every opposition card and a pair never formed. Both fixed: `cardValue` now values opposition like a light synergy (the first is a mild liability, each one after it turns a −2 into a seatable +2 pair), and `autoPlay`, when the other hand lane is an open hold and a second opposition card is in hand, scores this one as the pair it is about to become — the +2 counted once, since the second lane then reads the real partner off the board. **On-band by construction** (it moves both the deck the sim drafts and the line it plays), so it was measured, not assumed: the pin **held** at n=300 46.7% / n=600 46.5% — dead on ~46 and *tighter* than the 44.7/47.2 it replaced, which is the finding — opposition, made visible, is a balanced archetype and not a hidden lever. The drift ledger records the re-pin with its date; the `NARR-11` journal guard was raised n=250 → n=600 because opposition lifted the low-journal bands (it is journal-independent power) so the 7- and 14-page bands converged to within ~1 point and the n=250 per-band SE flicked them into a benign inversion — verified monotonic at n=600 (31.8/45.8/46.5) and n=1000 (34.1/47.6/48.8), the same fix `ROUTE-8` and `CARD-9` took. Guarded deterministically: given two opposition cards and an equal-power plain control across two open holds, `autoPlay` seats the **pair** rather than the locally-stronger single; the seated pair reads the full `OPPOSE` swing; and `cardValue` values a second opposition card above a lone one. 113/113, slow balance guardrails included.

### v9.86 — UI-1: the climb screen, reworked — the route is stone, your moves are paper (2026-08-11)
The headline of the fourth audit, and the one item asked for by name: the UI needed work. Rendered on a real phone viewport, the climb screen's fault was structural — its two board rows (the route's holds and your placed cards) were both a warm tan wash a shade apart (`#ded5c2` vs `#f0eade`), and the hand cards were that same `#f0eade`, so *the wall* and *my hand* read as one surface. The map screen — hand-drawn topo, real hierarchy — was the quality bar the rest of the game fell short of. This closes the gap on the screen you spend the most time on, and it is **pure presentation: no engine or content touched, so the pinned band cannot move** (measurement waived by construction; 104/104 core+sim pass, and the render was verified, not assumed). Four moves: **(1) two materials.** The route now takes a cool grey **stone** ground with a little carved depth (`--stone`), your placed moves keep warm **paper** and a tan margin stripe — the line you're drawing (`--card`); the menu tiles share `--card` so every card surface in the game is one material. **(2) a readable pump meter.** The preview segments — where COMMIT takes you — were a 45° hatch that muddied into the solid fill beside them; they now read as a soft solid tint capped by a **dashed top edge**, and the dash *is* the colour-blind-safe texture cue (solid = now, dashed = after this turn), so the projection survives without hue. Taller bar, faint ground. **(3) a type scale.** Bigger hold and card names, stronger ability lines, a prominent goal line — hierarchy where it was flat. The pip sizing is left exactly as the two-digit-span guard pins it (`.pip{min-width:20px`, `.pip.o{width:20px`), so that guard is untouched, not weakened. **(4) a sectioned page.** Carved headers — **THE ROUTE → YOUR MOVES → YOUR HAND** — so the screen reads top-to-bottom instead of as three unlabelled card grids. The `seg.will/willfall/shedding` selectors and the `.nomo .bar.tremble` rule the VIS-1 guard checks are kept verbatim; every new rule scales with `--fs`, so the text-size guard holds. `UI-1` opens a new ID family for full-screen overhauls, distinct from `ART` (assets) and `VIS` (single cues).

### v9.85 — CARD-14: three more one-shots, and the third audit closes (2026-08-10)
The tenth and final ship from the third audit. The consumable kit — the cash-sink one-shots you buy at a post, spend once, and never draft — was a four-item stub (shed / draw / +Power / an extra burn), so the trading-post's single kit line, indexed `(act·3 + tier) mod count`, cycled through the same four fast across a campaign. Three more, filling the gaps the audit named: **Tick Stick** (a grip-cut — −2 Grip to every hold on the wall, a mid-climb wire-brush pass), **Skin Salve** (a skin repair — +3, capped at the run maximum), and **Pep Talk** (a psyche restore — +1, capped at `PSYCHE_MAX`). All three resolve through the one shared `useKitStep` path (SIM-5), so nothing reimplements them. **Off the balance band** — consumables ride their own shop line and inventory, the drafting sim never buys or spends kit (the default campaign run leaves it untouched, which is what keeps the pinned band still), and the CARD-9 guard's buy-and-spend path hardcodes Second Wind, so the wider pool does not disturb it; slow guardrails run as insurance and held. Guarded: every consumable does something and explains itself (the check now knows the new fields), the kit is past four, and each new one-shot actually resolves — Tick Stick eases every lane's hold, Skin Salve patches skin, Pep Talk lifts psyche and never past its ceiling. **This closes the third audit, ten for ten — and with it all three audits (thirty items, thirty guards), the completion band defended throughout.**

### v9.84 — ENG-24: dialed-in flow reads the wall, never the clock (2026-08-10)
The ninth ship from the third audit, and the one where the audit's suggested fix turned out to be a trap the ledger had already sprung. The flow bar runs to 5, but its one clock payoff — the unanswered-lane tax relief — is spent by `FLOW_AT` (`HANG_TAX` is 1, so the tax is already 0 by flow 2, nothing left to graduate), so a deck with no momentum or dyno cards to spend the top of the bar on got nothing from flow 3-5. The obvious fix — ease the per-turn pump at `FLOW_HIGH` — is **the exact change `ENG-23` measured at +7 to +13 points of completion and deliberately cut** (per-turn pump relief compounds), and its guard still pins the clock identical at every flow level. So the top of the bar pays in **information**: dialed in, you read the coming holds (`FLOW_READ`), the same band-neutral lever `ROUTE-12` uses — it moves `readAhead` only, which resolution never consults and the greedy sim never reads, so it cannot touch the clock the flow-and-throw lesson protects. A steady deck finally gets something for sustaining high flow: a look up the wall. **Band-neutral by construction** (n=300 unchanged from the current baseline); the `ENG-23` clock guard and the exact-preview guard both still pass, and the slow guardrails held. Guarded: `FLOW_READ` is real; reaching `FLOW_HIGH` reads further up the wall than staying at `FLOW_AT`; and the per-turn pump is identical at both, so the cut clock relief has not crept back.

### v9.83 — UX-19: the scroll lists grow with the text-size setting (2026-08-10)
The eighth ship from the third audit, and the completion of the `A11Y-7` text-size work. Eleven scrolling lists — the Collection, the deck pool, the camp cut-list, the shop, the logbook, the glossary, history, deeds, stats, saves, the pack — hardcoded their scroll box at a fixed pixel height (`maxHeight: 470–660`). So when a low-vision player turned on **TEXT LARGER**, everything inside the box grew but the box did not: the one accessibility setting they reached for made these lists show the *fewest* rows. Each height now reads `min(<px> * var(--fs), 70dvh)` — it scales with the same `--fs` multiplier the text does, so the box grows in step and the row count holds, and it is capped to 70% of the dynamic viewport so a short phone never overflows into a double scroll. **UI-only, off the band**; slow guardrails run as insurance and held. Guarded: no scroll list uses a bare pixel `maxHeight` any more, and every one scales with `--fs` and caps in `dvh`.

### v9.82 — SKIRM-6: the Circuit offers a boon at its deep zones (2026-08-10)
The seventh ship from the third audit. The endless Circuit handed out one card every third line and nothing else — so the deck could only ever get *bigger*, never *different*, and boons (the things that "make a run worth telling somebody about") never appeared, leaving the deep grind a pure attrition race with a deck that never changed shape. Now, crossing into a deep zone offers a **boon**: entering **The Business** (line 10) and **Into the Dark** (line 14) drops you onto the gear-offer screen with a rule-breaker (guaranteed, plus gear to round it out), the next line already queued behind it so the pick hands straight back to the Circuit. It's deliberately gated to lines **past the enduro deed** (8 lines) so the mode's one pinned checkpoint is untouched, and the gear screen now reads for the Circuit ("Deeper In — this far in, the rock offers something back") rather than the campaign's van. This gives the endless run an *arc* — the same reason `SKIRM-4` gave it named zones and `SKIRM-5` gave it teeth. **Off the guarded campaign band** (the Circuit is its own mode; the drift guard runs the campaign); the boon reward reuses the campaign's `gearOffers`/`pickGearStep`, made Circuit-aware so a mid-run pick returns to `circuitNext` instead of the map. Slow guardrails hold. Guarded: a send crossing into line 10 or 14 offers a boon with the next line queued; no send below the deep zones ever does (the enduro deed is safe); and taking the boon adds it and returns to the next line.

### v9.81 — RUN-12: the weekly ladder gets a deed and a share (2026-08-10)
The sixth ship from the third audit, and the cleanest — a whole meta-loop that was built and then never hooked up to anything. `weekScore` and `weekBest` were accumulated every daily, saved, loaded, and printed as a line of text, and that was the entire feature: the daily got a streak deed and a shareable grid, the week got a number nobody could chase. Two additions close it. **In Season** — a new deed for banking a thousand points in a single week (a strong daily tops out around 600, so it takes coming back across the week, not one lucky morning) — gives the weekly ladder its first thing to *earn*. And **Share the Week** — a `weekShare` that pastes the running total, whether it is a personal best, the best week behind it, and the streak still going — gives it the same no-server social hook the daily has had since `SOCIAL-1`, sitting right beside the daily share on the session-end screen. **Off the band** — the daily and weekly run entirely apart from the campaign completion the drift guard pins, and the deed (like all deeds, INJ-1) pays and gates nothing; slow guardrails run as insurance and held. Guarded: the `bigweek` deed exists, is earned only at the all-time best week reaching the bar and never by a fresh save or a near miss, and `weekShare` carries the week's total and its best and calls out a personal best.

### v9.80 — CARD-12: the singleton snap/peel/cycle effects get siblings (2026-08-10)
The fifth ship from the third audit. Three of the game's effect keywords were carried by exactly one card each — `snap` (Deadpoint, clears a weak hold outright), `peel` (Ripcord, draw when it blows) and `cycle` (Read And React, draw each turn it holds) — so the crux-clear and card-advantage identities had no draftable breadth, while `greedy` sprawled across seven near-clones. Each identity now has a second card, deliberately at a **different stat shape** so it plays differently rather than reskinning: **Pounce** (uncommon snap, 4/5 — more staying power than Deadpoint's 4/3, so it snaps a weak hold and holds a stiff one), **Bail Out** (rare peel, 4/4 — more Power than Ripcord, so it blows and peels more often, a finisher with a net), and **Latch And Look** (rare cycle, 2/9 — low Power, high Contact, so it clings for turns and keeps feeding cards, the patient engine to Read And React's aggressive one). The sim's draft heuristic scores these by their stat line only (it gives snap/peel/cycle no special bonus), so it won't over-pick them; the band effect is whatever they do in play. **Measured band-neutral: 47.2% at n=600** against the ~46 pin (n=300 reads 44.7%, the offer-reshuffle noise any new draftable card causes, which converges out by n=600). **The three cards also tipped the `ROUTE-8` guard red — and that turned out to be worth catching.** That guard checks no map line beats the guide by more than a hair, and it ran at n=150 where the per-line standard error (~4 points) is bigger than its 3-point ceiling; it had been passing on luck while **the traverse was quietly a ~+3.5 free win** (it dropped two cruxes "for the same height" — pure difficulty, no trade). The extra draftable cards nudged it over and exposed it. Fixed at the root: the traverse now runs `dClear +1` as well, so it is genuinely the long way — two fewer hard moves, but more ground to cover before the top, an endurance-vs-power trade that measures within a point of the guide (46.5 vs 45.5 at n=400). The `ROUTE-8` guard was raised from n=150 to n=500 for a reliable read (the CARD-9 precedent) — at 500 every line settles: guide on its ~47 pin, direct +1, traverse −3. The pinned completion band runs the GUIDE line (`line:0`) and is untouched by the traverse change. Guarded: each of `snap`/`peel`/`cycle` now has at least two cards, no two of a kind share a stat line (breadth, not a reskin), every one is a costed non-curse move, and each sibling comes off `spawn` still carrying its effect; and no line beats the guide by more than 3 or sinks 8 below it.

### v9.79 — CARD-13: curses that bite — and the spawn bug that had CARD-11 dead (2026-08-10)
The fourth ship from the third audit, and it turned into two fixes for the price of one. **CARD-13** gives curses a mechanic of their own: every curse was inert filler (a weak move or a no-effect bonus, pure deck-dilution), so a new `hex` makes a curse *dumped onto a lane sharpen the hold there* — Split Tip, Wet Holds and Flapper now add +1 Grip to whatever hold you use them on, the mirror of `CARD-11`'s `restChip`. A curse costs you a position now, not just a draw, and you still choose the least-bad lane to be rid of it. **In building it, the guard caught something bigger.** The CARD-13 guard compares a hexed curse against a hex-neutered clone of itself — baseline-independent — and it came back showing *zero* effect. The cause: `spawn()` and `makeDeck()`, which build every card in a real deck, copy an **explicit field list**, and `restChip` (from `CARD-11`) and `hex` had both been left off it. So **`CARD-11` — Chalk the Hold — shipped completely dead**: its chip never fired in an actual game, and its own guard passed only by coincidence (it asserted an absolute grip that happened to equal the un-chipped baseline). Both fields are now carried through `spawn`/`makeDeck`, so CARD-11 works for the first time and CARD-13 works at all; the CARD-11 guard was rewritten to the baseline-independent form; and a new **tripwire test** asserts that a card off `spawn` equals its definition on every field, so a dropped effect can never silently ship again. **Band re-measured with both effects genuinely live for the first time: 47.0% at n=600, unmoved from the pin** (the greedy sim plays rests defensively and rarely dumps curse-moves, so both wash out at scale); n=300 reads 45.3%, within the band. Slow guardrails — drift, climber spread, acts-deadlier — all hold. Guarded: a spawned Chalk the Hold chips its lane by exactly its `restChip` (the test that would have caught the dead ship); a curse sharpens the lane it lands on by exactly its `hex` and no other; every hex card is a curse and only ever raises grip; and `spawn` carries every defined field.

### v9.78 — A11Y-8: headings, a polite log, and real modal sheets (2026-08-10)
The third ship from the third audit, and the broadest accessibility pass since the shape-not-colour work. Three gaps in the screen-reader/keyboard experience, all off the balance band. **The live regions shouted.** The running climb log and the tutorial step were `aria-live="assertive"`, so every log line and every teaching step *interrupted* a screen-reader user mid-sentence; both are `polite` now, and the turn result is still announced — just without cutting the user off (the guard that formerly required `assertive` was itself the thing keeping the log rude, so it was corrected to check the announcer exists rather than that it shouts). **There was no heading tree.** Every screen title was a styled `<span>`/`<div className="h1">` — zero headings in ~3000 lines — so a reader had nothing to jump between; all 30 titles now carry `role="heading" aria-level={1}`, giving each phase a navigable top. **The overlays weren't modal.** The two climb bottom-sheets (the "what is left" pile and the marks-key legend) were plain divs: focus never moved into them, nothing trapped it, and Escape did nothing, so a keyboard user opening the most information-dense overlays on the climb screen stayed stranded behind them. Both are `role="dialog" aria-modal="true"` with an accessible name now, focus moves in on open (a shared ref + effect), and Escape dismisses whichever is open. **UI-only, off the band**; slow guardrails run as insurance and held. Guarded: no `aria-live="assertive"` survives anywhere; every `h1` title is also a `role="heading"`; both sheets are named modal dialogs; and the Escape handler and focus-move are present.

### v9.77 — VIS-7: the forecast reads without colour, on every node (2026-08-10)
The second ship from the third audit, and the sibling of `VIS-6`. The map's forecast — worth a stated ~46 points of send rate — appends a non-colour cue on ordinary route nodes, `☁ weather · ⛰ rock · ▲ in nick / ▼ out of nick`, so a colour-blind player can read whether conditions help or hurt. But the three *bordered* node types dropped the arrow: **Project** and **Established** rendered the same conditions line styled green/red only, and the **First Ascent** node showed no forecast line at all. On exactly the lines a player weighs hardest — the projects you commit a burn to, the FA you stake a grade on, the line you put up and came back for — the read collapsed to hue, which `cbSafe` (a hue shift, not a text cue) does nothing for. All three now carry the same `▲/▼` text the ordinary nodes do, and the FA node gained the conditions line it was missing. **UI-only, off the band**; slow guardrails run as insurance and held. Guarded: every `☁/⛰` conditions line in the map — one per node type — must be immediately followed by the `▲ in nick / ▼ out of nick` cue, so no forecast can ride on colour alone again.

### v9.76 — META-10: the mastery deeds are honest (2026-08-10)
The first ship from the third audit, and it fixes a real defect in shipped work rather than adding anything. Two of the `META-9` mastery deeds were quietly broken. **Left No Trace** (`purist`) checked `s.ending === 'kept'` — but the ending is written as `` `${endingFor(s)}-${kind}` `` (e.g. `stranger-kept`, `known-told`) and read everywhere else via `.endsWith('kept')` / `.startsWith('known')`, so the bare literal `'kept'` **never occurred** and the deed was impossible to earn. **The Whole Quiver** (`quiver`) and **The Hard Way** (`hardway`) derived from `s.history`, which is capped at `HISTORY_MAX = 20` and evicts oldest-first — so a player who won on all five climbers across more than twenty trips could watch the deed tick and then *un-tick*, a checklist that lies. Fixes: the predicate now reads `.endsWith('kept')`, and the two history-based deeds read durable, monotonic records — `archWins` (the set of archetypes that have ever topped the finale) and `mutatorWin` (whether any finale win carried a mutator) — stamped in `endSession`'s finale branch beside `wins`, persisted through save/load and carried across expeditions. Both deeds union the durable record with whatever `history` still holds, so a deed earned before this shipped is never lost. **Off the band entirely** (deeds pay and gate nothing, per INJ-1; the new fields are inert to resolution); slow guardrails run as insurance and held. The third audit's other nine claims were each verified on disk before this was written, and one surveyor lead was thrown out on inspection — the exposure→psyche walk-away cost is implemented after all (`App.tsx:639`), not the dead constant it was reported as. Guarded: a fresh save earns neither mastery deed; putting the stone back on a `stranger-kept`/`known-kept` ending earns `purist` while a `-told` ending does not; and `quiver`/`hardway` stay earned from the durable record alone with an empty history, surviving a save round-trip.

### v9.75 — CARD-11: a rest that poses a decision (closes the second audit) (2026-08-10)
The tenth and final ship from the second audit. Every one of the game's rests was a flat "subtract pump" button — the seven of them differed only by a shed number, so half the pump economy never posed a choice beyond *how much do I shed*. **Chalk the Hold** adds one that does. It sheds **one less** than a plain full rest (2 instead of 3), and in exchange it brushes and chalks the hold in the lane you rest under, taking **−2 Grip** off it for next turn. That makes *where* you rest a real decision — shake out under the hold you most want to soften (offense), or under the bite you most need to block (defense) — a trade, not a strict upgrade. Implemented as a new per-card `restChip` applied only to its own lane's hold, only when a rest is actually possible (a roof or No-Shakes nullifies both the shed and the chip). **Rests are load-bearing on the band, so this was measured, not assumed:** the chip itself is provably band-neutral (completion identical at chip 0, 1 and 2, because the greedy sim only plays rests defensively when pumped), and the only movement is the offer reshuffle that adding *any* card to a rarity pool causes — the n=300 point estimate slid to 45.3%, n=600 read 47.0%, so the **~46 pin holds** and the drift ledger is dated to say so. Guarded: the card exists, rests, chips, and sheds less than a plain rest (a trade); and — proven through `resolve` — the hold in the lane you rest under loses exactly its grip while the other lane is untouched, and moving the rest moves the chip. **Both audits (v9.56 and v9.66) are now fully shipped — twenty items, twenty guards, the band defended throughout.**

### v9.74 — VIS-6: the shape language reaches every screen where you draft (2026-08-10)
The ninth ship from the second audit, and the natural companion to `UX-18`. The `Fam` ink mark — hand, feet, rest, technique, protection, beta, curse — is drawn on the board and hand so a climb is scannable by *shape*, not colour (`VIS-3`, an accessibility win for colour-blind players). But every card-*list* screen dropped it and fell back to bare names, and those are exactly the screens where "is this a feet card, a rest, a curse?" decides a draft: the reward offers, the trading-post cards, the level-up pack. `UX-18` already brought the inline `FamMark` to the Collection; this drops it into the other three, in a small `famname` row that keeps the mark beside the card's name. Reused, not reinvented — the same component, the same shape vocabulary. **UI-only, off the guarded band** (no engine or content touched); the slow guardrails were run as insurance and held. Guarded: the reward, shop and pack screens each render a `FamMark`, and both the inline mark component and its row style still exist — so a draft screen can't quietly fall back to names-only again.

### v9.73 — ROUTE-12: a signature does something, on the lines you replay (2026-08-10)
The eighth ship from the second audit. A `signature` was only a base hold with its numbers nudged and a name on it (`ROUTE-5`), set on just 11 of ~38 routes — and the modes you actually grind (circuit, daily, skirmish, first ascents) had none, the exact sameness `ROUTE-5` set out to kill. Two changes. **First, signatures now pay a one-time hook:** working one for the first time **reads the next holds off the wall** — the sequence you suss from that one good stance. Per `ROUTE-10`'s hard-won lesson, it is deliberately **information, not power**: it moves `readAhead` only, which the resolution never consults and the greedy sim never reads, so it cannot touch the band. **Second, the grind lines get a named feature too** — tagged band-neutrally: an ability-less signature matched to a hold of its own base type, setting only the name, note and read hook with **no stat, type or ability change**, so the hold resolves exactly as the plain one it already was. The choice is drawn from an **isolated per-line RNG** (hashed from the line's identity), so it consumes nothing from the run stream — the daily and every seed replay identically. **Band-neutral by construction and confirmed empirically:** the read hook rides all 11 campaign signatures and first ascents sit on the guarded band, yet completion measured **46.7% → 46.7%** at n=300, unmoved. Guarded: every signature carries a read hook; the tag pool is real and ability-less; a worked signature reads off the wall while producing the *identical* clears, pump and board as the untagged hold (band-neutrality proved directly); a circuit line gets exactly one tag, from the safe pool, adding no ability and showing a feature name; and the same line names the same feature on replay.

### v9.72 — NARR-13: your sandbagged lines come back with the consensus (2026-08-10)
The seventh ship from the second audit, and it closes the loop on the game's namesake act. You name and grade a first ascent, the cast reacts once the evening you come down (`faTalk`), and then the line went silent forever — `fa:<name>` sat in `seen` and never recurred. But a grade is a *claim others test over time*, and nobody ever repeated or re-graded yours. Now, a trip **later**, Marge brings it back with the community consensus, read straight off the drift you already stored: a **sandbag** (you undergraded) is confirmed stiffer and ages well; a **soft** call gets flashed and downgraded, exactly as she warned; a **square** grade stands, the rarest thing in the book. It's gated to a genuinely later expedition — a new `run` field is stamped on the line at the claim and the beat only fires once `s.runs` has advanced past it — so it reads as time passing, not an echo. Deterministic, no new node, no RNG, no touch to the campaign path: pure flavour, off the band. **It also repairs a latent bug it was built on top of:** the talk screen resolved `st.talkId` with `TALKS.find` alone, but first-ascent talks mint dynamic `fa:<name>` ids that were never in `TALKS`, so *every* line-of-yours conversation dead-ended at a blank BACK button in the UI (the sim exercises talks at engine level, so it slipped). A new `talkById` resolver handles the static cast plus the dynamic `fa:`/`con:` ids, and the screen routes through it. Guarded: the consensus fires only in a later run, only after the first reaction, only once, and never for an unstamped pre-existing line; the three drifts read differently and state the real grade; a fresh line's first word still precedes an old line's consensus; and `talkById` resolves static, `fa:` and `con:` ids while a phantom line resolves to nothing.

### v9.71 — SKIRM-5: Into the Dark keeps biting past the grade cap (2026-08-10)
The sixth ship from the second audit. The Circuit's grade is `min(10, floor(n · 0.7))`, so it pins at 10 by line 15 and everything derived from it — `clear`, `crux` — freezes with it. That made **Into the Dark** (line 14+), the zone players brag about reaching, the *shallowest* mechanically: identical forever, pure skin attrition with no new teeth. `SKIRM-4` had already given it a name and a sense of place; this gives it a reason to fear it. A new route-level `dBite` grows past the cap — **+1 the moment the grade pins, then +1 every three lines deeper** (`ceil(max(0, n−15) / 3)`) — applied to every hold through `biteAgainst`. It's a pure function of the line count that is **zero at and below line 15**, so the whole lower curve and the `enduro` deed (line 8) are byte-identical, and it's wired so the RNG draw order in `circuitRoute` is unchanged (lower lines generate exactly as before). **Off the guarded campaign band by construction**: only the Circuit sets `dBite`; every campaign route leaves it undefined, so `biteAgainst` adds 0 there — the completion band can't move. Slow guardrails run as insurance and held. Guarded: the grade really does cap at 10 and stay there; `dBite` is zero through line 15, grows monotonically past it, and is ≥1 the instant the grade caps; and — verified through `biteAgainst` — the same hold bites harder deep in the dark than in the shallows, by exactly the route's `dBite`.

### v9.70 — RUN-11: Sustained, a mutator that changes the game not the grade (2026-08-10)
The fifth ship from the second audit. All eight existing mutators are one-directional harder sliders — worse conditions, less Contact, fewer burns, no gear, stiffer grades, more curses. Not one changes how a climb *plays*; they only move the difficulty dial. **Sustained** is the first that alters texture. Normally the hand dumps at the end of every move and a fresh five is dealt — high churn, a new draw to bail you out of a bad one. With Sustained on, **you never re-rack**: your unplayed cards carry between moves, and the working hand is smaller (`HAND_SIZE − SUSTAINED_CUT`, 5→3), so you climb the whole boulder on what you're holding and husband a good card for when the crux comes, instead of churning to it. It's the exact mirror of the **All In** boon (draw deep, keep nothing), and it pays 40 XP like every other opt-in hardship. Implementation is two gates in the turn loop keyed on a new `retain` flag off `mutMods` — the end-of-turn discard is skipped and the draw target is cut. **Off the guarded band by construction**: the campaign sim runs with no mutators, so `mutMods([]).retain` is false and every non-mutator run is byte-identical; the slow guardrails were run as insurance and the band held. Guarded: Sustained is a real reachable mutator that flips `retain`, pays XP, shrinks the opening hand by exactly the cut, and — verified through an actual burn — carries an unplayed card across a turn where the default hand dumps to zero.

### v9.69 — UX-18: the Collection is a codex, not a checklist (2026-08-10)
The fourth ship from the second audit, and the smallest-risk of them — pure display. The Collection is the one screen whose whole job is *"here is everything you have,"* and it showed the least of any card list in the game: a bare name and a `power/contact` fraction, no rules text, no family, nowhere to look up what a card actually does. Every *other* card surface — the hand, the board, reward and shop — carries the `Fam` shape mark (`VIS-3`), and the deck and logbook screens use a two-line `deckrow` with the rules text; only the reference screen was left behind. It uses that same layout now: each owned card shows its **scannable family mark** (an inline version of the card mark, `FamMark`), its **numbers**, its **family in words**, and its **rules line**. Unowned cards stay a mystery — the mark and the text are withheld behind `have`, so the codex still rewards finding a card rather than spoiling it. **UI-only, off the guarded band** (no engine or content touched); the slow guardrails were run as insurance and held. Guarded: the collection screen uses `deckrow`, renders `FamMark` and `c.text` and the family label, and gates the reveal on ownership — so it can't quietly slide back to a bare checklist.

### v9.68 — ROUTE-11: the route move reads the weather and rock you're in (2026-08-10)
The third ship from the second audit, and the one that finally makes **rock live**. "The route is the opponent" has been the thesis since v0, and `ENG-11` gave the route a telegraphed move — but it picked from flat, condition-blind thresholds, so a drizzle-on-sandstone climb and a crisp-on-granite climb threw the *identical* stream of grease/dry/gust/crumble. Rock, in particular, did nothing at all once the holds were dealt. Now the live condition weights which move comes: **seeping air greases** (humid, drizzle push the roll toward the hold getting harder), **crisp air dries** (crisp, freezing push it toward coming into nick), and **friable sandstone flakes** (it crumbles where the hard rock gusts) — the two things the player already reads off the paper now drive what the wall does to them.
