A full campaign runs **~27 minutes** and the grade label reads `?` on the finale everywhere it appears.

### v1.3 — META-3: the ascent-style ladder (2026-07-27)
Difficulty now scales the way climbing actually scales — not by a bigger number but by **better style**. Seven cumulative rungs: **Working It · Second Go · Redpoint · Headpoint · Flash · Onsight · Ground Up**. Each tightens a different dial rather than stacking the same one — +1 Bite, then a skin off the run, then two burns a boulder instead of three, then weaker camps, then **no beta banks between burns** (every burn is an onsight), then +1 Grip on the rock itself. Sending the act unlocks the next rung; the picker sits on the menu above the run button.

Measured across the whole ladder, and it does what the item was opened for. With the **starter loadout**, completion runs **22.3% → 4.3%**. With a **fully-built loadout**, **58.3% → 17.3%**. So a maxed collection is comfortable at the bottom and back to a genuine fight at the top — roughly a 3.4× difficulty range set against the collection's ~2.6× power gain. The answer to a strong collection turned out to be a harder act, exactly as `META-3` predicted, and no reward needed clawing back. One structural detail worth remembering: *Headpoint* raises **reach-boss to 100%** while lowering completion, because two burns a boulder means you fail climbs faster and arrive with more skin and a worse deck.

Also fixed a harness break introduced while hoisting `runOnce` out of the run mode so the ladder sweep could share it — the reopening `if (mode === 'run') {` never landed because the return statement had gained a field since the pattern was written, leaving an orphan brace. `node --check` in the sim caught it before it could quietly report nonsense.

### v1.2 — META-2: the loadout (2026-07-27)
A **fixed 15-card loadout**, built from the starter kit plus everything you have unlocked, now serves as the starting deck for **both** The Lost Line and The FA. Fixed size is the point: the choice is composition, not bulk, so a growing collection cannot bloat the deck or dilute its consistency. Runs still grow from there via rewards. The editor lists every buildable card with a stepper, shows rare and uncommon slot use, and warns when the deck is short on feet — the lever the RUN-1 sim showed players will otherwise miss. Defaults to the old starter 15, so nothing changes until you touch it.

Two constraints came out of simming the ceiling rather than guessing it. A greedy full-collection build first hit **100% on every route in the act, boss included** — the feature was breaking the game. Per-card copy limits scaled by rarity (3 common / 2 uncommon / 1 rare) barely helped: the build simply fielded **eight distinct rares**, and rares probe at +18.9pt against common's +5.5. The fix that worked is a cap on how many you may *carry* — `RARE_SLOTS` 1 and `UNCOMMON_SLOTS` 3 — which pushed the optimal build back onto good commons in multiples (Lock Off ×3, Pinch Grip ×3, Mantle ×3) and the boss down to 80%. Against the starter's 17% that is a real deckbuilding payoff rather than a bypass, and the residual is logged as `META-3` rather than nerfed: the answer to a strong collection is a harder act, not a weaker reward.

### v1.1 — EVT-1: events, choices, and the journal (2026-07-27)
**Seventeen events** now sit on the Act 1 map as a third node type alongside climbs and camps — storms, a breaking flake, an access notice, a bolt war, an injured climber, the van, a bear-shredded food bag, a sandbagging stranger. Each is a two-way choice with a declarative outcome (skin, a specific card, a random card of a rarity, a curse, XP, a forced cut, a journal page), so events are pure content in the `EVENTS` table. This is also the delivery mechanism the curses never had.

**`NARR-1` seeded.** Six **journal pages** in the first ascensionist's voice, found through events and readable from the menu. They persist across runs — the DES-1 decision that meta-progression stays narrative rather than a stat drip, finally implemented.

Balance work was the bulk of the turn and the finding was a design fault, not a number. Comparing a climb-greedy policy against an event-seeking one, **events strictly dominated: 29.7% completion versus 20.3%.** Capping event healing barely moved it (28.6 vs 20.0), which ruled out the obvious suspect. The real cause was that **a failed climb in a run gave literally nothing** — so climbing was a coin flip that could whiff entirely while an event was guaranteed, and skipping climbs was correct. That directly contradicts the *falling is learning* pillar. A lost session now banks a weaker two-card offer, which closed the gap to 5.4 points (22.3 vs 27.7) and made the cautious line cost deck quality (20.1 cards versus 21.6). The residual gap is logged as `EVT-2` rather than tuned blind, since it partly reflects a drafter that undervalues cards.

### v1.0 — menu, skirmish, levels, packs, saving, and 94 cards (2026-07-27)
A proper front end. The **main menu** carries level, an XP bar and collection count, and opens two modes: **The Lost Line** (the Act 1 run, resumable) and **The FA** — skirmish, a procedurally named and generated boulder scaled to your level, played with a deck built from everything you own. Practice mode folded away behind a toggle.

**Progression.** Every send grants XP (5 + 3×grade, 50 for an act win); every level grants a **pack of 3 cards** rolled 60/30/10 common/uncommon/rare, biased toward cards you do not own. The pack screen sits between the fight and wherever you were headed. A **collection** screen lists all 94 by rarity with unowned entries masked.

**`ENG-5` closed.** `localStorage` behind try/catch so a blocked storage context still plays, just forgetfully. `SAVE_FILE_VERSION` 1, loader is `{...freshRun, ...loadGame()}` so new fields need no migration. Saves land at every safe point — and **starting a climb immediately writes the "you abandoned it" state**, so quitting mid-battle costs you that node instead of letting you reroll a bad opening hand.

**The pool went 30 → 94 cards**, costed to the measured law (budget ≈ 2·Power + Contact; common 10–13, uncommon 13–16, rare 15–19) with three new engine capabilities behind them: **Tough** (immune to Sharp and Razor), **Settle2** (settles twice as fast), **Greedy** (+2 Power when pumped), plus `restore` finally implementing card recovery from the burnt pile. Verified with a new per-card probe that swaps three copies into a fixed shell: mean delta by rarity comes out **rare +18.9 > uncommon +8.2 > common +5.5 > curse −14.2**, so the rarity ladder is real rather than asserted. Absolute deltas are dominated by shell weakness, so outliers are judged within rarity — only three showed, all utility bonuses the probe over-penalises by stacking. One was a genuine fault and got fixed: **Beta Spray charged pump for draw, and pump is your health** (−23pt); it now sheds instead. Two harness bugs found on the way — the sim fell out of its loop on the new `pack` phase and miscounted the last result as a win (96.8% completion, obvious nonsense), and the bonus policy played draw cards whenever it could afford them rather than when the hand was thin.

### v0.9 — RUN-1: the Act 1 run loop (2026-07-27)
A run, at last. Nine stages, one choice per tier — which boulder, or spend a camp — ending at **The Priest**. The deck persists and grows: send a climb and pick 1 of 3 cards (70/25/5 common/uncommon/rare), fail and you get nothing, so weak climbs compound into a weak deck at the boss. Camps trade **+2 skin** against cutting a card, which is the first place deck-thinning becomes a real decision. Skin is now run-scoped rather than per-boulder. Practice mode kept alongside for balance testing. Two out-of-range bugs caught before simming: failing *or* bailing on the boss tier advanced past the end of the map and would have crashed the map screen.

The run economy was simmed, not eyeballed, and the first result was a structural failure rather than a tuning one: **100% of runs reached the boss** with 5.9/10 skin to spare, so six climbs of play threatened nothing and the run was a walk plus one coin flip. Two findings fixed it. First, the sim's reward policy ignored lane and starved the feet lane — an empty feet lane is +1 Bite on both hands, and making the drafter feet-aware moved completion 11% → 22% on its own. That is a genuine strategic lever and the player will need to learn it. Second, `RUN_SKIN` at 10 was inert; at **6** with `CAMP_SKIN` **2** the deaths spread across stages 4, 6, 8 and 9 — an attrition curve instead of a single wall. The Priest eased to clear 10 / crux 3 to pay for it. Final: **77% reach the boss, 22% completion, ~70 turns (~14 min) per run.**

### v0.8 — ENG-4: Settle, and a hand that scrolls itself (2026-07-27)
**The hand is now its own horizontal scroller.** With a full hand the cards overflowed the viewport and the whole *page* scrolled sideways, dragging the board off screen. Fixed with a full-bleed `overflow-x:auto` scroller (snap points, hidden scrollbar, contained overscroll) plus `overflow-x:hidden` on html/body and the wrap; top padding leaves room for the selected card's lift so `overflow-y:hidden` cannot clip it.

**`ENG-4` closed.** Ported the costing sweep into the TS harness — which needed a `customDeck` hook on `GameState` so the sim can inject synthetic Power/Contact probes — and the baseline was worse than DES-2 recorded: at P1, send% *fell* from 21% to 10% as Contact rose from 3 to 10. Contact was not merely weak, it was **negative** at low Power, because surviving without clearing just buys more clock ticks. Fix is **Settle**: a move that survives resolution gains +1 Power, capped at +2. Durability compounds into offense, and it reads true — you shift your weight and the hold gets better. Result: **1 Power ≈ 2 Contact** (from 4:1 and unreachable at C10), and the P1 row now *rises* with Contact instead of falling. Two compensations were tried and rejected on evidence: restricting Settle to non-clearing turns pushed the ratio straight back to 4:1, and `BITE_BUMP 2` *degraded* it to 3:1 — past a point nothing survives regardless of Contact, so Bite stops being the lever. Settled on `BITE_BUMP 1` (the ratio sweet spot) with clear targets restored on the top five routes, which buys difficulty from length rather than from lethality. Ladder monotonic across all three deck tiers, sessions **0.6–3.5 min**, The Priest at 13% / 45% / 51%.

### v0.7 — abilities, conditions, and a readable top-out (2026-07-27)
**Every hold type now does something.** Jugs *Rest* (answer one, shed 1 pump) · crimps are *Sharp* (blow a card, +1 pump) · sharp crimps *Razor* (blow a card, burn another out of hand) · slopers are *Greasy* (−1 Power unless your feet are on) · pinches *Squeeze* (+1 Bite while both hands are busy) · pockets are *Two-finger* (ignore Support entirely) · cruxes are *Committing* (need Power 2+ and add +1 hang tax). Feet features too: *Slick* and *Featureless*, the latter shutting off Support from that lane — which is what finally makes slab hurt for the right reason. **Cards answer them:** Precise (+2 vs crimps), Friction (ignores Greasy), Static (−1 Bite), Balance (blocks Squeeze), Hooked (cancels crux tax), Snap (outright clears Grip ≤3), and Dyno finally does what a dyno does — *Commit* clears any hold at all, then burns out. Brush and Perfect Beta now strip a hold's ability as well as its Grip. **Conditions:** six weathers and five rock types rolled per battle off the run seed, shown in the header. **Win clarity:** a tick-track across the top plus "N MORE HOLDS TO TOP OUT" — the goal is now stated, not inferred. **Length:** `ATTEMPTS` 4 → 3 and clear targets trimmed; sessions run **0.6–3.6 min** against a 5-minute target, measured at 12s/turn. `ENG-2` closed properly — `sim/` esbuild-bundles and imports `App.tsx`'s own `resolve` and `autoPlay`, so game and harness are one source of truth and the Python sims are retired. Three bugs caught in the process: the policy couldn't play targeted bonuses, which made the *late* deck lose to mid on hard routes (Fridge 77→76, Priest 35→33) — the DES-2 dead-card trap again; `dContact` was a declared, assigned, never-read field, so *freezing* had no downside; and weather's `dPower ±1` swung battles from 29% to 98%, which is the 4:1 Power dominance biting a third time. **Conditions may never touch Power globally** — weather now moves Bite and Contact only, and the spread tightened to 57–96%. Deck tiers are strictly monotonic on all ten routes; The Priest reads 3% / 27% / 43%.

### v0.6.1 — the build was shipping corrupted JS (2026-07-27)
v0.6 never ran: the page dumped raw minified JS as text under `SyntaxError: missing ) after argument list`. The game was fine; `scripts/build-html.mjs` was not. It inlined the bundle with `String.replace(re, bigJsString)`, and a replacement **string** interprets `$&`, `` $` ``, `$'` and `$$` — so React's `$$typeof` sentinel became `$typeof`, corrupting 117 characters across the bundle and making it unparseable. Fixed by passing a **function** to `.replace()`, which disables all `$` interpretation. Then hardened the pipeline so this class of failure cannot ship again: `build:html` now extracts the script back out of the *assembled* HTML, asserts it is byte-identical to what was inserted, runs `node --check` on it, and fails if any external `src`/stylesheet reference survived inlining. Any of those exits nonzero. The round-trip check found a second, subtler bug while being written — the unescape step was converting esbuild's own legitimate `<\/script>` sequences back to `</script`, eating a backslash; esbuild had already handled that correctly and the "fix" was undoing it. Gate negative-tested by reintroducing the original bug and confirming the build refuses. No game logic changed.

### v0.6 — ENG-1: first code, lane combat playable (2026-07-27)
Repo scaffolded (React 19 + TS + Vite 6, single-file `App.tsx`, one-file HTML build) and the combat prototype landed. All ten Act 1 boulders are playable end-to-end against three deck tiers (Starter 15 / Mid-act 21 / Late-act 30), with 30 cards defined in the `CARDS` table — the 8 not yet in any deck are the reward pool RUN-1 will hand out. Engine and content are separated as required: `HOLD_STATS`, `STYLES`, `ROUTES`, `CARDS`, `DECKS` are pure tables, and the whole DES-3 boulder set ported straight across. Seeded `RNG` class with the seed held in state and consumed fully in handler bodies before `setState`, so updaters stay pure under StrictMode; zero `Math.random()` outside a comment. Pile transitions go through `pileDraw` / `pileDiscard` / `pileExhaust` / `pileFromHand` — no ad-hoc splices. Implements the full v0.4 rule set: simultaneous lane resolution, Support/campusing, exhaust-on-blow with Anchor and Latch, the flat-plus-unanswered clock, flow cutting tax, and beta banking across burns. Two bugs caught before shipping: an orphaned `nextBurn`, and beta-banking that scraped the log for `works the …` instead of tracking state — which silently never banked crux holds. Also found and fixed a rest-card bug where a *persisting* Shake Out kept shedding pump without breaking flow, because `rested` was only set on placement; shed now lives in `resolve` as the single source of truth. Verified in the built HTML: all glyphs ship as real UTF-8 bytes (`·` ×12, `▸` ×3, `−` ×7, `—` ×4) with no `\uXXXX` leakage, and the hand fan measures 354px against 366px available. 212 KB, one file.

### v0.5 — NAME-1: the game is Sandbagged (2026-07-27)
Name locked. The game is **Sandbagged** — a route graded softer than it climbs, which is the premise of a finale graded "?" and of a first ascensionist who undersold what he'd found. Campaign mode is **The Lost Line**; the other mode stays **Skirmish**. Repo slug `sandbagged`, public URL `rcjlabs.github.io/sandbagged/?v=NNNN`, version marker `// SANDBAGGED vX` on line 5. Propagated through the project instructions and all three design docs. Collision-checked against Steam and itch: no game by that name, and the climbing-game shelf holds no card battler. Accepted cost: the gaming-culture sense of "sandbagging" (deliberate underperformance) is roughly the inverse of the climbing sense, so general search skews to fighting-game threads. Two consequences noted rather than changed: the curse card **Sandbagged Beta** now shares the title and stands as a deliberate title drop, and the campaign mode shares a name with the finale route — which the lore already answers, since the FA vanished before naming it and *the lost line* is only what everyone else calls it. **Phase 1 closes with this**; ART-1 is all that remains and it gates nothing.

### v0.4 — DES-2, the card pool and the costing law (2026-07-27)
48 cards across starter/common/uncommon/rare/curse, each with Power/Contact, a lane tag and keywords (**Support, Shed, Anchor, Latch**). Starter deck grew 12 → 15. Landed **ENG-3 (exhaust-on-blow)** — a blown move is gone for the burn, Anchor exempts it, which is what finally makes Bite matter through permanent attrition. Measured the **costing law** on a swept 4×8 Power/Contact grid: **1 Power ≈ 4 Contact**, and Contact is a *threshold* stat that jumps at C7 (survives two typical hits) then saturates — so cards get costed by hits-survived, not raw points. Building that grid exposed the clock as the reason defense was worthless: taxing *uncleared* holds made deleting doubly rewarded, measuring at 5–7:1 against Contact. **Clock reworked to +1 flat per turn plus +1 per unanswered hold**, which halved the ratio to 4:1 and gives holding a lane a purpose; `PUMP_MAX` 10 → 11 (swept 10–13) to pay for the flat tick, and Bite now bumps at half rate by grade. Ladder re-verified monotonic across three deck tiers with **zero deck-outs**, The Priest gating at 24% mid / 48% late. Style-responsive deckbuilding confirmed for the first time: a cost-matched TANK deck beats POWER by **+17** on compression while POWER edges the endurance route — race the sharp routes, grind the tanky ones. An earlier matchup showing POWER winning everything by 13 was invalid (decks weren't cost-matched), which is precisely why the costing law now exists. Logged `ENG-4`: Power still dominates 4:1 and Contact saturates, so defensive cards need a keyword payoff rather than more points.

### v0.3 — DES-3, the Act 1 boulder set (2026-07-27)
Ten authored forest boulders V0–V5 as **hold decks** — style, clear target, crux count, feet pool, and a journal line each, ending in **The Priest** (V5 mini-boss). Pure content: a future `ROUTES` table, no engine code touched. Harness refactored from procedural grade weights to boulder specs, with the v0.2 grade curve re-run as a regression. Ladder is **monotonic on both decks** (starter 100→0%, mid 100→20%); two routes were reordered rather than re-tuned to get there. The Priest sends at 20% on a mid-act deck, so the boss is deliberately not beatable on the deck that cleared the act. Styles verified to differ mechanically: **card attrition spreads 3.4×** (jug haul 4.0/burn → power 13.5), and compression is the clock-purest route at 87%. Two engine experiments built, swept and **reverted** rather than half-landed: overkill spill has no usable range (even capped at 1 pump it collapses every route above V1, since moves blow ~5× a burn), and exhaust-on-blow differentiates well but decks you out of a 12-card starter. Both deferred to `ENG-3`, to be decided alongside DES-2's card list. Also established: style Grip deltas cap at ±1, because Grip is superlinear against the clock.

### v0.2 — DES-1 rewritten: Hearthstone-style lane combat (2026-07-27)
Combat model pivoted from a pump-ledger to a **3-lane board**. Lanes are limbs (LH/RH/feet); the route plays holds with **Bite/Grip**, the player answers with moves carrying **Power/Contact**, and lanes resolve simultaneously with survivors persisting. **Pump became health *and* mana** — unblocked Bite fills it, bonus cards spend it, 10 = fall. Added the load-bearing clock rule: **+1 pump per uncleared hold at end of turn**, without which turtling all three lanes is free and the fight never ends; with it, clearing a lane slows the clock. Two traps caught in sim before they reached paper: additive Support+Flow bonuses on base-2/3 Power made moves one-shot every hold (**100% flash at every grade** — the same trap v0.1 hit, so flow was moved off Power entirely and now cuts hang tax), and the rest-card policy gate could never fire, leaving **2 of 12 starter cards dead weight** in every number produced before the fix. Honest re-run gives a clean curve: starter V2 80% / V3 53% / V4 2.6%, mid-act deck V4 50% / V5 37%, burns 4.9–5.6 turns, projecting 2.4% → 17.2% across four burns. Board PIL-mocked and proven at 390px — 3×112px lanes fit with 18px margin, but the 5-card hand **must** be an overlapping fan (unfanned overflows by 102px). `loopsim.py` retired in favour of `lanesim.py`.

### v0.1 — DES-1, the core loop (2026-07-27)
Loop spec written and validated on 3000-session Monte Carlo per grade. Turn anatomy (draw 4 · play free · hang tax on end turn · pump 10 = fall), the three dials (pump/flow/beta), and the constants table. Two structural bugs caught in sim before they reached paper: an uncapped flow discount that made moves free (100% send at every grade under V4), and a flat shake-out value that reduced difficulty to route length and walled every grade at exactly 9 holds. Fixes: cap the flow discount at −1, and shed pump **by hold type** — jugs 5, other holds 2, cruxes 0 — which makes *where* you rest the core decision and route shape a real difficulty axis. Confirmed deckbuilding is the dial (+4 cards moves V4 from 0% → 74%) and that projecting pays (V3: 2.3% → 29.9% over three burns). Combat screen PIL-mocked at true 390px; hand size 4 independently validated by layout — 5 cards overflow. Beta-persistence decision resolved. Dyno commitment check flagged untested for feel.

### v0 — inception (2026-07-27)
Project instructions and this roadmap. Design baseline locked from the founding brainstorm: route-as-opponent thesis, pump/flow/projecting core, journal art direction, three-act campaign to The Lost Line, bouldering-only MVP. Name undecided — "The FA" placeholder in force.
